- [ ] **Inline `HashMap<String, String>`-as-`Extractor` idiom for envelope trace fields — MH lands first, MC's R-57 (task #6) will need the sibling (2026-07-03, task #27; corrected 2026-07-04 at Gate 2)**: Task #27 (MH) wires `webtransport/connection.rs::handle_connection()` to extract inbound W3C trace context from `MhClientMessage::{trace_parent, trace_state}` (proto tags 20/21) and attach it as the span parent. **Correction from the original Gate-1 note**: the landed code does NOT add a bespoke `Extractor` impl — it builds a `HashMap<String, String>` carrier (skipping proto3-default-empty fields) and calls the global propagator's `.extract(&trace_carrier)`, reusing `opentelemetry`'s existing blanket `Extractor` impl for `HashMap<String, String>` (the same technique `otel_grpc.rs`'s own unit tests already use). So there is no new `Extractor` TYPE to hoist — the residual duplication is just the ~9-line "build a `HashMap` from two proto string fields, call the global propagator, `set_parent`" idiom itself. `ClientMessage` (MC-bound) carries the identical `trace_parent`/`trace_state` field pair (`proto/dark_tower/signaling/v1/signaling.proto:354-364`) and MC's R-57 task (#6, not yet landed — `crates/mc-service/src/webtransport/connection.rs` only zero-inits these fields in test fixtures today) will need the byte-identical idiom. Extraction opportunity, NOT true duplication — MH #27 consumes `common::observability::otel_grpc`'s propagator/bounds machinery as-is and does not reimplement it; the idiom has exactly ONE consumer today and is arguably too small (9 lines, no branching beyond two `is_empty()` guards) to need its own named helper vs. just copy-pasting the idiom a second time. Per the ADR-0019 DRY Reviewer Exception this is TODO-tracked, not fix-or-defer. Likely target, if it proves worth hoisting once MC's copy exists: a tiny `common::observability::otel_grpc::extract_context_from_trace_fields(trace_parent: &str, trace_state: &str) -> opentelemetry::Context` wrapping the carrier-build + global-propagator-extract, consumed by both MH's and MC's connection handlers (leaving the `set_parent` call, which is span-plumbing, at each call site). **Defer trigger / why-not-now**: MC's R-57 (task #6) is the natural second consumer — worth confirming at that point whether 2 call sites justify a named helper over the copy-paste, given how small the idiom is. Owner: dry-reviewer to propose at MC task #6; media-handler + meeting-controller co-consumers; observability owns the `common` helper home if hoisted. **Update 2026-07-06 (task #6 Gate 2)**: MC #6 landed the 2nd consumer — but instead of copy-pasting the inline idiom, it EXTRACTED the helpers into a named local module `crates/mc-service/src/webtransport/trace.rs` (`extract_parent`/`reparent_current_span`/`inject_current_context`, all over the GLOBAL propagator + the two `PROPAGATED_HEADERS` keys), consumed at every MC WT call site. It ALSO added the OUTBOUND inject half MH never had (populates `ServerMessage.{trace_parent,trace_state}` via `inject_context` into a `HashMap` injector — NO hand-rolled W3C string, verified at Gate 2). So the idiom is now N=2 for extract (MH inline + MC `trace.rs`) and N=1 for inject (MC only); the hoist target is UPGRADED from the old extract-only `extract_context_from_trace_fields` to a SYMMETRIC inject+extract pair over the two-field carrier, mirroring the already-shipped `common::observability::otel_http::{inject,extract}_trace_context` (the `http::HeaderMap` sibling of exactly this pattern). Kept local this PR per the Gate-1 DRY adjudication (a `common` hoist adds a cross-crate boundary beyond a wiring task); revisit when a 3rd consumer or an MH retrofit schedules the symmetric helper. (Superseded item (b) of the task-#6 consolidated breadcrumb below — canonical here.) Surfaced 2026-07-03 by @dry-reviewer Gate 1 review of task #27 (MH OTel wiring); corrected 2026-07-04 by @dry-reviewer Gate 2 review against the landed diff.
- [ ] **`SpanLayer` — tower ambient-span workaround for tonic `Interceptor`/span-timing, MH-only today (2026-07-04, task #27 Gate 2)**: Task #27 adds `crates/mh-service/src/grpc/span_layer.rs::SpanLayer` (a bare `tower::Layer`/`Service` pair) because `MediaHandlerServiceServer::with_interceptor(server_interceptor())` alone does NOT reparent the handler span: tonic's `Interceptor::call()` runs synchronously/eagerly inside `Service::call()`, before the handler's own `#[instrument]` span is created (that happens only when the response future is later polled), so `Span::current()` is `Span::none()` at extraction time and `set_parent()` silently no-ops. `SpanLayer` creates+enters a bare per-request span before the interceptor runs and `.instrument()`s the response future so the reparented span stays active through the handler's span creation. Verified NOT a duplicate of anything existing: grepped `crates/common` + AC/GC/MC for `tower::Layer`/`Instrument`/an ambient-span pattern — GC's `grpc/auth_layer.rs` and MC's `grpc/auth_interceptor.rs` are tower Layers too but solve JWT auth (already tracked as its own near-identical-layer extraction candidate under "gRPC Auth (Cross-Service)" in the dry-reviewer INDEX), not span timing; GC's inbound gRPC server (`main.rs`) does not use `otel_grpc::server_interceptor()` at all yet (no R-56 inbound wiring on GC), so GC hasn't hit this bug. Extraction opportunity (Pattern-A future-SPOT), NOT true duplication — self-contained, ~90 LoC, no `crates/common` changes. **The underlying bug is NOT MH-specific**: any service accepting inbound gRPC AND wiring `otel_grpc::server_interceptor()` behind a `tonic::transport::Server` will hit the identical eager-interceptor-vs-late-span timing gap. MC's own inbound gRPC servers (GC→MC `McAssignmentService`, MH→MC `McMediaCoordinationService`) will need the same fix whenever MC wires its own R-56 inbound extraction (no task number yet — MC's OTel wiring, task #6, is main.rs/config.rs scaffold only per the entry above; inbound interceptor wiring on MC's *own* gRPC server is a separate, not-yet-scheduled step). Likely target once a second consumer exists: hoist `SpanLayer` verbatim into `common::observability::otel_grpc` (it takes no MH-specific state — no config, no dependencies beyond `tower`/`tracing`) so both MH and MC's inbound servers `.layer(SpanLayer)` from one definition, and so the bug fix + its regression test live in the same module as `server_interceptor()` itself (arguably its correct home even at N=1, but hoisting speculatively before a second consumer's `Server::builder()` call site exists risks guessing at the wrong seam, e.g. if MC's server needs the layer to also carry auth-adjacent state). **Defer trigger / why-not-now**: N=1 — MC has no inbound `server_interceptor()` wiring yet to confirm the hoisted shape is actually shared as-is. Owner: dry-reviewer to propose when MC wires inbound R-56 extraction on its own gRPC server; media-handler + meeting-controller co-consumers; observability owns the `common` module if hoisted. **Update 2026-07-06 (task #6 Gate 2) — MC did NOT become the 2nd consumer; `SpanLayer` stays N=1 MH-only, DOWNGRADE to informational**: MC #6 wired inbound `server_interceptor()` on its own gRPC servers (GC→MC `MeetingControllerService`, MH→MC `MediaCoordinationService`) and hit the exact same eager-interceptor-vs-late-span gap — but resolved it with the OFF-THE-SHELF `tower_http::trace::TraceLayer::new_for_grpc()` as the outermost layer (matching GC's `main.rs`), NOT by copying `SpanLayer`. Verified MC's `McAuthService` carries the identical `BoxBody` body-type constraint as GC's `GrpcAuthService`, so the `.layer(TraceLayer).layer(auth_layer)` order drops in cleanly (zero new dependency). Net: GC + MC both use `TraceLayer::new_for_grpc()`; MH's `SpanLayer` is the intentional odd-one-out (its module docs deliberately choose a bare attribute-less anchor span over TraceLayer's full request span — a legitimate MH-local design, not a defect). The "any inbound-`server_interceptor()` service needs `SpanLayer`" premise is therefore VOID — TraceLayer is the proven zero-new-dep fix. No longer a hoist candidate; only revive at N=2 if a future service both needs the bare-anchor semantics AND copies `SpanLayer` verbatim. - [ ] **GC Claims struct duplicates common::jwt::ServiceClaims**: `crates/gc-service/src/auth/claims.rs` defines its own `Claims` identical to `common::jwt::ServiceClaims`. Should migrate to `pub type Claims = common::jwt::ServiceClaims;` (same pattern as AC). **Blocks GrpcAuthLayer extraction** (see GrpcAuthLayer entry below) — extraction to `common::grpc` requires unifying on `ServiceClaims` first, and that migration must also refactor GC's HTTP `validate_user()` path.
- [ ] **AC private MeetingTokenClaims/GuestTokenClaims duplicate common types**: `crates/ac-service/src/handlers/internal_tokens.rs:235-264` defines private `MeetingTokenClaims`/`GuestTokenClaims` structs that are structurally near-identical to `common::jwt::MeetingTokenClaims`/`common::jwt::GuestTokenClaims`. AC should migrate to use the common types. **Deepened 2026-08-06 by devloop `2026-08-06-roster-display-names` (dry-reviewer Gate 3)**: the roster-display-name carrier fix added a `display_name` field to BOTH `MeetingTokenClaims` copies in lockstep — `common::jwt::MeetingTokenClaims` (as `#[serde(default) ] pub display_name: String`, Debug-redacted) and the AC-local serialize-only struct at `internal_tokens.rs` — so the two now share one MORE field that must be kept wire-agreeing by hand (they already agreed on the `display_name` wire key: common uses serde-default field naming, AC-local emits the same key, no `#[serde(rename)]` on either). This is exactly the drift surface this entry warns about: the deserialize side defaults-on-missing while the serialize side always emits, so a future field added to only one copy would silently diverge. Per the ADR-0019 DRY Reviewer Exception this stayed TODO-tracked (not fix-or-defer) — consolidating the two structs would touch the JWT signing + MC validation surface, well beyond a carrier fix. No THIRD copy was introduced by this devloop (the MC/MH/`mc-test-utils` edits are field additions to struct-literals of the *common* type, i.e. consumers, not new definitions). The consolidation target is unchanged: migrate the AC-local serialize path onto `common::jwt::MeetingTokenClaims` (its field types differ — AC-local uses `String` for `participant_type`/`role`/`home_org_id` vs common's enums/`Option`, so the migration must reconcile those), collapsing this and entry #54 (`sign_meeting_jwt`/`sign_guest_jwt`) together. - [ ] **`add_auth` helper duplication (4 call sites)**: `crates/mc-service/src/grpc/gc_client.rs:GcClient::add_auth`, `crates/mc-service/src/grpc/mh_client.rs:MhClient::add_auth`, `crates/mh-service/src/grpc/gc_client.rs:GcClient::add_auth`, `crates/mh-service/src/grpc/mc_client.rs:McClient::add_auth` — all ~10 lines identical (create Request, get token from TokenReceiver, format Bearer header, insert metadata, map parse error). Extract crate-local free function in each crate's `grpc/mod.rs` first; extract to `common` when a third crate needs it.
- [ ] **The `GcClient` pair's interval-application idiom is the same clone drifting, and it hides an ADR-0036 §8 applied-echo gap in two proto messages (owner: protocol + global-controller for the proto half; mc/mh for the Rust half)**: attached to the `add_auth` entry above rather than filed separately, because it is the *same* near-duplicate pair — `crates/mc-service/src/grpc/gc_client.rs` and `crates/mh-service/src/grpc/gc_client.rs` — drifting further apart, and describing one pair in two entries is the failure this section exists to prevent. `crates/mc-service/src/grpc/gc_client.rs:239-252` applies GC-pushed cadences as `if response.fast_heartbeat_interval_ms > 0 { …store }` (and again for the comprehensive interval); `crates/mh-service/src/grpc/gc_client.rs` carries the identical idiom for `load_report_interval_ms`. Three instances, one shape. **The latent defect the idiom encodes**: if GC sends 0 the receiver silently keeps its own default and GC never learns which cadence is actually live — `RegisterMCResponse` and `RegisterMHResponse` are push channels whose responses cannot express *"my live behaviour reflects what you sent"*, only *"received and parsed"*. That is precisely the failure ADR-0036 §8 fixes for `RegisterMeeting` with `applied_generation`, and precisely the cautionary precedent §11 cites — "a capacity value advertised to GC and enforced nowhere: declared in one place, assumed in another, verified nowhere." Surfaced 2026-09-01 by protocol's mechanism restatement at Gate 1 of `docs/devloop-outputs/2026-09-01-internal-contract-reshape/` and narrowed by @dry-reviewer, who checked the *apply sites* rather than the message shapes: **`AssignMeetingWithMhResponse` is NOT a member** — `crates/mc-service/src/grpc/mc_service.rs` applies synchronously (`store_mh_assignment(...).await?` completes before the response is constructed) and `rejection_reason` already makes failure expressible. Including it would make this entry over-claim, with the concrete cost that someone later "fixes" it by adding an echo field that echoes nothing. The mechanism is **asynchronous or silently-partial apply**, not "the response is a boolean". Two messages, not three. Note the separate entry at the top of this file proposes *deleting* `accepted` from these same messages; these are compatible — `accepted` is the field that cannot mean "applied", and the fix is a real applied-echo, not a better boolean.

The integration variant uses `Box::leak` instead of `OnceLock` but the intent is identical. Candidate for extraction to `mc-test-utils`.
- [ ] **GrpcAuthLayer extraction (GC/MC/MH)**: GC, MC, and MH all now have async JWKS-based two-layer auth (`GrpcAuthLayer`/`GrpcAuthService`, `McAuthLayer`/`McAuthService`, `MhAuthLayer`/`MhAuthService`) that are structurally near-identical tower Layer/Service implementations with the same 5-step validation chain (structural fast-path, JWKS crypto, scope check, `service_type` routing, claims injection). All three include identical `classify_jwt_error()` helpers and `failure_reason` metrics. Remaining differences: `REQUIRED_SCOPE` constant value (`service.write.{gc,mc,mh}`), Layer 2 path routing table (GC maps 2 gRPC services, MC maps 2, MH maps 1), tracing targets (`{gc,mc,mh}.grpc.auth`), metric prefix (`{gc,mc,mh}_`). Extract to `common::grpc::GrpcAuthLayer` parameterized by scope, routing table, and metric prefix. **Blocker**: GC still uses local `auth::Claims` while MC/MH use `common::jwt::ServiceClaims` — see the "GC Claims struct duplicates common::jwt::ServiceClaims" entry above. Unify the claims type first, then extract. **Also carry into extraction**: the `caller_type_rejected_total{actual_type}` label takes `claims.service_type` directly from the JWT, which is technically unbounded if AC ever issues a token with a novel `service_type`. Cross-service parity (all three services behave identically) is intact today; the extraction should add a match-and-allowlist at the recording site (known values → as-is; everything else → `"unknown"`). Locations: `crates/gc-service/src/grpc/auth_layer.rs:GrpcAuthLayer`, `crates/mc-service/src/grpc/auth_interceptor.rs:McAuthLayer`, `crates/mh-service/src/grpc/auth_interceptor.rs:MhAuthLayer`.

- [ ] **`IWebTransport` interface canonical-home decision (test-utils ↔ sdk-core, R-13/R-39)**: `packages/test-utils/src/contracts/IWebTransport.ts` declares a minimal `IWebTransport`/`WebTransportBidirectionalStream`/`WebTransportCloseInfo` shape so `MockWebTransport` has something to implement. test-utils ships first (browser-client-join task #8); sdk-core ships in task #9 with the canonical production interface (R-13). Current strategy is **Pattern A — parallel structural declarations**: when sdk-core lands its `IWebTransport`, `MockWebTransport` will satisfy both via TypeScript structural typing. No circular dep, no dev-dep edge from sdk-core's prod build. Decision to revisit at task #9 review time: (a) keep Pattern A indefinitely (cheap while the shape is stable), or (b) flip to test-utils dev-depending on sdk-core's canonical declaration. Trigger for (b): meaningful shape evolution (e.g., adding stream-prioritization, congestion-control hints, or a third optional field) that risks the two declarations drifting silently. Surfaced during browser-client-join task #8 DRY review (2026-05-06). **Task #9 resolution (2026-06-21): kept Pattern A.** sdk-core landed the canonical `IWebTransport` at `packages/sdk-core/src/transport/IWebTransport.ts` with the identical shape; no dep edge either direction. The drift forcing-function is now live: an explicit `const transport: IWebTransport = mock;` (sdk-core interface ← `@darktower/test-utils` `MockWebTransport`) in `packages/sdk-core/src/transport/__tests__/BrowserWebTransport.test.ts` fails to compile if sdk-core's interface grows a member the mock lacks. The residual gap (test-utils copy gaining a field sdk-core lacks) stays one-directional, as noted. Revisit trigger (b) unchanged; likely consolidates with the `MetricsSink` entry below at task #12.

- [ ] **`MetricsSink` interface canonical-home decision (test-utils ↔ sdk-core, R-24/R-39)**: `packages/test-utils/src/contracts/MetricsSink.ts` declares the minimal `MetricsSink` + `MetricLabels` contract that `InMemoryMetricsSink` (R-39, this PR) implements. The interface contract is **co-owned with observability** per user-story §Design line 256. sdk-core (task #12, R-24) will ship the canonical `MetricsSink` + production impls (`OtelMetricsSink`, `ConsoleMetricsSink`, `NoopMetricsSink`). Same Pattern A reasoning as the `IWebTransport` entry above: parallel structural declarations, no circular dep. Decision to revisit at task #12 review time, **paired with the `IWebTransport` entry** — likely both interfaces consolidate together. Trigger for "extract to sdk-core, test-utils consumes": adding a fourth method (e.g., `histogramWithExplicitBuckets`), changing `MetricLabels` shape, or introducing a base-trait/factory pattern that benefits from one source of truth. Subset-filter assertion semantics (`InMemoryMetricsSink.assertCounter` etc.) intentionally mirror `crates/common/src/observability/testing.rs:681,769,861` `MetricAssertion::with_labels(...)`; cross-language symmetry is not duplication. Surfaced during browser-client-join task #8 DRY review (2026-05-06). **Task #12 resolution (2026-06-24): kept Pattern A.** sdk-core landed the canonical `MetricsSink` + `MetricLabels` at `packages/sdk-core/src/telemetry/MetricsSink.ts` (named convention author per ADR-0024 §6.5 Pattern B) plus the four sinks. The intended consolidation (test-utils `export type`-re-exports sdk-core) was attempted but **REVERTED**: it adds a `test-utils → @darktower/sdk-core` package edge, and since sdk-core already devDepends test-utils (test doubles), Nx rejects the resulting build-task cycle (`sdk-core:build → test-utils:build → sdk-core:build`, verified via `nx run-many -t build`). So both copies stay as parallel structural declarations, now drift-locked by a compile-time conformance test `packages/sdk-core/src/telemetry/__tests__/contractParity.test.ts` (asserts the two interfaces are mutually assignable — `tsc` fails on drift). Re-evaluate (b) "extract to one source" only if the Nx graph can be restructured to break the cycle (e.g. splitting test doubles off sdk-core's build inputs), or on a shape change that the parity test alone cannot hold.

- [ ] **`ts-name-guard-dt-client` should ALSO check `sink.counter('dt_client_*')` literal CALL SITES (dt-guard, task #13/#14 follow-up)**: today the guard (`crates/dt-guard/src/ts_metric_naming.rs`) only checks string literals passed to OTel `meter.createCounter/createHistogram/createGauge(...)` factory calls. Task #12 introduced the `// dt-metric-name-dynamic: <reason>` escape hatch so the canonical generic `OtelMetricsSink` — which forwards a runtime-validated VARIABLE name to `meter.createX(name)` — opts out (it validates via `assertClientMetricName(name, mode)` on the line above; the marker rescues ONLY the non-literal case, never a bad literal — enforced by `ts_metric_naming::tests::bad_literal_with_marker_present_still_hard_fails`). BUT the actual `dt_client_*` metric NAME LITERALS will live at the **emission call sites** in #13/#14 (`sink.counter('dt_client_join_attempts_total', ...)`), which the guard does NOT scan today (they are `.counter(` not `.createCounter(`). **Action (observability-policy + infrastructure-tooling, co-owned per ADR-0034)**: when #13/#14 add those call sites, extend the guard to also validate the first string-literal arg of `.counter(`/`.histogram(`/`.gauge(` on a `MetricsSink` so a bad literal at the emission site is caught statically (the runtime guard catches it too, but CI should catch typos pre-merge). The dynamic-name marker covers the generic sink internals only; it is NOT a license to skip literal-call-site checking. Surfaced 2026-06-24 at task #12 Gate-2 (Layer 3 name-guard conflict resolution).

- [ ] **Client SDK OTel bundle-size budget (sdk-core, task #12)**: task #12 added 7 `@opentelemetry/*` runtime deps to `@darktower/sdk-core` (its first runtime deps) for the client telemetry scaffolding (R-19/R-24). They are **externalized** from sdk-core's own bundle (Vite `rollupOptions.external = [/^@opentelemetry\//]`), so sdk-core's `dist/index.mjs` stays small (~21.7 KB, unchanged from pre-OTel) and emits bare `import '@opentelemetry/...'`. **The size cost lands on the bundling CONSUMER** (`@darktower/web-app`, task #15+), which will pull the OTel SDK + OTLP-proto exporter + protobuf transformer into its app bundle. No budget is enforced yet. **Action (operations, by web-app bundle-analysis at task #15+)**: measure the OTel contribution to the web-app production bundle, decide whether to (a) accept it, (b) lazy-load telemetry behind a dynamic `import()` so it is off the join critical path, or (c) trim the dep set (e.g. drop `sdk-trace-web` if the root-span helper can use `@opentelemetry/api` + `core` alone). Pinned exact versions: api 1.9.1, core/resources/sdk-metrics/sdk-trace-web 2.8.0, exporter-metrics-otlp-proto 0.219.0, semantic-conventions 1.41.1. Surfaced 2026-06-24 by @operations at task #12 Gate-1 (deliberate deferred decision).

- [ ] **Name-guard prelude shared between `ConsoleMetricsSink` + `OtelMetricsSink` (sdk-core telemetry, task #12)**: `packages/sdk-core/src/telemetry/ConsoleMetricsSink.ts` and `OtelMetricsSink.ts` share an identical guard prelude — the `readonly #mode: GuardMode` field, the `constructor(mode: GuardMode = 'throw')` default, and the `if (!assertClientMetricName(name, this.#mode)) return;` first line in all three of `counter`/`histogram`/`gauge` (6 guard-line sites + 2 copies of the mode plumbing across the two classes). Genuinely parallel structure, but held at extraction-candidate (NOT extracted now) because: (a) N=2 within-package classes, not cross-service — below the ADR-0019 ≥3 threshold; (b) a shared `abstract GuardedMetricsSink` base would abstract over ~2 trivial fields and a one-line guard call, borderline-not-worth-the-indirection at N=2; (c) `NoopMetricsSink` deliberately skips the guard (emits nothing) so a common base could not cover all three production sinks anyway — the guarded subset is only Console+Otel. **Extraction trigger**: a 3rd name-guarded sink lands (e.g. a batching/buffering sink, or a sampling wrapper), at which point a `GuardedMetricsSink` base (holds `#mode`, exposes a `protected guard(name): boolean`) pays for itself. Surfaced 2026-06-24 at task #12 Gate-2 @dry-reviewer review.

- [ ] **`wait_for_busy` test-helper busy-wait pattern (devloop-helper, 6 sites)**: `crates/devloop-helper/src/main.rs` `concurrency_tests` module repeats the same 8-line busy-wait at 6 call sites: `for _ in 0..100 { if h.ctx.snapshot_busy().is_some() { break; } std::thread::sleep(Duration::from_millis(20)); } assert!(h.ctx.snapshot_busy().is_some());`. Iter-1 had 1 site; iter-2 (2026-05-03 helper-concurrency, process-group cancel + `cancel_pending`) added 5 more — clears the ADR-0019 ≥3-4-site threshold for an extraction opportunity. Extraction candidate: `fn wait_for_busy(ctx: &Context)` (or method on `TestHelperWithCtx`) returning `BusyHint` so callers can inline `let bh = wait_for_busy(&h.ctx);` and drop the assert. Low priority (test-only, single crate, behavior is correct) — surface on next material edit to `concurrency_tests`. Surfaced 2026-05-03 by @dry-reviewer iter-2 verdict.
- [ ] **`trace_parent`/`trace_state` envelope-level field pair (3 envelopes in `proto/signaling.proto`)**: Browser-client-join Task #2 added flat `string trace_parent = 20; string trace_state = 21;` (W3C Trace Context, RFC 9287) at envelope level on `ClientMessage`, `ServerMessage`, and `MhClientMessage`. The user story design explicitly chose flat fields over a `TraceContext { string trace_parent = 1; string trace_state = 2; }` sub-message because (a) the wrapping cost (1-2 bytes per envelope) repeats on every signaling message, (b) generated SDKs see one extra layer of nesting per envelope access, and (c) the field tags 20/21 are fixed by spec. Worth revisiting if a third trace-context field (e.g. `baggage` per W3C Baggage spec) is ever added — at that point the cost-benefit of a sub-message flips because (i) tag-space management gets tighter and (ii) the per-field doc-comment surface grows linearly with envelopes × fields. Until then, flat is correct. Surfaced during browser-client-join Task #2 DRY review (2026-05-03).

- [ ] **Layer-runner loop duplicated between `scripts/layer-all.sh` and `scripts/verify-completion.sh` (Wave 1 #1, ADR-0033)**: `scripts/layer-all.sh:32-51` and `scripts/verify-completion.sh:102-113` both implement the per-layer-invoke + tee + `parse_status_line` loop (~12 lines each). The `--layer full` path in verify-completion already delegates to layer-all (verify-completion.sh:89-92), so duplication is bounded to the quick/standard branch. Wave-2-or-later refactor: add `--layers <list>` option to layer-all.sh and have verify-completion.sh invoke it for the quick/standard subsets too. Both files are correct as-is; this is a structural deduplication to land before a third caller emerges. Surfaced during ADR-0033 Wave 1 #1 DRY review (2026-05-08).

- [ ] **GH Actions setup steps duplicated across `ci.yml` and `ci-client.yml` (task #17, 2026-06-23)**: `ci-client.yml` (new) and `ci.yml` share 5–6 identical step blocks: `actions/checkout`, `pnpm/action-setup@v4 version: 10.33.2`, `actions/setup-node@v4 node-version: '22' cache: pnpm`, `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile`, `bufbuild/buf-setup-action@v1`, and `Swatinem/rust-cache`. Inside `ci-client.yml` alone the pnpm+Node+install block is repeated across three jobs (lint, unit, component). GitHub Actions has no native cross-workflow step sharing without composite actions. Extraction candidate: `.github/actions/setup-client/action.yml` composite action for the pnpm+Node+install trio (used 3× within `ci-client.yml`, potentially reusable by `ci.yml`); the Rust+dt-guard build block is lint-job-only so lower priority. Composite extraction also centralises the Dependabot version-bump surface (currently 3 jobs in `ci-client.yml` + `ci.yml` = 4 copies of the `pnpm/action-setup` version). **Defer trigger / why-not-now**: N=2 workflows and N=3 jobs; correct as-is; composite action adds indirection with minimal payoff at this scale. Natural trigger: a third client-tier workflow (e.g. E2E browser CI) or a fourth job in `ci-client.yml`. Owner: infrastructure. Surfaced 2026-06-23 by @dry-reviewer Gate 1 review of task #17.

- [ ] **Nx `targetDefaults.lint` command not extracted — per-package `project.json` lint duplicates (task #17, 2026-06-23)**: Task #17 adds `tsc --noEmit && eslint . && prettier --check .` to `sdk-core/project.json` and `test-utils/project.json` lint targets (N=2 copies). `nx.json::targetDefaults.lint` currently sets `inputs`+`cache` only, not a default `command`. A default `options.command` in `targetDefaults` could replace both per-package copies for TS-source packages; `proto-gen` (buf lint) would still need its own override. **Defer trigger / why-not-now**: only 2 TS-source packages today; per-package entries remain readable and idiomatic; `targetDefaults` default would be invisible when reading individual configs. Natural trigger: a third TS package joins the workspace. Owner: infrastructure. Surfaced 2026-06-23 by @dry-reviewer Gate 1 review of task #17.

- [x] **`lang/<X>/changed.test.sh` locality-self-test harness duplicated (Wave 1 #2, ADR-0033)** — **RESOLVED 2026-08-20 by deletion.** The `changed.sh` classifier and its `changed.test.sh` self-tests (rust/ts/proto) + `run_with_cache` were removed entirely when the skip-if-untouched policy was retired (ADR-0033 §2/§3 amendments; validation-run-all devloop). With the files gone, the duplicated harness they shared is gone too — the DRY debt this entry tracked is cleared incidentally, exactly as predicted for the remove option.

- [ ] **`scripts/lang/ts/{compile,fmt,lint,test}.sh` shared prelude — `_nx_affected.sh` extraction (Wave 2 #5, ADR-0033)**: The four TS runtime wrappers (`scripts/lang/ts/compile.sh`, `fmt.sh`, `lint.sh`, `test.sh` — added Wave 2 #5 / task #36) share an identical ~7-line prelude: shebang + one-line verb comment + `set -euo pipefail` + `IFS=$'\n\t'` + `__here` resolution + `source _common.sh` + `BASE_SHA="$(...)"` resolution, before the single line `run_and_emit "nx-<target>" pnpm exec nx affected -t <target> --base="$BASE_SHA" "$@"`. Total redundancy: 4 × ~7 lines = ~28 lines. The Rust counterparts (`scripts/lang/rust/{compile,fmt,lint}.sh`) share only a 4-line prelude (no `BASE_SHA` resolution), which is why Rust did not warrant extraction. Likely target: `scripts/lang/ts/_nx_affected.sh` exposing `run_nx_target <target> <reason-prefix> [args...]` that internally resolves `BASE_SHA` and calls `run_and_emit`. **Defer triggers**: (a) a 5th Nx-driven TS wrapper lands (e.g., `e2e.sh` in task #15 or a `coverage.sh` follow-on), OR (b) a second Nx-consuming language directory is added (would justify lifting from `lang/ts/_nx_affected.sh` to `lang/_nx_affected.sh`). Current 4-instance shape is borderline; held at deferred because parallel-structure-with-Rust + N=4 + low individual prelude weight keeps the wrappers self-describing for the 3am debugger (ADR-0033 §6 trade-off framing). (A prior draft of this entry cited a 4×-duplicated `TODO(post-#36)` comment as additional consolidation pressure; that pressure was retired at Gate 3 by relocating the marker into `scripts/lang/_get_base_ref.sh` + `/work/TODO.md` per code-reviewer F1, so the extraction trigger now depends purely on (a) or (b).) Surfaced during Wave 2 #5 DRY review (2026-05-11).

- [ ] **`tests/common/test_state.rs` per-service test scaffolding (AC + MC + GC, three-crate sibling)**: `crates/ac-service/tests/common/test_state.rs` (AC, ADR-0032 Step 4 — `make_app_state`, `seed_signing_key`, `seed_service_credential`, `TEST_CLIENT_SECRET`), `crates/mc-service/tests/common/mod.rs` (MC, ADR-0032 Step 3 — equivalent per-service AppState/Config builders + DB seeding helpers), and `crates/gc-service/tests/common/jwt_fixtures.rs` (GC, ADR-0032 Step 5 — JWT signing fixtures: `TestKeypair`/`TestUserClaims`/`TestServiceClaims`/`build_pkcs8_from_seed`) all house in-crate test scaffolding. Three-crate sibling pattern matches the per-service Cat B precedent (`record_token_refresh_metrics`). Likely consolidation target if a fourth caller emerges: extend the per-service `*-test-utils` crate if the helpers stay service-private (most likely — each service has a distinct `AppState`/JWKS topology), or promote to a workspace-level `crates/test-utils-common` if a test-helper genuinely needs cross-service share. Evaluate when 4th caller emerges. Surfaced during ADR-0032 Step 4 @dry-reviewer Finding 2; GC sibling added in Step 5.

- [ ] **GC dashboard panel for `gc_meeting_join_*` `participant` axis (ADR-0032 Step 5)**: The `participant=user|guest` label was added to the `gc_meeting_join_total` / `gc_meeting_join_duration_seconds` / `gc_meeting_join_failures_total` family in Step 5. Existing panels in `infra/grafana/dashboards/gc-overview.json` (`sum by(status)`, `sum by(error_type)`, success-rate ratio) work unchanged because Prometheus aggregates over the new label by default. A dedicated `by(participant)` panel would let operators triage user-vs-guest failure spikes at a glance — e.g. a `gc_meeting_join_failures_total{participant="guest", error_type="guests_disabled"}` rate panel signals meetings hosting unintended guest-join attempts. Estimated ~30 LoC to `gc-overview.json` (one new panel section). Deferred from Step 5 per @team-lead authorization (ADR-0032 Step 5 plan-stage scope decision).

- [ ] **GC db_query wrapper-Cat-C closure (ADR-0032 Step 5)**: 4 of 9 `gc_db_queries_total` ops have no business-logic error branch reachable from a component test (`activate_meeting`, `register_mh`, `update_load_report`, `mark_stale_mh_unhealthy` — UPDATE no-match returns `Ok`, idempotent UPSERT, or only `?`-propagated `sqlx` errors). Their error-status emissions are wrapper-Cat-C in `crates/gc-service/tests/db_metrics_integration.rs` with the canonical comment block. Replacement requires a `sqlx`-level fault-injection harness (poison-pill connection wrapper, ~50 LoC per op for in-DB setup). Lower priority than the typed-label-source weak-form rule. (3 additional ops — `count_active_participants`, `add_participant`, `remove_participant` — are tracked separately as orphan recording sites; see entry below.)

Disposition options: (a) wire the caller (closes the gap), (b) remove the dormant fn + recording site, (c) document as deferred-to-feature. Currently known orphans: the entire `ParticipantsRepository` (`count_active_participants`, `add_participant`, `remove_participant` — all in `crates/gc-service/src/repositories/participants.rs`). Production handler integration pending — see `crates/gc-service/src/repositories/mod.rs:21` "will be used in meeting join handler". Block on: ADR-0033 weak-form typed-label-source rule landing first. - [ ] **GC `gc_caller_type_rejected_total{actual_type}` cardinality clamping (ADR-0032 Step 5 finding F1)**: `crates/gc-service/src/grpc/auth_layer.rs:241` reads `claims.service_type` directly into the label without allowlist-clamping to {`meeting-controller`, `media-handler`, `global-controller`}. AC's JWKS-issuance trust boundary makes this low-severity (only AC-signed tokens reach the rejection branch, and AC emits only the 4 enumerated values), but a forged or off-spec token would inject an arbitrary string as a Prometheus label. Fix: clamp at the emission site with a `match` over known values, defaulting to `"unknown"`. ~5 LoC. Surfaced during ADR-0032 Step 5 @observability F1 finding.

- [ ] **TS production-file path filter — `no-secrets-in-ts.sh` + `no-pii-in-logs-ts.sh` (Wave 2 #6, ADR-0033)**: Both guards carry a byte-identical 16-line inline filter (constraint-#6 directories + type-decl + test/fixture path excludes) at `scripts/guards/simple/ts/no-secrets-in-ts.sh:58-75` and `no-pii-in-logs-ts.sh:64-81`. `name-guard-dt-client.sh:68-84` uses a related-but-distinct shape (positive include-list prefix gate `^packages/(sdk-core|web-app)/src/` first, then a SUBSET of the same excludes), so it is NOT a 3rd caller for extraction purposes (rule of three with asymmetry — the predicate is different in kind, not degree). **Defer trigger**: a 4th simple/ts/ guard that needs the EXACT same byte-identical filter (e.g., `no-eval-in-ts.sh`, `no-debug-statements-ts.sh`). **Likely target**: `filter_ts_production_paths()` in `scripts/guards/common.sh` taking a file list on stdin/argv, emitting filtered files on stdout. **Why not a variadic `filter_test_code_ts <file> [extra-patterns...]`**: that signature would invite misuse by include-list callers (like `name-guard-dt-client`) and paper over the include/exclude asymmetry. Structural alignment requirement (same var names `FILE_LIST`/`f`/`CHANGED`, same regex anchor, same comment shape pointing at sibling) was honored by the implementer per @code-reviewer's review framework, so future extraction is mechanical. Surfaced 2026-05-12 by ADR-0033 Wave 2 #6 DRY review.

- [ ] **Cross-stack `CANONICAL_SERVICES` mirror — Rust ↔ Bash (ADR-0034 Wave 1 F-DRY-3 residue, 2026-05-19)**: Wave 1 collapsed the 3 Rust mirrors of `{ac, gc, mc, mh}` into the canonical `crates/dt-guard/src/common/services.rs::CANONICAL_SERVICES` const + `SERVICE_METRIC_PREFIX_RE` derived from it. The 5th mirror — `scripts/guards/common.sh:343 CANONICAL_SERVICES` (bash associative array) — remains a separate stack. Cross-stack collapse to a single SoT requires either (a) bash codegen from Rust (e.g., a `dt-guard emit-services-shell` subcommand that prints the array on demand, sourced by `common.sh`), (b) a TOML/JSON intermediary read by both stacks at startup, or (c) a `validate-services-sync.sh` meta-guard that fails when the two enumerations diverge. Adding a new service still requires updating Rust + Bash separately until then. **Defer trigger**: a 5th service joins (likely to surface the divergence as a real bug rather than hypothetical), OR a Rust-side refactor renames a service prefix (the bash mirror would silently keep the old name). Surfaced 2026-05-19 by ADR-0034 Wave 1 DRY review F-DRY-3 cross-stack residue.

- [ ] **Cross-stack exclusion/slug-derivation parity — Bash `_gate2_binding.sh` ↔ Rust `cross_boundary_scope.rs` (task #51 Gate-2 authority gate, 2026-06-09)**: `scripts/lang/_gate2_binding.sh::gate2_is_excluded` deliberately mirrors `crates/dt-guard/src/cross_boundary_scope.rs::is_symmetric_exclusion` (`:64-84`) entry-for-entry — `docs/TODO.md` exact, `docs/specialist-knowledge/*/INDEX.md` single-segment, `docs/user-stories/*.md` depth-2 — and `gate2_derive_slug` / `gate2_staged_complete_slug` mirror `find_active_main_md` (`:192-202`). Same cross-stack-no-single-SoT class as the `CANONICAL_SERVICES` Rust↔Bash entry below: the two stacks cannot share executable code (the pre-commit hook is coreutils-only by design — it must NOT depend on a built dt-guard binary, see `_gate2_binding.sh:16-22`), so the parity is maintained by hand. The ONE intentional divergence is documented and correct: gate2 widens `docs/devloop-outputs/**` tree-wide (vs the Rust precedent's self-referential single-main.md exemption) because Gate-3 write-back would otherwise self-invalidate the binding (`_gate2_binding.sh:106-111`). NOT true duplication — no shared helper was reimplemented (the Rust fn is a guard-policy function, not a sourceable bash SoT); per the ADR-0019 DRY Reviewer Exception this is TODO-tracked, not fix-or-defer. **Risk if it drifts**: a Rust-side change to `is_symmetric_exclusion` (e.g. adding/removing an exempt path, or tightening `is_user_story_path`) would silently desync the gate2 binding's universe from the cross-boundary-scope guard's, so a file could be bound by one and exempt by the other. The `scripts/guards/simple/selftest-gate2-verdict.sh` (j)-block asserts the bash predicate's direction (MUST-BE-BOUND vs MUST-BE-EXCLUDED smuggling paths) but does NOT cross-check it against the Rust precedent at build time. **Defer trigger / consolidation options**: (a) a `dt-guard emit-exclusions-shell` subcommand that prints the predicate for `_gate2_binding.sh` to source (mirrors option (a) of the `CANONICAL_SERVICES` entry) — but this would reintroduce the built-binary dependency the hook intentionally avoids, so it is only viable if the hook is allowed a graceful binary-absent fallback; (b) a `validate-gate2-exclusion-parity` meta-guard (Rust-side test, runs in the bash-4 pipeline not the hook) that asserts the bash predicate and the Rust fn agree over a shared fixture-path corpus — this is the lower-risk option (keeps the hook coreutils-only, catches drift in CI). Recommended: option (b) when either side's exclusion set next changes, OR if a third consumer of the exclusion semantics emerges. Owner: dry-reviewer to propose; infrastructure (gate2 owner) + code-reviewer (cross_boundary_scope owner) co-consumers. Surfaced 2026-06-09 by dry-reviewer Gate 2 review of task #51 (`docs/devloop-outputs/2026-06-09-gate2-authority-verdict-gate/main.md`).

- [x] **`MacroKind`-derived alternation in `crates/dt-guard/src/metric_macros.rs`** (ADR-0034 Wave 1 E-DRY-1, surfaced 2026-05-19; resolved 2026-05-22 in Wave 2 #45). Wave 2 collapsed the three-way encoding onto `MacroKind::ALL` as the single source of truth: `impl MacroKind { pub const ALL: &'static [Self] = &[...]; }` at `crates/dt-guard/src/metric_macros.rs:42-93`; `LABEL_MACROS` + `DESCRIBE_MACROS` + `MACRO_NAME_ALTERNATION` all derived from `MacroKind::ALL` at `:99-127`; the regex statics `MACRO_INVOCATION_RE` + `MACRO_INVOCATION_WITH_FIRST_ARG_RE` consume `*MACRO_NAME_ALTERNATION` so adding a new variant auto-extends every consumer. Defer-trigger (fourth macro family / cargo-feature-gating) closes without intervention. Resolution pointer: `docs/devloop-outputs/2026-05-20-wave2-bash-guards-dt-guard/main.md` (Wave-2 in-loop fix-up).

- [x] **8-way wrapper-prelude duplication in `scripts/guards/simple/*.sh` dt-guard wrappers (ADR-0034 Wave 1 E-DRY-2, 2026-05-19)**: Resolved 2026-05-20 in `docs/devloop-outputs/2026-05-19-wave1-python-guards-dt-guard/` in-loop fix-pass (landed early; user accepted the "tracking overhead exceeds fix cost + defer trigger unlikely to fire as a fix-event" framing). Extracted shared prelude to `scripts/guards/simple/_dt_guard_wrapper.sh` (non-executable so `run-guards.sh:180` `[[ -x ]]` gate skips it). Each of the 8 wrappers collapses to a 3-line `source ... <subcommand>` invocation; helper computes SCRIPT_DIR/REPO_ROOT/DT_GUARD + binary-missing check + `exec "$DT_GUARD" <subcommand> --root "$REPO_ROOT"`. Smoke-tested: STATUS=OK path via `validate-doc-citations-no-line-numbers.sh` → exit 0; binary-missing path via `DT_GUARD=/nonexistent` → `STATUS=FAIL REASON=dt-guard-binary-missing` + exit 1; full guard suite 31/31 pass.

- [x] **`scripts/guards/run-guards.sh` per-guard timeout case-classifier duplicated across verbose / non-verbose branches (Wave 1 #11 test F1)**: Resolved 2026-05-19 in `docs/devloop-outputs/2026-05-19-wave1-python-guards-dt-guard/` in-loop fix-pass. Extracted `classify_guard_exit(exit_code, captured)` shell function at `scripts/guards/run-guards.sh:132`, called from both verbose branch (`:189`, empty captured) and non-verbose branch (`:196`, `$OUTPUT`). Load-bearing `|| true` sentinel + comment block + capture-form preserved verbatim. Smoke-tested with 10s-sleep fake guard vs 1s timeout → exit 124 → `STATUS=FAIL REASON=guard-timeout-validate-sleepy` correct in both modes.

- [ ] **Per-toolchain cache-volume + env-var pattern in `infra/devloop/devloop.sh` `podman run` (3 instances, browser-client-join task #42)**: After task #42 lands, the `podman run -d --name "$DEV_CONTAINER"` block at `infra/devloop/devloop.sh:516-532` carries 3 sibling cache-volume mounts paired with toolchain env vars: `-v cargo-registry:/tmp/cargo-home/registry` + `-v cargo-git:/tmp/cargo-home/git` + `-e CARGO_HOME=/tmp/cargo-home` (cargo, two volumes one var), and `-v pnpm-store:/tmp/pnpm-store` + `-e npm_config_store_dir=/tmp/pnpm-store` (pnpm, one volume one var). The shape is "shared download cache as a named volume + toolchain env var pointing at it"; the user-story §"Surfaced Feedback Log" explicitly endorses this as the cross-toolchain canonical for devloop dev containers. **Defer trigger**: a 3rd toolchain joins (likely candidates: uv/pip cache for Python tooling if Python ever lands in the workspace; a Java/Maven/Gradle local repo if a JVM consumer of the proto contracts emerges; a Go module cache if Go services are added). **Likely target**: a small bash helper in `infra/devloop/devloop.sh` (or a sourced fragment) that registers `(volume-name, container-path, env-var-name, env-var-value)` tuples and emits the corresponding `-v`/`-e` `podman run` args, OR — if the cardinality stays at 3 toolchains and only the `podman run` call needs them — a literal block kept as-is. Current 3-instance shape is below the abstraction threshold because (a) the cargo pair (registry + git) is structurally distinct from the pnpm singleton, so a tuple helper would have to model the 1-or-2-volumes-per-toolchain variability, and (b) the env-var names diverge per toolchain (`CARGO_HOME` vs. `npm_config_store_dir`) so no single var-name convention emerges. Held at deferred — three sibling lines next to each other in `podman run` args is the right level of explicitness. Surfaced 2026-05-14 by browser-client-join task #42 DRY review (R-62, ADR-0033 Wave 2 follow-up).

- [ ] **`tracing_subscriber::registry()` init block duplicated across all four service `main.rs` (browser-client-join task #24)**: `crates/ac-service/src/main.rs:27-33`, `crates/gc-service/src/main.rs:56-62`, `crates/mc-service/src/main.rs:78-84`, `crates/mh-service/src/main.rs:52-58` each carry a near-identical 6-line `tracing_subscriber::registry().with(EnvFilter::try_from_default_env().unwrap_or_else(|_| "<svc>=<level>,tower_http=debug".into())).with(fmt::layer().json()).init()` block. Once the four service-wiring tasks (#25 AC, #26 GC, #6 MC, #27 MH) land, each will additionally `.with(otel.layer)` from the common OTel helper (`crates/common/src/observability/otel.rs::init_otel`) before `.init()`, so the duplication grows from 6 to 7 lines per service. **Defer trigger**: a 5th service joins the workspace, OR a 3rd common layer (e.g. a metrics-events layer) needs composing into the same Registry stack. **Likely target**: `common::observability::install_default_subscriber(svc_name, default_filter, otel_layer)` that handles registry construction + `.init()`; services pass their per-service default filter (`ac_service=debug` for AC, `mc_service=info` for MC, etc.) and the helper-produced OTel layer. **Not done in task #24** because (a) the per-service EnvFilter defaults diverge (`ac_service=debug` vs. `mc_service=info`) and a wider design pass is needed to decide whether the default belongs in the helper signature, an env-var fallback chain, or the per-service `Config`; (b) the helper would expand task #24's scope from "OTel SDK + interceptor" to "subscriber stack", which conflicts with the layer-return contract that explicitly leaves Registry composition to callers (`crates/common/src/observability/otel.rs:69-70`); (c) the helper has to land first as it does today before any subscriber consolidation has a stable layer-return shape to depend on. Surfaced 2026-05-23 by browser-client-join task #24 DRY review (R-54, R-56).

- [ ] **The "org exists and is active" lookup is implemented three times across two services, and one of the three carries the exact defect story R-6 just removed from the others** (surfaced 2026-08-14 by dry-reviewer at Gate 3 of `docs/devloop-outputs/2026-08-14-gc-meeting-refusal-causes/`, task #2 of the story-runner-hardening story). Three sites: (a) `crates/ac-service/src/repositories/organizations.rs:31 get_by_subdomain` — `WHERE subdomain = $1 AND is_active = true`, returning `Option<Organization>`, so organization-missing and organization-inactive collapse into one `None`. This is **the identical mechanism** R-6 removed from GC's creation CTE, still live in AC, and it is production-reachable: `crates/ac-service/src/middleware/org_extraction.rs:113` is its only caller (ADR-0020 subdomain extraction). (b) `crates/ac-service/src/repositories/organizations.rs:60 get_by_id` — already solves the distinction correctly (no `is_active` filter; `is_active` carried on the struct so the caller can tell missing from inactive) but is `#[allow(dead_code)]` with zero callers. So AC contains both the bug and its fix, and the fix is the dead one. (c) `crates/gc-service/src/repositories/meetings.rs` — GC now reads the org columns inline in the creation CTE with no Rust struct at all, deliberately (see below). **Extraction opportunity, NOT true duplication** — nothing in a shared home was reimplemented (`crates/common/` has no organization model or lookup of any kind), so per the ADR-0019 DRY Reviewer Exception this is TODO-tracked rather than fix-or-defer. **Explicitly NOT to be "fixed" by having GC call AC's repository**: separate crates, `AcError` vs `GcError`, and GC's lookup is fused into an atomic CTE whose single-statement property is the load-bearing security claim (`meetings.rs` module header) — extracting it to a shared function would either break that atomicity or export a meetings-specific CTE as if it were a generic org lookup. **Fix when acted on**: the AC-side half stands alone and is the valuable half — give `get_by_subdomain` a three-way outcome the way GC's `MeetingRefusal` now does, and either wire up `get_by_id` or delete it. Whether an `Organization` model belongs in `crates/common/` is a separate and much weaker question: two consumers, one of which (GC) deliberately wants no struct. **Defer trigger**: any devloop touching AC's org-extraction middleware or adding a second GC consumer of org state. Owner: auth-controller (+ dry-reviewer to re-check whether a shared model is warranted at that point).

- [x] **RESOLVED 2026-08-21** (see resolution note at the end of this entry) — **`McRejectionReason` was mapped to strings by two parallel `match` statements, one exhaustive and one not** (surfaced 2026-08-14 by dry-reviewer at Gate 1 of `docs/devloop-outputs/2026-08-14-gc-meeting-refusal-causes/`). `crates/gc-service/src/services/mc_assignment.rs:288-299` matches the same enum twice in the same function: once to `(status, rejection_reason)` metric labels (exhaustive, five arms) and once to a client-facing `reason_str` (four arms ending in `_ =>`). Adding a variant therefore updates the metric label but silently inherits the generic `"No meeting controllers available"` message — the label and the message disagree, and only the label half reds at compile time. This is the "parallel string tables" shape that GC's new `MeetingRefusal` deliberately avoids: story R-6 established the in-crate convention of one `&'static str` method on the cause enum (`MeetingRefusal::metric_label()`), mirroring `GcError::error_type_label()`, with the two-taxonomy junction pinned by a test (`refusal_maps_to_its_error_variant` in `errors.rs`). `McRejectionReason` predates that convention and is now the only GC cause-enum not following it. **Not a same-owner sibling of R-6's mechanism** — R-6's defect is a zero-row query result encoding several unrelated preconditions; this is the *fix pattern* applied inconsistently elsewhere — which is why it was TODO-tracked at Gate 1 rather than folded into task #2. **Fix when acted on**: give `McRejectionReason` a `metric_label()` and a `client_message()`, both exhaustive, and delete the `_ =>` arm so a new variant is a compile error at both sites. ~12 LoC plus join-path test surface; `services/mc_assignment.rs` was outside task #2's changeset. **Defer trigger**: the next devloop adding an `McRejectionReason` variant or touching MC assignment rejection handling. Owner: global-controller. — **RESOLUTION (2026-08-21, `docs/devloop-outputs/2026-08-21-gc-invalid-request-rejection-reason/`, verified by dry-reviewer at Gate 3):** the defer trigger fired exactly as predicted (that devloop added the `InvalidRequest` variant and put `services/mc_assignment.rs` — previously named here as out-of-changeset — squarely in the changeset), and the **load-bearing half was fixed rather than re-deferred**. The `_ =>` catch-all on the `reason_str` match is **deleted**; both matches are now exhaustive over `Option<McRejectionReason>`, so a future variant is a compile error at *both* sites, which is the forcing function this entry existed to demand. `Unspecified` also gained its own correct message ("A meeting controller rejected the assignment for an unrecognized reason") instead of silently inheriting the factually-wrong "No meeting controllers available". **Consciously NOT done, and not to be re-raised blindly:** the `metric_label()` / `client_message()` methods-on-the-enum convention alignment. Rationale (dry-reviewer, Gate 1 of that devloop, re-affirmed at Gate 3 against the code): the metric match is **not** a pure label map — it carries a second axis (`status`, e.g. `InvalidRequest => ("error", ...)` deliberately grouping with `no_mcs_available` rather than `("rejected", ...)`) plus a `None` arm with no corresponding enum variant, so a `metric_label()` accessor would absorb only half of it and would *hide* a per-variant policy decision that is currently explicit at the match. That makes `McRejectionReason` a genuinely different shape from `MeetingRefusal`, where discriminant→label is the whole mapping. With both matches now compile-forced, the residual is a naming-convention preference with no live failure mode behind it — reopen only if a **third** consumer of the variant set appears in code, which is the point at which a shared accessor starts paying for itself.

- [ ] **The `gc_mc_assignments_total{rejection_reason}` value set is hand-enumerated at six sites with no drift guard — the anchors say "keep in sync", nothing enforces it** (surfaced 2026-08-21 by dry-reviewer at Gate 3 of `docs/devloop-outputs/2026-08-21-gc-invalid-request-rejection-reason/`). **Source of truth**: the terminal `match last_rejection_reason` in `crates/gc-service/src/services/mc_assignment.rs` (the `(status, rejection_reason)` tuple match), which now carries an `ANCHOR (DRY):` naming all five mirrors. **Mirrors**: (1) `docs/observability/metrics/gc-service.md` label list under `gc_mc_assignments_total`, (2) the same file's Cardinality-Management table row (a *count* derived from that list — a second-order mirror, and the one @operations correctly identified as "the tell" for a partial edit), (3) `docs/observability/alerts.md` `GCMCAssignmentFailures` Response step 2, (4) `docs/runbooks/gc-incident-response.md` "Common Rejection Reasons" legend, (5) `crates/gc-service/tests/mc_assignment_metrics_integration.rs:ALL_REJECTION_REASONS`, (6) `crates/gc-service/src/observability/metrics.rs` metric-cluster test array. **Extraction opportunity, NOT true duplication** — nothing in a shared home was reimplemented; per the ADR-0019 DRY Reviewer Exception this is TODO-tracked rather than fix-or-defer, and the guard lands in `scripts/guards/simple/` (shared gate tooling owned elsewhere), which is the same cross-owner routing that sent that devloop's S-1/S-2 to spin-out. **This is a demonstrated-drift case, not a hypothetical**: before that devloop the catalog was wrong in *both* directions simultaneously — missing `unspecified` and `no_mcs_available` (emitted for months, undocumented) while listing `rpc_failed` (declared, never emitted), and the runbook legend had drifted differently again. All six sites were hand-corrected in one changeset; nothing prevents the seventh edit from re-splitting them. **Fix when acted on**: a `validate-rejection-reason-label-sync.sh` (+ self-test) in the mould of the two existing precedents — `scripts/guards/simple/validate-subdomain-regex-sync.sh` and `validate-slug-class-sync.sh` — whose `SITES` array pattern generalises here at near-zero cost; extract the emitted value set from the SoT match and assert each mirror enumerates it. **Two traps for the implementer, both discovered at Gate 3:** (a) the mirrors are legitimately *not* set-identical, so a naive set-equality guard will red on correct code — the catalog and runbook legend include `none` (success path) and tag `rpc_failed` as `(declared/unemitted)`, `ALL_REJECTION_REASONS` deliberately excludes `none` (so its label-swap loops don't assert the success value absent), and `alerts.md` lists only the six non-success values; the guard must compare against a declared per-site expected shape, not a single canonical set. (b) `metrics.rs`'s array is a **spelling** mirror with an intentionally partial set (it loops over the three values that test itself records at `status="rejected"`, and widening it would break the test's `assert_delta(1)`), so it must be checked for value *spelling* only, never for completeness. **Defer trigger**: the next devloop adding an `McRejectionReason` variant, or any edit that touches the catalog list, the cardinality count, or the runbook legend. Owner: observability (catalog + label taxonomy per ADR-0011) with infrastructure for the guard harness; dry-reviewer to re-check mirror enumeration at that point.

- [ ] **"What makes an `AssignMeetingWithMhRequest` malformed" is encoded on both sides of the RPC, and divergence silently restores the exact bug the 2026-08-21 devloop removed** (surfaced 2026-08-21 by dry-reviewer at Gate 3 of `docs/devloop-outputs/2026-08-21-gc-invalid-request-rejection-reason/`, cross-flagged by @code-reviewer and @team-lead). **Producer side**: `crates/gc-service/src/services/mc_assignment.rs:selection_is_malformed(&[MhAssignmentInfo])` — `handlers.is_empty() || handlers.iter().any(|h| h.grpc_endpoint.is_empty())`. **Validator side**: `crates/mc-service/src/grpc/mc_service.rs:store_mh_assignments` — the same two conditions over proto `MhAssignment`, each returning `McError::InvalidArgument` → `REJECTION_REASON_INVALID_REQUEST`. Semantically identical today; both now carry reciprocal `ANCHOR (DRY):` comments. **Extraction opportunity, NOT true duplication** — nothing in a shared home was reimplemented (`crates/common/` encodes no part of this contract), so per the ADR-0019 DRY Reviewer Exception this is TODO-tracked rather than fix-or-defer. **Why this coupling deserves more than the usual "keep in sync" comment — the failure mode is not fail-safe, it is fail-silent into the original defect.** GC does not trust MC's `INVALID_REQUEST` claim; it adjudicates it against its own request (deliberate, and correct — it stops a buggy or rolled-back MC from vetoing assignment for the whole pool). So if MC ever gains a **third** malformed condition (empty `mh_id`, empty `webtransport_endpoint`, a length bound…) without GC's predicate learning it, MC rejects, GC's predicate says "well-formed", GC takes the *misreporting* branch: relabels to `Unhealthy`, pushes to `tried_mcs`, and walks the entire pool — which every MC rejects identically. That is precisely the burn-the-retry-budget-and-record-a-sick-fleet behaviour this devloop existed to remove, restored silently, with the fail-fast fix inert and **no test failing**. The reverse drift (GC stricter than MC) costs availability instead: GC fails fast on requests MC would have accepted. **Why not extracted now** (dry-reviewer, agreeing with the implementer): the two predicates operate on different types at different layers — GC validates its own `MhAssignmentInfo` *before* building the proto, MC validates decoded proto `MhAssignment` *after* the wire — so a genuinely shared predicate has to live over the wire type, and **`crates/common/` does not depend on `crates/proto-gen` today** (verified: no such dep in `crates/common/Cargo.toml`). Adding it is an architectural change affecting multiple consumers and brushes the S-1 GSA-glob issue, which is exactly the protocol's "needs cross-service coordination (common crate change affecting multiple consumers)" deferral pattern — not a PR-sized edit. **Fix when acted on**, cheapest first: (a) a shared-case-table test — one list of malformed fixtures asserted against both sides, so a third condition on either side reds; (b) a `dt-guard` check that both anchored sites enumerate the same condition count; (c) full extraction of `fn request_is_malformed(&[MhAssignment]) -> bool` into `common` (or a small `wire-contract` crate) called by GC post-build and MC post-decode — only worth it if a third condition actually arrives. **Also worth considering at that point**: GC already has an endpoint-validity helper used by both registration ingress paths (`crates/gc-service/src/grpc/mh_service.rs:145` and `mc_service.rs:208`, `validate_endpoint`) — a consolidation should decide whether the assignment predicate reuses it rather than growing a third spelling. **Defer trigger**: any change to the malformed-condition set on *either* side, or a third consumer of the contract. Owner: protocol (owns the wire contract) with meeting-controller + global-controller; dry-reviewer to re-check reciprocity at that point.

- [ ] **`dt-guard` now holds two answers to "which Kubernetes kinds carry a pod spec", and they differ by exactly one kind — `DaemonSet`** (surfaced 2026-08-28 by dry-reviewer at Gate 1 of `docs/devloop-outputs/2026-08-28-env-config-guard-per-instance-workloads/`). **Site A**: `crates/dt-guard/src/kustomize_tools.rs::SECURITY_CONTEXT_KINDS` = `{Deployment, StatefulSet}`, consumed by `check_security_context` for the R-18 invariants (`runAsNonRoot`, `allowPrivilegeEscalation`, `capabilities.drop: [ALL]`, `readOnlyRootFilesystem`). Its narrowness is deliberate and documented — it preserves parity with the bash guard ADR-0034 replaced. **Site B**: `crates/dt-guard/src/env_config.rs::WORKLOAD_KINDS_WITH_POD_SPEC` = `{Deployment, StatefulSet, DaemonSet}`, consumed by kind-based manifest discovery. Both sites now carry reciprocal `ANCHOR (DRY):` comments naming the other and stating why they differ. **Extraction opportunity, NOT true duplication** — nothing in a shared home was reimplemented, and the two lists answer the same question for genuinely different checks under different constraints, so per the ADR-0019 DRY Reviewer Exception this is TODO-tracked rather than fix-or-defer. **The live consequence, which is why this is tracked rather than left to the anchors**: a `DaemonSet` landing in any `infra/services/<svc>/` would have its env wiring checked by `env-config` while R-18 **silently skips** its security-context invariants and reports green — a guard alive and never applied, to a manifest the sibling guard *in the same binary* is checking. `env-config`'s `unclassified_manifest_kind` hard FAIL does **not** catch this: it fires only for kinds *neither* list knows, and `DaemonSet` is classified. No service directory contains a DaemonSet today, so there is no live defect — this is a latent asymmetry, and a one-kind gap is less visible than the three-kind gap it started as (`Job`/`CronJob` were dropped from Site B at Gate 1 on a YAGNI challenge, which sharpened this rather than easing it). **Explicitly NOT to be "fixed" by folding the two lists into one SSoT, and NOT by quietly widening `SECURITY_CONTEXT_KINDS`** — R-18's coverage is a behaviour question owned by whoever owns the bash-parity constraint, and widening it inside an unrelated devloop is an unreviewed behaviour change with no scoped reviewer (the flattering direction of the same move as silently narrowing it). **Fix when acted on**: decide whether the bash-parity constraint still binds; if it does not, widen `SECURITY_CONTEXT_KINDS` to match Site B deliberately, with R-18 test coverage for the added kinds, and collapse to one list only if the two checks genuinely want identical membership. **Defer trigger**: the first `DaemonSet`/`Job`/`CronJob` to land in a service directory, or any devloop retiring the R-18 bash-parity constraint. Owner: infrastructure (dt-guard maintainer), with whoever owns the R-18 parity decision; dry-reviewer to re-check the anchors still describe reality at that point.

- [ ] **`dt-guard` reads YAML three different ways, and the hand-rolled one is deliberate — do not "fix" it naively** (surfaced 2026-08-28 by dry-reviewer at Gate 1/Gate 3 of `docs/devloop-outputs/2026-08-28-env-config-guard-per-instance-workloads/`). (a) **Structural `serde_norway`** — five subcommands: `application_metrics.rs`, `infrastructure_metrics.rs`, `grafana_datasources.rs`, `alert_rules.rs` (typed struct + derive) and, as of 2026-08-28, `env_config.rs`. (b) **Hand-rolled line/substring walk** — `crates/dt-guard/src/kustomize_tools.rs::split_yaml_docs()` + `field_one_line()` plus raw `doc.contains("runAsNonRoot: true")`-style membership tests in `check_security_context` / `check_empty_secret_data`. (c) **A third line walk** — `gsa_sync.rs::check_yaml_mirror()`, over `cross-boundary-ownership.yaml`. **Extraction opportunity, NOT true duplication** — per the ADR-0019 DRY Reviewer Exception this is TODO-tracked, not fix-or-defer. **The caveat is the load-bearing part of this entry.** (b) is deliberate and self-documented: it preserves bash's substring-match semantics *including its false positives* ("false-positive on string-literal-mention-of-the-token is bash's behavior too") for ADR-0034 port fidelity. It is therefore **explicitly NOT to be fixed by having `env_config.rs` call `field_one_line()`** — that would re-import into a structurally-parsed guard the exact fragility the 2026-08-28 rewrite removed, and it is the mistake a reader who sees only "three YAML parsers, collapse them" would make. The 2026-08-28 devloop deleted two `Lazy<Regex>` statics and a 3-lines-after-`configMapKeyRef` window walk from (a)'s newest member for precisely that reason; moving in the other direction would undo it. **Fix when acted on**: (b) becomes a genuine candidate only once the bash-parity constraint is retired — at which point porting `check_security_context` onto `serde_norway` eliminates R-18's known substring false-positive for free, which is the actual prize. (c) is a small, self-contained mirror check and is the weakest of the three; fold it in only if it is already being touched. **Defer trigger**: any devloop retiring the R-18 bash-parity constraint (same trigger as the kind-list entry immediately above — the two are likely one piece of work), or a sixth `serde_norway` consumer that wants a shared document-loading helper. Owner: infrastructure (dt-guard maintainer).

- [ ] **`docs/DEVELOPMENT.md`'s local-run blocks are an unguarded, unanchored mirror of the `MissingEnvVar` set, and 19 of 23 required vars are absent from them today** (surfaced 2026-08-28 by dry-reviewer at Gate 3 of `docs/devloop-outputs/2026-08-28-env-config-guard-per-instance-workloads/`, quantifying @operations' OPS-3). **Source of truth**: `Config::from_vars()` in each `crates/<svc>/src/config.rs` — the `ConfigError::MissingEnvVar("VAR")` set is what the service actually refuses to start without. **Mirror sites**: (1) `infra/services/<svc>/*configmap*.yaml` `data:` keys, (2) `infra/services/<svc>/*deployment.yaml`/`statefulset.yaml` `env[].name` + `configMapKeyRef`, (3) `infra/kubernetes/overlays/kind/services/*/configmap-otel-patch.yaml`, (4) `docs/DEVELOPMENT.md`'s per-service local-run `export` blocks + the `.env` template. As of the 2026-08-28 devloop, sites 1 and 2 are cross-checked against the SoT and each other by `dt-guard env-config`; site 3 is a declared, tracked coverage gap (see §Infrastructure Validation in Devloops); **site 4 is checked by nothing and carries no `ANCHOR (DRY):` comment naming its source of truth**, which is the convention every other cross-encoding mirror in this repo follows. **Measured drift at 2026-08-28** (per-service, required vars absent from that service's block): **AC 0/2** — clean; **GC 2/3** — `GC_CLIENT_ID`, `GC_CLIENT_SECRET`; **MC 9/10** — `AC_ENDPOINT`, `AC_JWKS_URL`, `MC_BINDING_TOKEN_SECRET`, `MC_CLIENT_ID`, `MC_CLIENT_SECRET`, `MC_GRPC_ADVERTISE_ADDRESS`, `MC_TLS_CERT_PATH`, `MC_TLS_KEY_PATH`, `MC_WEBTRANSPORT_ADVERTISE_ADDRESS`; **MH 8/8** — every required var it has. The MC and MH blocks additionally export `AC_CLIENT_ID`/`AC_CLIENT_SECRET`, which those services do not read (they read `MC_`/`MH_`-prefixed names), so the doc names variables that have no effect while omitting the ones that are mandatory. **Following the MC or MH block verbatim yields a service that exits at startup on its first `MissingEnvVar`** — the doc is not merely incomplete, it is non-functional as written, and it is the on-ramp a new contributor uses first. **Extraction opportunity, NOT true duplication** — nothing in a shared home was reimplemented; per the ADR-0019 DRY Reviewer Exception this is TODO-tracked rather than fix-or-defer. **Why a guard rather than a one-time correction** (@operations' OPS-3 disposition, which I agree with): hand-correcting the four blocks fixes today's snapshot and leaves the drift mechanism intact — the blocks have already drifted twice (the 2026-08-28 devloop itself corrected `BIND_ADDRESS` → `MC_`/`MH_WEBTRANSPORT_BIND_ADDRESS` and `MAX_STREAMS` → `MH_MAX_STREAMS`, both of which had been naming variables the services ignore). `extract_required_env_vars()` in `crates/dt-guard/src/env_config.rs` already computes the authoritative set and has exactly one consumer, so the marginal cost of a second consumer asserting "every `MissingEnvVar` for service X appears in X's `docs/DEVELOPMENT.md` block" is small. **Fix when acted on**, cheapest first: (a) add an `ANCHOR (DRY):` comment to each block naming `crates/<svc>/src/config.rs::Config::from_vars()` as the SoT — costs nothing and makes the coupling visible to the next editor; (b) extend `dt-guard env-config` (or a sibling policy) with a doc-block check reusing `extract_required_env_vars()`; (c) correct the 19 absent vars, which (b) will enumerate for free rather than by hand. **Trap for the implementer of (b)**: the blocks legitimately contain non-required vars (`RUST_LOG`, `OTEL_ENABLED`, `OTLP_ENDPOINT`), so the assertion is one-directional — every required var must appear, but not every listed var must be required. A set-equality check will red on correct docs. **Defer trigger**: any devloop editing `docs/DEVELOPMENT.md`'s env blocks, or adding a `MissingEnvVar` to any service's `config.rs`. Owner: operations (doc) + infrastructure (guard). Provenance: OPS-3 was dispositioned "deferred, operations records the follow-up" in that devloop's review table; no `docs/TODO.md` entry existed at Gate 3, so dry-reviewer filed this one per the review protocol's tracking-at-verdict-time requirement.

- [ ] **The per-connection transport contract now has three declarations in two languages, and the Rust↔TypeScript edge has no compiler and no guard between them (filed 2026-09-01 by @dry-reviewer at Gate 2 of `docs/devloop-outputs/2026-09-01-mh-transport-seam/`; owner: client + media-handler, dry-reviewer to re-check)**. **The three sites**: `packages/sdk-core/src/transport/IWebTransport.ts` (canonical TS contract), `packages/test-utils/src/contracts/IWebTransport.ts` (the Pattern A structural copy tracked as #58 above), and as of this devloop `crates/mh-service/src/transport/mod.rs` (`MediaTransport` + `MediaSendStream`). **The asymmetry is the point**: the TS↔TS edge *is* drift-locked — structural typing exercised by an explicit `const transport: IWebTransport = mock;` assignment in `packages/sdk-core/src/transport/__tests__/BrowserWebTransport.test.ts`, which fails to compile if the interface grows a member the mock lacks. The Rust↔TS edge has neither a compiler nor a guard nor an `ANCHOR (DRY):` marker. **Do NOT "fix" this with a cross-language parity test.** The mirror is deliberately *conceptual, not member-level*, and ADR-0036 §10 asked only for a seam "mirroring the client's transport interface": `IWebTransport` is `ready` / `closed` / `datagrams {readable, writable}` / `createBidirectionalStream()` / `close()` — Web Streams over *bidirectional* streams; `MediaTransport` is `send_datagram` / `recv_datagram` / `open_uni` / `write_all` / `finish`, with no lifecycle members at all. A member-parity assertion would be asserting a parity that was never claimed, which is the failure this entry exists to prevent rather than cause. **What actually drifts is the shared behavioural assumption, and two divergences already exist in-tree** — both found during this devloop, neither visible from either declaration: (i) **zero-copy is asymmetric and neither side says so.** `packages/test-utils/src/MockWebTransport.ts:234` copies on capture (`out.set(chunk)`), so the TS double cannot assert a zero-copy property; on the Rust side production copies *below* the seam (`wtransport-proto-0.7.2/src/datagram.rs:50-66` prepends the H3 session-id varint into a fresh buffer), so the Rust seam's own docs carry a "fidelity boundary" block saying the shim is deliberately more zero-copy than production. Neither side may assume the other's guarantee, and today nothing states that at either declaration. (ii) **adverse-condition capability diverges** — the Rust shim has loss, send-capacity and delay knobs; the TS mock has injection and inspection only. That half is tracked under §Test Debt ("`MockWebTransport` has injection and inspection but NO adverse-condition knob"), which cross-references this entry; **the two are limbs of one fact — the Rust and TS halves of one mechanism diverging with no compiler between them — so closing one without the other leaves the mechanism half-built.** **Extraction opportunity, NOT true duplication** — nothing in `crates/common` or a shared home was reimplemented (there is no shared home: `crates/common/src/webtransport/**` is a GSA glob that matches nothing), and cross-language mirroring is designed-in per the ADR-0036 §2 codec precedent, so per the ADR-0019 DRY Reviewer Exception this is TODO-tracked rather than fix-or-defer. **Fix when acted on**: not a parity test — a short named-invariant list stated at all three declarations (what each side may and may not assume of the other about copying, back-pressure signalling and drop policy), plus reciprocal `ANCHOR (DRY):` comments so the coupling is greppable, following the pattern already used for the four `crates/media-protocol/` constants. **Defer trigger**: ADR-0036 Assumption 1's "handler server half and browser client half in one harness through the transport seam" — that harness is the moment the divergence bites, because it is the first code to hold both doubles at once — or either side gaining a behavioural guarantee the other lacks. **Related boundary, deliberately filed elsewhere and not duplicated here**: quinn's silent drop-oldest beneath MH's egress counter is tracked under §Media Path Obligations, including @security's S5 constraint that the counter may never be described as observing transport back-pressure.

### From ADR-0010 Phase 4a Review (2026-01-31)
