- [ ] **gRPC input validation duplication**: MC and MH services duplicate validation logic (~100 lines). Locations: `crates/gc-service/src/grpc/mc_service.rs`, `crates/gc-service/src/grpc/mh_service.rs`
- [ ] **Heartbeat interval constants**: Defined in 3 places with different names. Locations: `mc_service.rs`, `mh_service.rs`, `meeting-controller/gc_client.rs`

## Port Constant Scattering (Kind / K8s / Env-Tests)

- [ ] **MC/MH port constants duplicated across 6+ files with no single source of truth**: Host ports (4433, 4434, 4435, 4436), NodePorts (30433, 30434, 30435, 30436), and observability ports (9090/30090, 3000/30030, 3100/30080) are hardcoded independently in `infra/kind/kind-config.yaml`, `infra/services/{mc,mh}-service/service.yaml` (NodePort values), `infra/services/{mc,mh}-service/{mc,mh}-{0,1}-configmap.yaml` (advertise addresses), `infra/services/{mc,mh}-service/configmap.yaml` (bind addresses), `infra/docker/{mc,mh}-service/Dockerfile` (EXPOSE + bind defaults), `infra/kind/scripts/setup.sh` (print output), and `crates/env-tests/src/cluster.rs` (ClusterPorts::default). Changing a port in one file without updating all others causes silent breakage. Consider extracting port assignments to a shared config (e.g., `ports.env` sourced by setup.sh, referenced by Kustomize configMapGenerator). The new `kind-config.yaml.tmpl` (ADR-0030) correctly uses envsubst placeholders for its hostPorts, avoiding this issue for devloop clusters. **Update (2026-09-02, story task #9 / devloop `2026-09-02-mh-transport-parameter-manifests`, @dry-reviewer's condition on an accepted deferral)**: the closing "low priority — manual workflow only" in the original text is now FALSE and has been struck. Three of the scattered MH values are bind addresses a running service reads, and `crates/mh-service/src/config.rs:22,25,28` holds byte-identical duplicate strings (`DEFAULT_GRPC_BIND_ADDRESS`, `DEFAULT_HEALTH_BIND_ADDRESS`, `DEFAULT_WEBTRANSPORT_BIND_ADDRESS`) of all three ConfigMap values. **Fresh MH inventory**: `4434` at 9 sites, `8083` at 10, `50053` at 9 — ~28 across `configmap.yaml`, `mh-0-configmap.yaml`, both deployments (containerPort, both probes, and `MH_GRPC_ADVERTISE_ADDRESS`'s embedded `"grpc://$(POD_IP):50053"`), `service.yaml` and `network-policy.yaml`; `service.yaml:32,56` and `network-policy.yaml:30` also restate ports in **prose comments**, which a derivation would leave stale. **No port guard exists** (`dt-guard` has no `containerPort` logic). **Why kustomize `replacements:` alone cannot close this** (established while landing the `MH_TERMINATION_GRACE_SECONDS` derivation): (a) `infra/kind/scripts/setup.sh:1211-1217` **rewrites** `mh-{0,1}-config`'s `MH_WEBTRANSPORT_ADVERTISE_ADDRESS` with `kubectl patch` *after* `apply -k`, so no build-time replacement can ever guard it; (b) deriving `containerPort` from a ConfigMap needs `options.delimiter`, which yields a **string**, and `containerPort` is int32 — so the chain must run source=containerPort → target=ConfigMap, the opposite of the intuitive direction; (c) the shared `mh-service-config` has no single instance to source from, unlike the grace-period case whose source and target sit inside one object. A partial derivation covering only the kustomize-reachable subset would manufacture false assurance. **Named follow-up, owner infrastructure**: *Port SSoT for per-instance services* — **step 1 is a compare-only `dt-guard` drift check** across the five MH manifest files plus `config.rs` (cheap, zero deploy blast radius, and the only half that can reach the `setup.sh` patch); step 2 designates each deployment's `containerPort` as canonical and derives ConfigMap bind addresses, Service `port`/`targetPort`, NetworkPolicy ports and both probe ports, chaining mh-0→mh-1 for the shared-ConfigMap source question; then MC, then GC/AC. Revisit the single-file `replacements:` idiom at ~6 entries. **Update (R-37, task #4)**: the AC/GC host-side E2E ports are now also scattered — static host ports 8443 (AC) / 8444 (GC) and NodePort containerPorts 30082 (AC) / 30180 (GC) are hardcoded in `infra/kind/kind-config.yaml` (new AC/GC `extraPortMappings`), `infra/kind/kind-config.yaml.tmpl` (30082/30180 under `${HOST_PORT_*}`), `infra/kubernetes/overlays/kind/services/{ac,gc}-service/nodeport.yaml`, `infra/kind/scripts/setup.sh` (`print_access_info`), and the committed client config (`packages/web-app/vite.config.ts` proxy targets `127.0.0.1:8443/8444`). Same single-source-of-truth opportunity as MC/MH above.

- [ ] **AC and GC encode `DATABASE_URL` in two independently-authored places with nothing asserting they agree (2026-08-14)**: Surfaced during review of devloop `docs/devloop-outputs/2026-08-14-gc-meeting-refusal-causes/main.md` (owner @database; **pre-existing, not introduced by that diff**). GC's connection string is a literal in `infra/services/gc-service/secret.yaml:14` (`postgresql://…postgres.dark-tower.svc.cluster.local:5432/dark_tower`); AC's arrives via `secretKeyRef` → `ac-service-secrets` (`infra/services/ac-service/statefulset.yaml:42-46`), a Secret not tracked in this repo. Both services read the **same** `organizations` table, defined once in `migrations/20250118000001_initial_schema.sql:5` — so this is one logical database with two authored values and no guard on their agreement (CLAUDE.md: "when two places encode the same value, they will drift").

  **Why it matters concretely**: if the two ever diverge, AC mints a validly-signed token naming an org row GC cannot see, and GC returns `500 ORGANIZATION_NOT_PROVISIONED` — the referential-integrity branch added by R-6. That branch is otherwise reachable only by out-of-band mutation, so a config divergence would present as data corruption. The failure is silent until a meeting-create request happens to exercise it.

  **Already operator-visible, which is why this is worth closing rather than just knowing**: `docs/runbooks/gc-deployment.md` now instructs a deploy-time operator to check whether GC and AC point at the same database *before* reaching for a rollback. That turns an unguarded config invariant into a manual triage step performed under time pressure — the runbook is the compensating control, not the fix.

  **Shape of the fix**: derive both from one source (a shared Secret, or a Kustomize `configMapGenerator`/`secretGenerator` consumed by both services) rather than authoring them separately; failing that, a validation-pipeline check asserting the two resolve equal. Same defect class as the port scattering above. Owner: infrastructure + database. Defer trigger: next infra touch to either service's Secret wiring, or any report of `org_not_provisioned` in a non-test environment.

- [ ] **Auto rollout-restart on cert-secret recreate in `setup.sh`**: `scripts/generate-dev-certs.sh` uses 14-day WebTransport leaf validity (Chrome's `serverCertificateHashes` cap), so expiry is a routine dev event. On an already-running cluster, `setup.sh` regenerating an expiring leaf + recreating the `mc/mh-service-tls` Secret does NOT restart the pods serving the old leaf (`kubectl apply` is a no-op on unchanged Deployment specs). **The remaining work is the automation only**: add the rollout-restart to `setup.sh`'s secret-recreate path. Deliberately deferred out of R-36 — it's a behavior change firing on ALL runs including fresh setup, so it carries regression surface and needs its own test cycle. The operator-facing manual sequence is **documented** in `docs/runbooks/client-dev-local.md` §5 F7 and printed by `generate-dev-certs.sh`'s expiry hint; do not restate it here. Owner: operations / infrastructure. Low priority — dev-loop only. (Was mis-owned to "R-49 dev runbook (task #20)"; task #20 produced prose and discharged the documentation half only — a runbook cannot deliver a `setup.sh` behavior change. Re-owned 2026-07-29 by task #20.)

- [ ] **`MH_MAX_CONNECTIONS` is a chosen bound but not a memory-derived one — MH's connection-level QUIC receive flow control is undeclared (2026-09-02, story task #9)**: task #9 set `MH_MAX_CONNECTIONS: "500"` in `infra/services/mh-service/configmap.yaml`, a 20x reduction from the unchosen `DEFAULT_MAX_CONNECTIONS = 10_000` (`crates/mh-service/src/config.rs`, the `DEFAULT_MAX_CONNECTIONS` const) that no manifest supplied. It is sized against expected peak, **not** against the pod's memory limit, and the arithmetic does not close: quinn-proto 0.11.17 leaves `receive_window = VarInt::MAX` (unbounded — the dominant term), `stream_receive_window = 1.25 MB` and `datagram_receive_buffer_size = 1.25 MB`, giving a per-connection adversarial receive ceiling of **~206 MB against a 1 Gi limit** — roughly five hostile connections, not 500. Two amplifiers: ADR-0036's Context records that MH has **no `accept_uni` loop** today, so incoming stream data is never read and the windows fill and stay filled (the worst case is the *ordinary* case, not a tail); and **OOMKill is SIGKILL**, which bypasses `terminationGracePeriodSeconds` entirely, so the drain window task #9 derived is **void under exactly the adversarial condition where it would matter most**. **Lowering the number is not the fix** — at 100 connections it is still ~20 GB of worst-case credit; no usable value closes while `receive_window` is unbounded, so a reduction would be a false mitigation that looks more careful while fixing nothing. **Not fixable at the manifest surface**: a hand-typed receive-window ConfigMap key would be a second encoding of a budget whose real source is `resources.limits.memory` in the same file — the exact R-21 defect task #9 existed to remove — and it would be structurally invisible to `dt-guard env-config`, whose check 1 keys off `ConfigError::MissingEnvVar` and whose check 3 resolves `configMapKeyRef` from the deployment manifests, never from Rust. **Fix**: task #12 declares the receive side as named Rust constants (`receive_window`, `stream_receive_window`, and `datagram_receive_buffer_size` — the last its OWN constant, deliberately NOT derived from `MH_DATAGRAM_BUFFER_AUDIO_FRAMES`, since that quinn field also feeds the advertised `max_datagram_frame_size` and the two directions have opposing sizing pressures). That drops the per-connection ceiling to ~263 KB and makes 500 genuinely memory-derived (~132 MB, ~13% of 1 Gi). Owner: **media-handler (task #12)**; @operations directed this entry and @security set it as a Gate-1 condition; filed by infrastructure so one pen owns it — amend in place rather than opening a second entry. Defer trigger: task #12, or any change to `resources.limits.memory` in `mh-{0,1}-deployment.yaml`. **Observability addendum (@observability, task #9 Gate 3; placed by @operations to avoid a second entry):** once #12 lands, `500 x ~263 KB ~= 132 MB` against 1 Gi is a **statically-computed premise, not a derived one**. Nothing connects it to `resources.limits.memory`, so lowering that limit silently invalidates the arithmetic with no signal — the failure is a return to OOMKill-as-only-indicator, and it would arrive through an unrelated resource-tuning change that no reviewer would connect to connection admission. Drift-free shape: downward-API `resourceFieldRef: {resource: limits.memory}` feeding a #12 startup validation, so the premise is checked against the actual limit at boot rather than assumed. Task-sized (a sixth required var); flagged rather than folded in. **@operations condition on that shape**: it converts a silent OOMKill into a startup refusal, which is the right trade (loud and at deploy time, not at 3am under load) *only if* the refusal names both numbers and the remedy — the computed requirement, the observed limit, and which of the two to change. A bare `ConfigError` here reproduces the sentinel's failure mode: correct, fail-closed, and undiagnosable at the moment of use.

## Env-Test Resilience & Runbook Validation

- [ ] **`test_all_services_have_logs_in_loki` (`30_observability.rs:116`) hard-asserts Loki readiness with NO retry** (surfaced 2026-06-26 by task #56's first live Layer-7 run). The test does `assert!(cluster.is_loki_available().await, …)` (a single HTTP `GET /ready`) at test start; if Loki isn't warm yet — which it often isn't in the seconds right after a cluster bring-up — the test fails cold even though Loki comes up moments later (verified: a re-run with Loki warm passes). The crate already HAS `src/eventual.rs::assert_eventually` + `ConsistencyCategory::LogAggregation` (45s budget) — the body of this test uses it, but the upfront availability gate doesn't. Fix (either, @observability to decide at pickup): **(a) RETRY** — wrap the `is_loki_available()` gate in `assert_eventually(ConsistencyCategory::LogAggregation, …)` so it tolerates warmup (makes a cold-but-coming Loki GREEN); **(b) CONDITIONAL-SKIP** — skip `test_all_services_have_logs_in_loki` when `is_loki_available()` is false/None, honoring the crate's declared optional-Loki auto-skip intent (makes a genuinely-ABSENT Loki a clean SKIP, not a FAIL). (a) and (b) are complementary: (a) fixes the cold-start flake, (b) covers genuine absence; both together make absent-Loki non-failing. This is the ONLY thing that makes absent-Loki not a loud Phase-2 FAIL — neither task #56's Phase-1e soft-poll nor any classifier can (a classifier would reopen reverse-masking; ruling #3). Owner: env-tests / observability (@observability co-owns per task #56 Gate-1). Out of task #56's scope (that task fills the pipeline wiring, not the env-tests crate); task #56's Layer 7 correctly surfaced this as a loud `STATUS=FAIL`, no masking. Defer trigger: the next env-tests / observability touch, or a recurring cold-start flake in Layer 7. (Compensating control: task #56 may add an observability-endpoint readiness poll to layer7.sh Phase-1e — see that devloop's §Accepted Deferrals — which would convert this from a Phase-2 test flake into a Phase-1 wait.)
- [x] **Counter-delta baseline capture not robust to stale-series inflation across a pod rollout** (surfaced 2026-08-05, task #19 Gate-2 L7 attempt-2: `26_mh_quic.rs::test_mc_media_connection_update_increments_participant_mh_status_metric` flaked with "did not increase above baseline 5 within 60s (last observed: 1)" — full diagnosis in `docs/devloop-outputs/2026-08-04-e2e-negative-specs-pipeline/main.md` §Issues Encountered). Mechanism: the `sum(...)` instant-vector baseline is captured while old-pod series are still inside Prometheus's staleness window (Layer-7 Phase-1c `rebuild-all` still draining), those series expire mid-assertion, and the fresh pods' counters can never exceed the inflated baseline → false-negative 60s timeout (loud, never a false pass). Affects BOTH counter-delta helper families: the Rust ones in `crates/env-tests/tests/26_mh_quic.rs` (`assert_participant_mh_status_increases_past`, `mh_notification` twins) and the deliberate TS mirrors in `packages/web-app/e2e/mcMetrics.ts` (`pollUntilSumAbove` consumers; header there carries the matching caveat). The concrete browser-suite exposure is the Layer-7 RETRY path (@test, task #19 review): attempt N's spec increments the counter on the then-current MC pod; attempt N+1's Phase-1c rebuild rolls the pods; the retry's baseline read can include the old pod's soon-to-expire series — a guaranteed 60s timeout on exactly the attempt that burns the final budget. Fix shapes to evaluate at pickup: settle-wait until the target set matches current pods, or a per-current-pod query (`sum by` over live instances), applied consistently to both languages. Owner: env-tests / observability (+ test for the mcMetrics.ts mirror). Defer trigger: next env-tests crate touch or a recurring L7 counter-delta flake. **Occurrence 2 — 2026-08-28, defer trigger MET** (`docs/devloop-outputs/2026-08-28-env-config-guard-per-instance-workloads/`, Gate-2 `layer-all.sh` attempt 1): same test, same helper, signature `"did not increase above baseline 7 within 60s (last observed: 1)"` — identical to occurrence 1 but for the baseline integer (5→7). Did NOT reproduce on attempt 2 (`26_mh_quic` 7 passed/0 failed in 71.53s), so it was ruled operator-lane, not diff-caused; the changeset contained no service runtime code and no mc-service file at all, yet mc-0/mc-1 had rolled. This is the entry's stated forcing condition — a recurring L7 counter-delta flake — now met, 23 days after occurrence 1, on the same test. **Two occurrences is also the evidence that the 'loud, never a false pass' property is holding**: both instances cost a pipeline attempt and neither could have greened over a real defect. **RESOLVED 2026-08-29** (`docs/devloop-outputs/2026-08-29-l7-counter-delta-per-instance/`): both counter-delta helper families now capture the baseline as a PER-INSTANCE map via `sum by (instance)(...)` (grouping label `instance` = pod IP:port, confirmed by @observability against the scrape config) and pass when ANY currently-present instance beats its OWN baseline (a fresh post-rollover pod, absent from the baseline, passes once its value > 0). A stale old-pod series that expires mid-assertion is simply absent from the current map and can no longer inflate anything, because the comparison never sums across pods — the retry-path exposure is gone with it. The pure decision (`any_instance_exceeds_baseline` in `crates/env-tests/src/fixtures/metrics.rs`; `anyInstanceExceedsBaseline` in `packages/web-app/e2e/instanceCounters.ts`) is the single source of truth per language, each covered by a deterministic no-cluster unit test; the Rust↔TS parity is held by convention + those mirrored tests (NOT structurally — a NaN/Inf parse divergence was caught at Gate 3 and pinned as a parity case). **This closes 2 of the 3 sites of the stale-series defect class** — the two `26_mh_quic.rs` families and their `mcMetrics.ts` mirror. The third, `31_gc_telemetry.rs::pii_dropped_sum`/`assert_drop_counter_increased`, is the same cluster-wide-`sum` shape and is tracked as a separate same-owner follow-up below (it needs a magnitude predicate, not this boolean one — genuinely task-sized). **Qualifying the entry's "loud, never a false pass" property (@security, Gate 3):** that property held for the OLD cluster-wide form, whose churn failure was one-directional (false-negative only). The per-instance form trades it for rollover-robustness and carries a NARROW residual false-pass window: an instance absent from the baseline is compared against zero, so a *pre-existing* pod that was merely missing from the baseline read (unscraped beyond `query.lookback-delta`, or a transient empty result) and then reappears passes on its accumulated value rather than on a test-driven increment. It is documented on both decision functions rather than designed out (no cheap new-pod-vs-returning-pod discriminator without pod start time); the window it opens is narrower than the flake it removes.
- [x] **`26_mh_quic.rs::mc_participant_mh_status_counter` masks Prometheus query failure as a 0.0 reading** (`Err(_) => return 0.0`; same surfacing context as the entry above, flagged 2026-08-05 by task #19/@operations as a fail-loudly/never-mask violation). A Prometheus outage mid-poll reads as "counter is 0" — indistinguishable from a real zero, misdirecting triage toward the service under test. The TS mirror (`packages/web-app/e2e/mcMetrics.ts::promInstantSum`) deliberately THROWS on transport/query failure (global-setup already proved Prometheus healthy, so a failure there is a real problem) — the Rust helper should match that contract; audit sibling helpers in the crate for the same `unwrap_or(0.0)`-on-error shape while there. Owner: env-tests / observability. Defer trigger: next env-tests crate touch or a recurring L7 counter-delta flake. **RESOLVED 2026-08-29** (same devloop as the entry above): the per-instance readers now route through `PrometheusClient::instance_counter_map` → `instance_map_from_query`, which PANICS on a transport/HTTP-status/deserialization error (naming the PromQL + `PrometheusError` so triage lands on Prometheus, not the service under test) and maps ONLY an empty instant vector (a successful query for a not-yet-observed series) to an empty/zero map — the two are kept strictly distinct and the split is unit-tested. Sibling audit: `31_gc_telemetry.rs:324` was ALREADY fail-loud (`pii_dropped_sum` does `.expect(...)` on the query; its `unwrap_or(0.0)` is only the absent-series fallback) — left unchanged; `30_observability.rs`'s `Err(_) => return false` sites are inside `assert_eventually` retry closures (a readiness/warmup contract whose terminal failure panics loudly) — left unchanged, by design.
- [ ] **`31_gc_telemetry.rs::pii_dropped_sum`/`assert_drop_counter_increased` is the third (still cluster-wide-`sum`) site of the stale-series-inflation defect class** (surfaced 2026-08-29 by @test-reviewer + @dry-reviewer at Gate 3 of `docs/devloop-outputs/2026-08-29-l7-counter-delta-per-instance/`, which fixed the other two sites — the `26_mh_quic.rs` families + their `mcMetrics.ts` mirror, see the resolved entry above). `pii_dropped_sum` (`crates/env-tests/tests/31_gc_telemetry.rs:312`) captures a cluster-wide `sum(gc_telemetry_pii_attributes_dropped_total{kind=…})` scalar as `before`, then `assert_drop_counter_increased` (line 331) waits for `pii_dropped_sum >= before + expected`. On the Layer-7 retry path a `rebuild-all` rolls the 2-replica GC Deployment; an old GC pod that recorded drops in attempt N is still inside Prometheus's staleness window when attempt N+1 captures `before`, inflating it; the old series then expires mid-assertion and the fresh pods' sum can never reach `before + expected` (unless the rolled pod's contribution was 0 — which a prior drop-planting run makes false) → the same guaranteed-timeout-on-the-final-attempt exposure as `26_mh_quic.rs`. **Why a follow-up, not folded into the 2026-08-29 fix (Lead ruling, Gate 3):** this uses a MAGNITUDE predicate (`>= before + expected`), not the boolean `> baseline` the per-instance helpers provide, and its current correctness depends on the cluster-wide sum being pod-agnostic ("correct regardless of which pod served the POST" — the `C2′` comment at line 305). Per-instance-izing it needs a real design decision (identify the serving GC pod and assert the delta on that instance, or a different stale-robust shape) plus a `by ≥ expected` predicate variant of `any_instance_exceeds_baseline` — materially more than the boolean conversion. The now-shared `crates/env-tests/src/fixtures/metrics.rs` (`instance_counter_map` + the pure decision helpers) is the extraction point. **BLOCKING QUESTION, must be answered by @global-controller BEFORE any port — do not pick a default** (merged in from @dry-reviewer's entry so it lives with the authoritative record): does the assertion require that ONE pod saw the whole `expected` delta, or that the deltas SUMMED across pods reach it? These are different assertions, and the one-pod reading is strictly WEAKER for `expected > 1` — a 2-pod split of a 2-attribute drop satisfies the summed form and fails the one-pod form, or, if the predicate is written as "any pod moved by >= expected", silently passes on a partial. The `C2′` comment's R7 one-writer-per-`{kind}` argument SUGGESTS one-pod, but that is a claim about GC telemetry routing (does a single POST's attributes always land on one pod's counter?), not a property of the metric, and it is unverified. Record the answer here when obtained, because both fix shapes encode it. **Fix shapes, once the above is answered**: (a) a per-instance magnitude predicate beside the boolean sibling in `crates/env-tests/src/fixtures/metrics.rs` — `any_instance_exceeds_baseline_by(baseline, current, delta)` for the one-pod reading, or `total_increase_across_instances(baseline, current) >= delta` for the summed reading. **The summed variant is the safer default if the routing answer is uncertain**: it is per-instance for the STALE-SERIES purpose (only instances present in `current` contribute, so an expired old-pod series cannot inflate) while still summing the deltas, so it kills the flake WITHOUT weakening the assertion either way. Pin whichever is chosen with a unit test that names the rejected alternative. Or (b) leave the sum and add a pods-settled pre-read, accepting the retry-path exposure. Owner: env-tests / observability (@test co-owns the mcMetrics mirror class; @global-controller owns the routing answer above). Defer trigger: next `31_gc_telemetry.rs` touch, or a recurring L7 GC-telemetry counter-delta flake. (This is the single authoritative entry for the GC site; @dry-reviewer's §Cross-Service Duplication line is a one-line pointer here.)
- [ ] **No guard prevents a scrape-config change from silently reverting the per-instance counter-delta fix** (surfaced 2026-08-29 by @operations at Gate 3 of `docs/devloop-outputs/2026-08-29-l7-counter-delta-per-instance/`). The helpers' rollover-robustness depends entirely on `instance` being pod IP:port, which is Prometheus's *default* — nothing enforces it. Two surfaces: (1) `infra/kubernetes/observability/prometheus-config.yaml` — adding `labelmap __meta_kubernetes_pod_label_(.+)` to the `mc-service`/`mh-service` jobs would override `instance` with the pods' STABLE metadata label (`instance: mc-0`), so a rolled pod keeps its identity, no "new instance above zero" ever appears, and the 2026-08-05/08-28 flake returns with nothing failing. Not hypothetical: the `kubelet` job in that same file already uses `action: labelmap` (~line 135), one job above mc-service, so copy-paste is the realistic path. (2) `infra/docker/prometheus/prometheus.yml` (mounted by the live `docker-compose.yml`) uses `static_configs` with targets `mc-service:9100`/`mh-service:8083`, so `instance` there is a stable service DNS name identical across pods — and it publishes **9090**, the same port `ClusterPorts::from_env()` and `e2eEnv.prometheusUrl` both default to, so pointing the helpers at it degenerates the fix to one bucket, silently. Today the only control is a doc comment in `crates/env-tests/src/fixtures/metrics.rs` + `packages/web-app/e2e/instanceCounters.ts` — i.e. a warning in Rust/TS aimed at someone editing YAML in a different tree, who will never read it. Per ADR-0036 "a control's coverage must be demonstrated, not asserted", the real control is a guard: assert the mc/mh metrics scrape jobs emit no `labelmap` and no `instance` target_label, and fail on drift. Task-sized (new guard, ADR-0034 `dt-guard` subcommand or a `guards/simple/` script) and the config is @infrastructure/@observability co-owned — neither on this devloop's panel, hence deferred rather than fixed in-tree. Owner: operations (validation-pipeline guards), with infrastructure/observability co-sign on the config surface. Defer trigger: next guard-pipeline touch, next edit to either Prometheus config, or any recurrence of the L7 counter-delta flake.
- [ ] **Resilience tests** (`40_resilience.rs`): Pod restart recovery, network partition handling, DB connection loss. Should cover AC (StatefulSet rollout), GC (Deployment rolling), MC (graceful drain with GC coordination). NetworkPolicy canary tests already exist and are real.
- [ ] **Runbook validation tests** (`90_runbook.rs`): Validate documented operational procedures actually work. Coverage needed: AC key rotation (Scenario 2 in `ac-service-incident-response.md`), MC graceful drain before scaling (`mc-deployment.md` §2), MC registration/heartbeat with GC (`mc-deployment.md` §7), GC token refresh from AC, scale-up with load distribution verification. DB backup/restore is CloudNativePG responsibility, not service-level.
- [ ] **No guard covers `devloop-validation.md` STATUS-rank-ladder ↔ `_common.sh::__status_rank` drift** (surfaced 2026-08-05, task #22 Gate-3 by @operations). The runbook §3 manually restates the aggregation-rank numbers (`SKIPPED-NO-VERB`(0)…`UNKNOWN`(8)) that live authoritatively in `scripts/lang/_common.sh::__status_rank` (0-8). This is a single-source-of-truth duplication (CLAUDE.md: "add a guard that fails validation on drift"): the ladder had already silently drifted (pre-task-#56 numbers omitting `SKIPPED-NO-CLUSTER`/`PRECONDITION_FAILURE`), was found by eye during @observability's task #22 review, fixed on L101/L104/L449 — and the fix's first pass MISSED a sibling on L423 (caught only by @operations re-review). Both the drift and the missed sibling are direct consequences of there being no automated check. Fix (task-sized, NOT a trivial in-tree edit — deferred at task #22): a new `dt-guard` subcommand (ADR-0034) that parses the runbook's numeric `rank N` mentions + the §3 ladder line and diffs them against `__status_rank`'s case arms, failing validation on any mismatch (extend to the enum ORDER, not just presence). Owner: operations (validation-pipeline guards). Defer trigger: next `dt-guard`/guard-pipeline touch, or the next time a rank-ladder edit lands in the runbook.
  **RE-DEFER 2026-08-21 (fast-fail-guards devloop):** both defer triggers fired on this diff (a guard-pipeline touch AND a §3 ladder edit — the `PRECONDITION_FAILURE` row now names Layer 3). Still task-sized (a new `dt-guard` subcommand), so re-deferred rather than built; the diff landed the runbook §3 ladder + `_common.sh::__status_rank` consistent by hand as the manual mitigation. **SCOPE WIDENED (N4):** the guard as originally scoped (runbook §3 ladder ↔ `_common.sh::__status_rank`) would NOT catch the drift surface this devloop introduced — `scripts/guards/run-guards.sh`'s exit block now carries a THIRD hand-copy of the `PRECONDITION_FAILURE > FAIL > OK` precedence (it sources `guards/common.sh`, not `lang/_common.sh`, so it cannot call `status_to_exit_code`; the copy is pinned only by `run-guards.test.sh` deriving expected exit from `status_to_exit_code` at test time). When built, the guard's scope must be the **three-way** set: runbook §3 ladder + `_common.sh::__status_rank`/`status_to_exit_code` + the `run-guards.sh` exit-block ANCHOR (DRY) mirror.
- [x] **`validate-subdomain-regex-sync.sh`'s sweep scans generated files, not tracked source** (surfaced 2026-08-21 by dry-reviewer at Gate 3 of the fast-fail-guards devloop). **RESOLVED 2026-09-01** (`2026-09-01-subdomain-guard-tracked-only`): the step-(3) sweep is now `git -C "$REPO_ROOT" grep -I -n -F -e "$CANONICAL" -- :(exclude)…` (guarded by a REPO_ROOT-is-git-top-level precondition; no `:/` anchor — @security S1/S2) — git-tracked files only, so gitignored build output (`coverage/**`, `.nx/cache/**`) is excluded BY CONSTRUCTION, closing the false positive AND the exact-equality false negative (an untracked artifact can no longer offset a deleted tracked pin) in one move. SINGLE-PATH, per the constraint below: there is no scan branch — the guard runs the same `git grep` against the real root and the seam, and the self-test makes the seam viable by `git init`+`git add -A`ing its synthetic fixture (shape (a)) rather than the guard branching for a non-git tree. The audit-trail carve-out (`docs/devloop-outputs/**`, `docs/user-stories/**`) survives as `:(exclude)` pathspecs — those dirs ARE tracked and DO quote the pattern; the guard file self-excludes the same way. Pinned 10 re-verified over tracked source with `coverage/`+`.nx/` artifacts present (green); all 22 self-test assertions green. The stale grep-option-ordering / `--`-terminator header comment was replaced to describe the git-tracked mechanism. Original entry follows. The guard `grep -rn`'s the tree with a `--exclude-dir` denylist (`node_modules/target/dist/…`) and pins an EXACT-EQUALITY total (`total -ne EXPECTED_TOTAL_OCCURRENCES`, 10 = 7 enumerated SITES + 3 unenumerated test pins). A denylist is unsound in **both** directions: (false POSITIVE) a gitignored generated dir it doesn't list — `coverage/` (vitest HTML) and `.nx/` (nx cache) mirror the sdk-core `SUBDOMAIN_REGEX` into copies — inflates the count and reds a LOCAL guard re-run after a TS coverage build (observed this devloop: 10→13); (false NEGATIVE) because the pin is exact-equality, deleting an unenumerated test pin (10→9) while a stray artifact adds +1 (→10) passes SILENTLY, losing a real pin — the drift-masking the guard exists to prevent. Adding `--exclude-dir=coverage --exclude-dir=.nx` (the interim fix considered + REVERTED this devloop) is whack-a-mole on the positive and does nothing for the negative. **Proper fix (task-sized):** rewrite the sweep to scan **tracked files only** — `git ls-files -z | grep -F -z` (or per-file) — which closes the false-positive (generated files are untracked, never scanned), the false-negative (an untracked artifact can never offset a deleted tracked pin), and the build-state nondeterminism, by construction. **Why task-sized, not a trivial edit (deferral burden of proof, verified by dry-reviewer):** the guard resolves `REPO_ROOT="${SUBDOMAIN_GUARD_ROOT:-$__real_root}"` and the self-test drives it against a synthetic tree with **no `git init`**, so `git ls-files` there returns nothing → `total` hits 0 → the guard's own vacuity branch fires → every self-test case reds. The seam must be reworked in the same change. **Do NOT repair it by branching the scan** (`git ls-files` for the real root, `find`/`grep -r` under the seam): that makes the guard's tested path structurally different from its production path — the "a green test proves *a* path ran, not that *the* path ran" defect class recorded elsewhere in this file, and especially poor to build into a DRIFT guard. Single-path shape: keep ONE `git -C "$REPO_ROOT" ls-files -z` sweep and have the self-test `git init` + `git add -A` its fixture (a few lines in the test, no branch in the guard). Also re-verify the pinned 10 against tracked source once artifacts are excluded. Owner: operations (validation-pipeline guards). CI is unaffected today (Layer 3 precedes the coverage-generating Layer 4 on a fresh checkout); the bite is local re-runs + the latent false-negative. Deliberately NOT folded into the fast-fail-guards diff — an unrelated guard edit inside a validation-pipeline change is the shape that should not be convenient (Lead reversed an initial fold-in ruling on exactly this ground).

- [ ] **Env-test probe class: "observed nothing" must be distinguishable from "observed a passing value" — one invariant, three sites, no shared helper** (class named 2026-09-02 by @test-reviewer + @dry-reviewer at task #9 Gate 2; filed as a class rather than a fourth one-off so the next instance is greppable). The invariant: *an assertion whose purpose is to detect a non-empty bad thing must first prove its input set is non-empty; absence of evidence is not evidence of absence.* Three known instances, three different mechanisms, one violated invariant: (1) `26_mh_quic.rs::mc_participant_mh_status_counter` — error-swallow (`Err(_) => 0.0`), a Prometheus outage read as "counter is 0"; **RESOLVED 2026-08-29** (see the resolved entry above). (2) `31_gc_telemetry.rs::pii_dropped_sum`/`assert_drop_counter_increased` — stale-series/cluster-wide-`sum` baseline; **OPEN**, tracked in full above (magnitude-predicate variant needed; @global-controller owes the routing answer). (3) `00_cluster_health.rs:61,108` credential-leak probes — empty-selector (`-n default` matched zero pods, `!"".contains(...)` passed unconditionally); **FIXED in task #9** by namespace correction + an `assert_pods_exist()` panic guard that re-fires on the next namespace/label drift (Lead Ruling 2 — fixed, not deferred, because a bare namespace swap would re-arm the trap). **The generalizable fix is one shared helper, not three rewrites**: an env-test primitive that every probe calls to assert its result set is non-empty (pods matched / series present / rows returned) before asserting anything about contents — the shape `01_mh_deployment_config.rs::fetch_pod` already embodies (panics on empty `items`, on all-terminating, on absent field; never a vacuous green). Scopable as a single follow-up covering the still-open site (2) plus the extraction of the shared primitive; the next instance will be a fourth probe type (not `kubectl` or PromQL), and this named class is what lets a `git grep` for the invariant find the precedent instead of re-deriving it. Owner: **test / env-tests** (@observability co-owns the PromQL sites; @global-controller owns site (2)'s routing answer). Defer trigger: next env-tests crate touch that adds or edits a probe, or the next recurrence of a vacuous-green env-test.


## Env-Test Self-Sufficiency

- [ ] **AC org provisioning endpoint**: Add an admin/internal API to AC for creating organizations. Env-tests should create their own test org via this endpoint instead of depending on pre-seeded data in `infra/docker/postgres/init.sql`.
- [ ] **Remove init.sql seed data**: Once the AC provisioning endpoint exists, remove the `devtest` org/user seed logic from `infra/docker/postgres/init.sql` and update env-tests (20, 21, 23) to self-provision via the API.
- [-] **Env-test portability — remove localhost/port hardcoding**: Partially resolved. `ClusterPorts::from_env()` (ADR-0030) now reads `ENV_TEST_AC_URL`, `ENV_TEST_GC_URL`, `ENV_TEST_PROMETHEUS_URL`, `ENV_TEST_GRAFANA_URL`, `ENV_TEST_LOKI_URL` with localhost defaults. Remaining: (1) remove `mc_webtransport_url` from `ClusterConnection` — MC/MH endpoints should come from GC join responses, (2) tag the invalid-token negative test as local-only or discover MC endpoint dynamically.

## Bootstrap Data Provisioning (SQL seeding → admin APIs)

*One pattern, several instances: bootstrap state the product should own is written by raw SQL from `setup.sh`, so test suites reach around the services to arrange state, exercise paths no real client can take, and duplicate rules (subdomain shape, cap semantics, credential scopes) that a service should enforce once. Sequencing constraint across all of them: calling an admin API needs an admin credential, and credentials are themselves SQL-seeded, so none escapes seeding until the credential API lands first.*

- [ ] **AC org provisioning API**: `setup.sh:705` has carried `TODO: Replace with AC org provisioning API` at the `seed_demo_org` INSERT for some time; `provision_run_org()` (added 2026-08-15 for per-run layer-7 org isolation) is a second instance and is marked as a stopgap in place. Both write `organizations` rows via `kubectl exec … psql`, which additionally requires the dev container to hold a cluster-admin kubeconfig for what should be an API call. Would also give org **deprovisioning** a home — see the Organization Lifecycle entry, which is blocked on a separate FK problem. Note for planners: during the 2026-08-11 story-runner planning a specialist proposed exactly this and the lead scoped it out as unnecessary, on the incorrect reasoning that existing SQL seeding made an API a non-dependency. It is not a dependency of *provisioning working*; it is the difference between a test fixture and a product capability.
- [ ] **AC admin API for service credentials**: Add `POST /api/v1/admin/service-credentials` to AC for registering/updating service credentials (client_id, scopes, service_type). Idempotent upsert. Currently service credentials are seeded via raw SQL in `infra/kind/scripts/setup.sh:seed_test_data()`, which has caused bugs (missing `internal:meeting-token` scope for GC) because credential config is decoupled from the code that requires it.
- [ ] **Per-service credential registration Jobs**: Each service (GC, MC, MH) should own its credential registration via a K8s Job in its Kustomize base. The Job calls the AC admin API to register the service's client_id and required scopes. Deploy pipeline ensures AC is ready before downstream services deploy. This way, adding a new scope (e.g., `internal:meeting-token`) is part of the same PR that adds the endpoint requiring it.
- [ ] **Remove setup.sh SQL seeding**: Once the admin APIs and registration Jobs exist, remove the raw SQL seeding from `infra/kind/scripts/setup.sh` and `infra/docker/postgres/init.sql` — credentials (`seed_test_data`) **and** organizations (`seed_demo_org`, `provision_run_org`, and the `dt_psql` helper they share). Removing the org path also removes layer 7's need for a cluster-admin kubeconfig to provision.

## Organization Lifecycle

- [ ] **Org deprovisioning is unsupported — deleting an organization fails on a FK violation**: `organizations`' children cascade (`ON DELETE CASCADE`), but the cascade reaches `users` and stops: `auth_events.user_id REFERENCES users(user_id)` has no `ON DELETE` clause (`migrations/20250122000001_auth_controller_tables.sql:59`) and `auth_events` carries no `org_id`, so the delete aborts. Two consequences worth knowing before this is picked up: (1) a retention decision is needed first — audit rows probably should outlive the user, so `ON DELETE SET NULL` rather than cascade; (2) GC maps a missing org to **500** (`MeetingRefusal::OrganizationNotProvisioned`) on the reasoning that `org_id` comes from a signed JWT claim, so an org that does not exist is a provisioning bug rather than a lifecycle state — if deprovisioning ships, revisit that mapping, since a valid token for a deleted org becomes normal and 401/403 fits better. Surfaced 2026-08-15.

## Client Architecture

- [ ] **Evaluate HTTP/3 for AC**: AC currently serves HTTP/1.1 (TCP-based). For consistency with GC, evaluate adding HTTP/3 support. Low priority — client is protocol-agnostic via `fetch()`. Follow-up to ADR-0003.

- [ ] **Roster shows a departed participant for 30–60s on tab-close (leave-latency fix #64 incomplete)**: Manual test 2026-08-08 — closing a browser tab leaves the participant's name in other participants' rosters for 30–60s before it clears. Task #64 added `MC_QUIC_MAX_IDLE_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` (default 10s, `crates/mc-service/src/config.rs:54`) as the crash/network-loss fallback, but the observed 30–60s ≠ the configured 10s — which means the config value is almost certainly **not wired into the actual QUIC endpoint** (the underlying wtransport/quinn library default ≈30s is firing instead): a plumbed-but-not-wired gap in the same shape as #63 (config field + validation + parse tests exist, but the last wire to the transport is missing). **Verify**: grep where `quic_max_idle_timeout_seconds` is *consumed* by the endpoint builder. Deeper gap: a tab-close should trigger **prompt** departure via a WebTransport close event (~1s), not any idle timeout — the fix is (1) client sends an explicit leave on `pagehide`/`beforeunload` (reliable — do not depend on the browser's implicit connection-teardown timing), (2) MC handles the transport-close event immediately (broadcast ParticipantLeft), (3) idle timeout as last-resort fallback, actually wired this time. **Test-vs-reality gap**: #60's teardown E2E uses Playwright `context.close()` (likely a cleaner teardown than a user tab-close) and asserts *eventual* departure (60s poll), not promptness — so it passed while the real path is slow; the fix's test must assert **bounded** latency via a mechanism that matches a real tab-close. Owner: meeting-controller + client (+ test). Surfaced 2026-08-08 manual test.

- [ ] **`SignalingClient.#terminate` teardown-robustness — emit before `#teardown`** (slug: `signaling-client-terminate-emit-before-teardown`, **Owner: client**). In `packages/sdk-core/src/signaling/SignalingClient.ts`, `#terminate` calls `this.emit('error', …)` on the join-settled branch BEFORE `this.#teardown()`, so a throwing consumer `error` listener skips teardown and strands the transport/reader (`#terminated` already latched → no recovery). Same shape as the `MeetingSession.disconnect` issue fixed in task #14 (symmetric ~2-line fix: run the settle/emit in `try`, `#teardown()` in `finally`). **Pre-existing** — task #14's diff only renamed `#emit`→`emit`, not the ordering — so scoped out of that devloop. Surfaced 2026-06-29 by browser-client-join task #14 (operations reviewer, Gate 3).

## R-14 Transition

- [ ] **R-14 contract test transition — `packages/sdk-core/tests/bundle-content.test.ts`** (forcing-function obligation, scoped to browser-client-join task #9). When task #9 lands `packages/sdk-core/`, the implementer MUST land a Vitest contract test at the canonical path `packages/sdk-core/tests/bundle-content.test.ts` asserting that the prod `vite build --mode production` artifact does NOT contain the literal string `serverCertificateHashes` (R-14 dev-only WebTransport fingerprint trust path must be tree-shaken out under `__DEV_TRUST_FINGERPRINT__=false`). **Enforcement**: `scripts/guards/simple/ts/no-dev-trust-path-in-prod-bundle.sh` mechanically forces this — when `packages/sdk-core/` exists but the canonical test does not, the guard fails CI at state 2 with a message naming this canonical path. Once both exist, the guard moves to state 3 (Vitest test in Layer 4 carries the real check) and optionally state 4 (stale-dist tripwire). #9's devloop may then choose to delete or repurpose the guard. **No additional Nx wiring required**: `scripts/lang/ts/test.sh:10` already runs `nx affected -t test:unit test:component`; precedent `packages/test-utils/project.json`. **If #9's implementer prefers a different test filename**, update `no-dev-trust-path-in-prod-bundle.sh`'s expected path in the same devloop (one-line edit) — the canonical-path hardcoding is the explicit cost of the forcing function. Surfaced 2026-05-12 by devloop-outputs/2026-05-12-ts-guards-task37. **Task #9 resolution (2026-06-21): landed** at the canonical path on the `test:component` tier (real `vite build --mode production` + dist scan); the contract test also asserts `__DEV_TRUST_FINGERPRINT__` is absent (token must be substituted away, not left live), per @semantic-guard.

- [ ] **`sdk-core` framing codec property-based (fast-check) coverage — deferred to R-41 suite** (slug: `sdk-core-framing-fast-check`, browser-client-join task #9). Task #9 landed example-based framing tests in `packages/sdk-core/src/framing/__tests__/length-prefix.test.ts` (encode round-trip, partial reads across chunks, multi-frame chunks, max-frame + zero-length + truncated-prefix rejection, BE byte layout). ADR-0028 §182/184 calls for property-based testing (fast-check) of the frame codec — random payloads / random chunk-split round-trips. Deferred to the R-41 test-suite task to avoid pulling `fast-check` into this foundation package early. **Pickup at R-41 planning**: add `fast-check` devDep to `packages/sdk-core`, add a property test asserting `FrameDecoder(push(randomSplits(encodeFrame(p)))) === p` for arbitrary `p` and arbitrary chunk boundaries, plus a property that no chunk sequence grows `bufferedByteLength` past `4 + MAX_MESSAGE_SIZE`. Owner: test. Surfaced 2026-06-21 by browser-client-join task #9 (test reviewer Gate-1).

- [ ] **`@vitest/coverage-v8` provider not installed — blocks R-41 ≥90% coverage gate** (slug: `vitest-coverage-v8-tooling`, **Owner: test** — coordinate with infrastructure on the workspace-wide devDep). `packages/sdk-core/vitest.config.ts` and `packages/test-utils/vitest.config.ts` both declare `coverage: { provider: 'v8', ... }`, but `@vitest/coverage-v8` is not installed anywhere in the workspace, so `vitest run --coverage` errors `Cannot find dependency '@vitest/coverage-v8'`. Deliberately NOT added in task #9 (Lead scope decision 2026-06-21): it is a workspace-wide test-tooling devDep that would widen `pnpm-lock.yaml` beyond the additive sdk-core subtree and break the clean Mechanical/infrastructure lockfile classification. The Layer-3 `test-coverage` guard is presence-based and already passes; this only blocks the *measured* ≥90% gate. **Pickup at R-41**: add `@vitest/coverage-v8` (pinned, matching the `vitest ^4.1.8` major) as a workspace devDep with infra ack on the wider lockfile, then enforce the 90% threshold in the coverage config / CI. test-utils shares the identical gap and should be wired in the same change. Surfaced 2026-06-21 by browser-client-join task #9 (test reviewer Gate-2).

- [ ] **Root ESLint + Prettier + svelte-check `pnpm lint` toolchain** (slug: `root-eslint-prettier-svelte-check-toolchain`, **Owner: infrastructure**, status: to be scheduled). R-9 calls for "ESLint + Prettier + svelte-check wired into a root `pnpm lint` task." Today every package's `lint` target is `tsc --noEmit` (the repo has no ESLint/Prettier setup), and svelte-check needs Svelte (a later task). Standing up the root toolchain is a monorepo-bootstrap change — root `package.json` devDeps + shared `.eslintrc`/`.prettierrc` + a flip of every existing package's `project.json` `lint` target — which is infrastructure-owned per the R-8 bootstrap precedent. **Deliberately out of scope for browser-client-join task #9**: that task wired only sdk-core's own `tsconfig` + a real `tsc --noEmit` `lint` Nx target (so `nx run-many -t lint` covers the package). **Pickup**: schedule as an infrastructure task; coordinate the per-package `lint`-target migration and add svelte-check once Svelte lands. Surfaced 2026-06-21 by browser-client-join task #9 (client scope call, Gate-1).

## Dashboard Presentation Debt (ADR-0029)
