## Observability Debt



- [ ] **D1 — MH/MC/GC scrape cadences are configured in a file the cluster does not read; deployed cadence is 15 s, not 5 s/10 s (surfaced 2026-09-02, story task 7)**: `infra/docker/prometheus/prometheus.yml` sets `mh-service` to `scrape_interval: 5s` ("More frequent for real-time media metrics", `:44`) and `mc`/`gc` to 10 s (`:37`, `:30`). That file is **live but local-only** — mounted by `docker-compose.yml:87-98`. The **deployed** path is Kind: `infra/kind/scripts/setup.sh:587` applies `infra/kubernetes/overlays/kind/observability/`, a labels-only passthrough over `infra/kubernetes/observability/prometheus-config.yaml`, which declares **no per-job `scrape_interval` at all** — every job inherits `global.scrape_interval: 15s` (`:27`, its only occurrence). So MH is scraped at **15 s wherever anyone actually triages**, 3× coarser than intended. **Why it matters, not cosmetic**: a decomposed forwarding-latency histogram is close to useless for triage at 15 s — a media-quality incident is typically shorter than a few scrape intervals, so a responder sees two or three points across the whole event, not enough to distinguish a spike from a ramp or to tell which of the three latency phases moved. The 5 s cadence was chosen precisely to make that readable. **Fix**: add the three per-job `scrape_interval` overrides to the authoritative k8s config (~6 lines), and close the mechanism as well as the instance — reciprocal `ANCHOR (DRY):` comments in both files plus a sync guard, following the tree's existing precedents (`packages/sdk-core/src/validation/limits.ts:94`; `scripts/guards/simple/validate-subdomain-regex-sync.sh`, `validate-slug-class-sync.sh`). Without the guard the two files rediverge silently. **Also update `docs/observability/dashboard-conventions.md` §Periodicity as part of this fix** — that subsection currently documents the 15 s deployed cadence as a gap, and every statement in it (the Deployed column, "the deployed cadence is 15 s", the Intended/Deployed split, the triage-resolution argument) **inverts and becomes false** the moment the overrides are applied. Nothing else will prompt it, and the doc that describes the drift truthfully today would become the tree's most confident wrong statement about cadence. **Note the deploy step**: the ConfigMap edit changes nothing until `kubectl apply -k infra/kubernetes/overlays/kind/observability/` runs and Prometheus reloads — an in-tree edit alone re-creates the same doc-says-X/reality-says-Y gap pointing the other way. Rollback is one line (revert the keys, re-apply; no migration, no restart ordering, existing series unaffected). **Cost is explicitly NOT an objection** — scrape interval changes samples-per-series, not cardinality, and does not multiply series count. **Why not fixed in task 7**: Domain-judgment on a deployed config owned by infrastructure, who was not on that panel; ADR-0024 §6.3 routes that to owner-implements, and the required `kubectl apply` is a host operation the devloop container cannot perform. Documented truthfully in `docs/observability/dashboard-conventions.md` §Periodicity in the meantime. Owner: **infrastructure** (+ operations for the cadence decision). **Corroboration — three independent arrivals, 2026-09-02 story task #9 (@observability, Gate 3)**: two further specialists reached this gap from directions unrelated to the original latency-triage argument. @media-handler-consult arrived via *buffer dynamics* (asking what scrape cadence a datagram-queue gauge needs to be meaningful); @operations arrived via *config precedence*, and stated it in the most legible form yet — "a media service scraped at 15 s in the environment that matters, while the config that says media deserves 5 s is the one nobody deploys." Recorded because the entry previously read as one observer's finding: three arrivals from three directions is materially better prioritisation evidence, and the third route is the one that shows the drift is reachable without any media-specific context at all. **A live instance of the cost, same day**: `infra/services/mh-service/configmap.yaml` briefly carried the literal "MH is scraped at 15 s" in a comment; @code-reviewer read the nearest Prometheus config (the local-only compose file), concluded 5 s, and filed the comment as a factual error. A careful reviewer was actively misled by the two-config split during review — not hypothetically. The comment was rewritten to state the *relationship* (scrape interval ≫ buffer fill/drain time) and cite no figure at all, which is the cheapest form of re-derivability: do not restate a value the tree already encodes twice. That fix does not close this entry — it removes one consumer of the ambiguity, not the ambiguity.

- [ ] **MH's transport-ceiling saturation signal exists only while task #16 stays on quinn's non-dropping send path — one boolean silently converts a live alert into a dead one (surfaced 2026-09-02 by @observability at Gate 3 of story task #9)**: quinn offers two datagram send paths and only one of them is observable. `Datagrams::send(data, drop=true)` pops the oldest queued datagram and emits `trace!(len, "dropping outgoing datagram")` and nothing else (`quinn-proto-0.11.17/src/connection/datagrams.rs:44-49`) — an untraceable, uncountable discard. `send(data, drop=false)` instead sets `send_blocked` and returns `SendDatagramError::Blocked(data)` (`:52-55`, with `Event::DatagramsUnblocked` at `connection/mod.rs:3327`) — a **returned value**, therefore countable, therefore the thing an alert can rest on. @media-handler-consult has committed task #16 to the non-dropping path via `send_datagram_wait`, which is what makes MH's transport saturation observable at all, and additionally makes the application queue trip first **mechanically** rather than merely numerically (a shallower app bound alone guarantees nothing if the code pushes into quinn unconditionally). **The exposure**: flipping that one boolean — a plausible edit for someone optimising away an `await`, in a hot path where awaiting looks like the expensive choice — silently removes the only countable saturation signal. **Why this is worse than losing a metric**: once an alert rests on the `Blocked` counter, the flip leaves the alert *defined, enabled, and rendering as active on the dashboard*, while it can never fire again. **A dead alert is worse than no alert** (@operations), because it manufactures assurance at exactly the wrong moment: a responder triaging audio loss who checks the media-saturation alert, sees green, and moves on has been *actively misled*, whereas an absent alert routes them to the next signal. This is ADR-0036 §11's "a control that applies but never fires" with the operational consequence spelled out, and it is the **third instance of one pattern in this story** — the struck `MH | Audio jitter` objective (`docs/observability/slos.md` §Struck), the gauge-only-alert prohibition on `MH_DATAGRAM_BUFFER_AUDIO_FRAMES`, and this. All three read as coverage, cannot fire, and stop anyone asking the question. **Why guard-able rather than merely documentable, which is unusual for this class**: the failure has a single mechanical signature — a `drop=true` argument, or a `send_datagram(` call where `send_datagram_wait(` is required — inside `crates/mh-service/`. That is a grep, not a semantic judgement, so unlike most "someone might later change X" residuals this one can be enforced rather than hoped for. **Fix shapes**: (a) a `dt-guard` simple check rejecting the dropping-send call form in mh-service, with the rationale naming the alert-invalidation consequence rather than merely asserting the rule — a guard whose justification names the failure it prevents survives review, one that asserts a preference does not; (b) at minimum, a comment at the call site recording that `drop=false` is load-bearing for **observability**, not only for back-pressure (@media-handler-consult has committed to this half regardless). Prefer (a); (b) alone leaves the property resting on a comment nobody re-reads. **Not actionable until task #16 lands** — the call site does not exist yet, so there is nothing to guard today. Owner: **media-handler** (task #16 for the call site and the comment; the guard is infrastructure's mechanism per task #17), **@observability reviews** — the property being protected is metric survivability, which is why this sits in Observability Debt rather than the alerting section: it is about whether a signal can be emitted at all, upstream of any expression-or-threshold question. Defer trigger: task #16 review.

- [ ] **D2 — client SDK OTel export interval is unconfigured; runs at the 60 s OTel default (surfaced 2026-09-02, story task 7)**: `packages/sdk-core/src/telemetry/telemetryConfig.ts:111` constructs `new PeriodicExportingMetricReader({ exporter })` with **no `exportIntervalMillis`**; `grep -rn exportInterval packages/` returns nothing. The intended cadence is 10 s. **There is no config key to cite**, which is why `dashboard-conventions.md` §Periodicity records this as an open item rather than naming one. **Operational consequence**: a 60 s client export against a 5–15 s MH scrape means client-side first-media and drop counts **cannot be lined up against MH-side metrics on any incident timeline** — the two sides of the media path are up to a minute out of phase, and the client drop counters are (per ADR-0036 §11) the *only* signal for a join or rotation path that has silently stopped delivering keys. **Fix**: set `exportIntervalMillis` from a named config value when the SDK media pipeline lands (story task 19). Owner: **client**.

- [ ] **D3 — seven ratio alerts do not carry the non-zero-denominator guard (surfaced 2026-09-02, story task 7)**: `GCHighErrorRate`, `GCErrorBudgetBurnRateCritical`, `GCErrorBudgetBurnRateWarning`, `GCMCAssignmentFailures`, `GCTokenRefreshFailures`, `GCDatabaseDown`, `MCGCHeartbeatWarning`. The convention (`and sum(rate(<denominator>)) > 0`) is now documented at `docs/observability/alert-conventions.md` §Non-Zero-Denominator Guard and seeded into `_template-service-alerts.yaml`, but these predate it. **None is currently misfiring** — all seven have pre-existing totals as denominators, and PromQL yields NaN for 0/0 which does not fire, so this is consistency rather than an active defect. **Read the convention before "fixing" these**, because the obvious rationale is wrong: the guard is not divide-by-zero protection, it is load-bearing only when the denominator is *attempts* (`forwarded + dropped`), where guarding one addend instead of the sum makes the alert **silent in the everything-dropped case**. Also do **not** normalise the three deliberate look-alike idioms into conformance: `mc-alerts.yaml:69` is a rate floor (`> 0.1`), and `gc-alerts.yaml:123,140` are offset baselines for silence detection — removing the `offset` inverts their meaning. Four of the seven are `severity: page`. Durable fix is a `dt-guard` check flagging unguarded ratio expressions, which must distinguish those three idioms or it will push someone to break the seasonal-baseline alerts. Owner: **observability** (mechanism) with the GC/MC service specialists (per-rule judgment, ADR-0031).

- [ ] **D4 — the media-path identity prohibition has no enforcement covering its stated scope (surfaced 2026-09-02, story task 7)**: `docs/observability/label-taxonomy.md` §Media-path identity states the meeting-identifier ban as applying to "any metric **anywhere in this design**", per ADR-0036 §11. The durable enforcement is §11's **directory-scoped macro deny**, which covers only the media hot-path directory — strictly narrower than the rule's stated scope. **Nothing enforces the difference**, and the vocabulary guard cannot close it: `meeting_id` is in no guard vocabulary (`crates/dt-guard/src/common/pii_vocabulary.rs` has no `meeting` token), and `meeting_id_hash` is **actively exempted by construction** — `HASHED_SUFFIXES` (`pii_vocabulary.rs:286`) contains `_hash` and `_id_hash`, consumed by `metric_labels.rs:529 is_hashed_label()`. So a raw or hashed meeting-id label on any non-media metric passes every guard in the tree today. **Wrong-fix trap**: adding `meeting_id` to the Category B denylist is *inert against the realistic spelling* and would read as coverage while shipping the violation — ADR-0036 §11's "vocabulary additions cannot be cited as the protection" as a concrete instance. A real fix needs segment-splitting matching plus a decision about the ADR-0028 grandfathered join-flow set, which legitimately carries `meeting_id_hash`. Task-sized. Owner: **observability + security**. **AMENDED 2026-09-02 (story task 8, security review).** Three facts D4 did not have. **(a) There is no server-side backstop — the proxy actively permits the label.** `crates/gc-service/src/services/telemetry_filter.rs:25` `ALLOWLIST` carries `meeting_id_hash` at index 3, applied by `filter_attrs` to datapoint attributes **and** exemplar `filtered_attributes` on Gauge/Sum/Histogram/ExponentialHistogram. So D4's "the client-side guard is missing" understates it: the guard is missing **and** the one control that would otherwise catch a client regression is configured to allow it. Three independent blessing points — `HASHED_SUFFIXES` exemption, `media-transport.test.ts:270` asserting it, and this ALLOWLIST — each individually defensible, composing to zero defence in depth. **(b) Exemplars are an uncovered surface.** R2 names four surfaces; the directory-scoped macro deny reaches log fields and span attributes, the (proposed) label allow-list reaches metric labels, and **nothing reaches exemplars**. ADR-0036 §11 rejects exemplars by name on this path, so the correct rule is a presence **deny**, not an allow-list extension — extending the allow-list would legalise them with a blessed label set. Note `packages/sdk-core/src/telemetry/telemetryConfig.ts` configures no exemplar behaviour at all, so OTel JS defaults govern: a config-shaped surface with no macro and no call site to match. **(c) Mechanism: count-don't-drop.** `filter_metrics` binds `metric` at the loop head (`:155`), so `metric.name` is available at every `filter_attrs` call site — name-aware handling is parameter threading, **not** the redesign it was first sized as. Enforcement stays client-side per R3; the server contributes the missing **detection**: increment a bounded counter when an allowlisted-but-media-inappropriate key appears on a media-prefixed metric. No behaviour change, no risk to the ADR-0028 grandfathered join metrics. **Both new labels must be projected onto a closed set before becoming dimensions** — the offending key bucketed by ALLOWLIST membership, the metric name matched against the known media set with an `other` bucket. Not `assert!`: this is production request-path code over untrusted OTLP, so a panic is client-triggerable and ADR-0002 prohibits it outright; `debug_assert!` is worse, compiling out exactly where the untrusted input arrives. Owner for the proxy half: **global-controller** (not on the task-8 roster).

- [ ] **D5 — client media-path metrics inherit `meeting_id_hash` by inertia; exemption not yet applied (surfaced 2026-09-02, story task 7)**: `docs/observability/label-taxonomy.md` §Media-path identity R3 requires client media-path metrics to carry **only** `client_version` and `org_id`. That is a **required end state, not current behaviour**: `packages/sdk-core/src/session/MeetingSession.ts:308` builds the implicit join label set (`client_version`, `meeting_id_hash`, `org_id`) and threads it into every emission site including the media module (`packages/sdk-core/src/media/events.ts:44`), and `packages/sdk-core/src/media/__tests__/media-transport.test.ts:270` **asserts** `meeting_id_hash` in that label set. So a passing test currently pins the behaviour the rule prohibits. The risk is inertia: the label set arrives by default and **doing nothing ships the violation**. A hashed meeting id is barred on both of §11's grounds — identical cardinality to a raw one, and per-meeting aggregation in a two-person meeting is nearly per-stream. **Fix**: apply the media-path exemption and update the test when the SDK media pipeline lands (story task 19). Owner: **client** (with observability review). **AMENDED 2026-09-02 (story task 8, security review).** The inertia is dangerous, not merely untidy: `reason` × `meeting_id_hash` is a **decryption oracle**. The reject-reason vocabulary is a faithful mirror of where in the crypto stack a frame died; aggregated it is operationally necessary and harmless, but joined to a meeting dimension it becomes a per-target distinguisher — an attacker who can inject crafted frames reads which crypto layer rejected each probe. `unwrap_failed` is the sharpest: it confirms the KEK unwrap **succeeded**, i.e. the prober's KEK generation matches the receiver's, which is a positive fact about receiver key state. The discriminator is **not** codec-versus-crypto but **byte-determined versus receiver-state-dependent**: a reason fully determined by the frame bytes and the public format spec tells the frame's author nothing they did not have (so all eight codec structural rejects are safe to emit per-token), while one depending on receiver-held state is an oracle (`no_kek_for_generation`, `no_roster_entry`, `no_transmit_key` — codec-layer but receiver-state-dependent — `signature_invalid`, `decrypt_failed`, `unwrap_failed`). **This is orthogonal to granularity**: all reason values are emitted individually per R-25/R-31; the restriction is solely about never joining them with a participant, meeting or stream dimension. The rule changes nothing operationally today — R2 already bars identity dimensions from all media-path labels — it exists to pre-answer *"the codec tokens are harmless, can we slice just those by meeting?"*: no, because R2's trace argument still reaches them even though the oracle argument does not.

- [ ] **D6 — `docs/observability/dashboards.md` catalogs almost none of the dashboards that exist (surfaced 2026-09-02, story task 7)**: 14 dashboard JSONs are on disk (`ac`/`gc`/`mc`/`mh` × `overview`/`slos`/`logs`, plus `errors-overview.json` and `_template-service-overview.json`), while the catalog documents a handful and marked two existing ones — `ac-overview.json` (75,849 B) and `mh-overview.json` (53,638 B) — as "🚧 To be created". Those two were corrected in task 7 because they sat in blocks already being edited. **The rest of the index was not audited.** This is the doc an oncall consults to learn what exists; for any service it under-reports, that means hand-rolling PromQL against a deployed dashboard nobody knew was there. Note `platform-overview.json` and `database-performance.json` are **correctly** marked as not existing. Owner: **observability**.
- [ ] **D7 — `ac-slos.json` plots token issuance at p95 while the AC SLO is p99 (surfaced 2026-09-02 by @operations during story task 7)**: `infra/grafana/dashboards/ac-slos.json` uses `histogram_quantile(0.95, …)` — its only quantile — while `docs/observability/slos.md` and ADR-0011 both define AC token issuance and validation as **p99** (< 350 ms / < 50 ms). **The dashboard an operator opens to check SLO compliance is tracking a quantile the SLO is not written against**, so it under-reports exactly the tail the objective exists to bound. On a bcrypt-dominated path p95 and p99 are materially different bars, so this is not a rounding difference. Surfaced only now because task 7 created the authoritative register that makes the comparison possible — which is the register's purpose working as intended, on its first day. **Not fixed in task 7**: changing an SLO dashboard's quantile alters what a shipped compliance signal reports and makes its history non-comparable, the same reason the GC availability-numerator entry is scoped separately; and `ac-slos.json` is AC's artifact per ADR-0031, with AC not on that panel. Check the AC alert rules for the same mismatch while fixing. Owner: **auth-controller** (dashboard), with **observability + operations** as reviewers (ADR-0031).
- [ ] **D8 — `slos.md` declares itself the authoritative SLO register while omitting ~11 targets the rest of `docs/observability/` already asserts (surfaced 2026-09-02 by @dry-reviewer at Gate 2 review of story task 7)**: task 7 created `docs/observability/slos.md` and gave it stated precedence ("This file is authoritative for SLO targets... Where the two differ, this file wins"). Its Live register holds **5** targets (AC issuance/validation, GC request + availability, MC session join) plus the two MH rows. The tree asserts **~16** SLO targets outside it, most absent from the register: `docs/observability/metrics/ac-service.md:30` (p99 <350ms), `:220` (p95 <200ms); `metrics/gc-service.md:49` (p95 <200ms), `:106` (p95 <20ms), `:139` (p99 <50ms), `:607` (p99 <200ms); `metrics/mc-service.md:82` (p99 <100ms), `:96` (p99 <10ms); `metrics/mh-service.md:51` (p95 <100ms); `dashboards.md:28` (200ms), `:32` (20ms), `:35` (50ms), `:167` (500ms); `alerts.md:714` (p99 <500ms); `alert-conventions.md:47` (500ms). (`dashboard-conventions.md`'s "a 200ms SLO sets the red threshold at 0.200" is a threshold-config *example*, not a target claim — leave it.) **Why this is newly load-bearing rather than pre-existing noise**: before task 7 these were scattered peers with no stated winner. Task 7 declared a winner. An authoritative-but-incomplete register is worse than no register, and `slos.md` names the exact failure itself — "an absent row is indistinguishable from a decision not to have an SLO, which is precisely how a paging gap survives behind a green dashboard" — then omits eleven rows. Concretely: a reader consulting the authoritative register for the MC assignment-latency SLO concludes there is none, while `dashboards.md:32` specifies a 20ms SLO line for it (and `GCMCAssignmentSlow` pages on it). **Two live inconsistencies already visible**: `dashboards.md:32` specifies a "20ms SLO line" for MC Assignment Latency, an SLO the authoritative register does not contain at all; and `dashboards.md:167` specifies a 500ms line for MC *message processing* while the register's only 500ms entry is MC *session join* — a different operation. **Fix**: reconcile each of the ~16 into one of three dispositions — (a) genuine ratified SLO, promote into the register; (b) a dashboard threshold convention, not an SLO, reword to drop the "SLO" claim; (c) stale, delete. Then make the satellites point rather than restate, as `dashboards.md`'s AC and MH blocks now do. **Not doable inside task 7**: per-target this is a ratification judgment, and `slos.md` itself states a target lands only when observability and operations have both signed off; eleven of those decisions is task-sized, not an inline edit. **Wrong-fix trap**: do not bulk-copy the 16 literals into the register to make it "complete" — that promotes unratified dashboard thresholds to fleet SLOs by clerical action, which is how `MHHighAudioLatency`'s unratified `>30ms` became load-bearing in the first place. Owner: **observability + operations** (joint, per ADR-0011:40). **WIDENED 2026-09-02 with @operations' independent Gate-3 finding (F6), which reached the same defect from live artifacts rather than from doc restatements — folded here rather than opening a parallel entry, since two entries for one gap is the failure mode this file already suffers from.** The omitted targets are backed by SHIPPED artifacts, not aspiration, and one of them **pages today**: **GC MC-assignment p95 < 20 ms** is enforced by `GCMCAssignmentSlow` (`infra/docker/prometheus/rules/gc-alerts.yaml`, `histogram_quantile(0.95, …) > 0.020`) and dashboarded at `gc-slos.json` "MC Assignment SLO – Compliance %" / "– Current p95"; **AC availability and error-rate** ship as dedicated panels in `ac-slos.json` ("Availability SLO – Error Budget Remaining", "– Uptime", "Error Rate SLO – Current") against a register carrying only AC's two latency rows; **MC heartbeat and token-refresh** ship in `mc-slos.json` ("GC Heartbeat SLO – Success Rate", "Token Refresh SLO – Success Rate", "– Current p99") against a register carrying only session join. These are `*-slos.json` **compliance** dashboards, not exploratory panels. **The paging case is the sharpest consequence and the one to write any fix against**: an operator paged by `GCMCAssignmentSlow` consults the authoritative register for the objective, its error budget and its burn posture — the register's stated purpose — finds no row, and under the precedence rule concludes the 20 ms threshold is an unratified guess when it is a shipped, alerting objective. That **inverts the triage**, which is worse than the ambiguity the precedence rule was added to remove. Note `ac-slos.json`'s "Token Issuance Latency SLO – Current **p95**" independently corroborates D7 (register says p99). Ratifiers therefore include **auth-controller, global-controller and meeting-controller** alongside the joint observability + operations owners. **Strongest single piece of evidence that the register was worth writing** (@dry-reviewer's observation at Gate 3): the AC token-issuance quantile drift shows up **twice, independently, in two different artifact kinds** — `dashboards.md:140-141` asserted p95 in *prose*, and `ac-slos.json` ships a "Token Issuance Latency SLO – Current **p95**" panel in *dashboard JSON* — against a register and an ADR that both say **p99**. Two encodings of one objective, drifted the same way, in two media, neither detectable before a register existed to contradict them. That is the argument for the backfill: each unreconciled target is not one stale line but an unknown number of mutually-consistent-looking copies. The prose half was fixed in task 7; the JSON half is D7.
- [ ] **D9 — no commit-boundary assertion that planned files are tracked or staged; eleven green guards validated an artifact that would not have been committed (surfaced 2026-09-02 by @operations at Gate 3 of story task 7)**: at the end of task 7, `docs/observability/slos.md` — the central artifact of the task — was **untracked**, along with the devloop output directory. A `git commit -am` stages only modified *tracked* files, so it would have committed the eleven modified files and **silently dropped `slos.md`**. Closed for that commit with `git add -A`; nothing structural prevents a recurrence. **THE MECHANISM IS THE OPPOSITE OF A BLIND SPOT, AND THIS IS THE PART TO READ BEFORE PROPOSING A FIX.** The guards do not fail to see untracked files — they **deliberately include** them: `crates/dt-guard/src/common/git_changes.rs:132` documents the changed set as *"Union of [`get_modified_files`] + [`get_untracked_files`], deduplicated"*, implemented at `:139-140` (`modified.extend(untracked)`) over `git ls-files --others --exclude-standard` at `:117-119`; `scripts/guards/common.sh:62` does the same on the bash side. So all eleven guards **saw `slos.md`, read it, validated its content and reported green** about a file that would have vanished at commit. `validate-cross-boundary-scope` stayed green *correctly*: the path was in the plan **and** in the guard's changed set, so planned-untouched drift rightly did not fire. **The green was the source of the false assurance, not absent from it** — the same false-green shape as the `dt-guard` path-roots entry in this section, where an empty result reads as success. **WRONG-FIX TRAP**: do **not** "make the guards check for untracked files". They already do, the inclusion is deliberate and correct for pre-commit linting, and removing it would break every guard's ability to validate new work. **Fix**: a separate assertion at the **commit boundary** — at Gate 2 or in `scripts/workflow/devloop-stop-hook.sh` — that every path named in the devloop plan's Cross-Boundary Classification table is **tracked or staged** (`git ls-files --error-unmatch`, or absent from `git ls-files --others --exclude-standard`). Different control at a different lifecycle point; the guards themselves need no change. **Why it is worth a guard rather than a checklist line**: it generalises to *every* devloop that creates a file, and the failure is invisible to content review by construction — task 7 verified every citation, count and line number across seven review rounds while the central artifact sat untracked in plain sight in `git status` output. **Concrete counterfactual, which is the reason to prioritise it**: the outcome would not have been "a missing file". It would have been **eleven confident pointers asserting authority over a nonexistent path** — ADR-0011's amendment, `alerts.md`, both `dashboards.md` blocks, `gc-incident-response.md`, `mh-alerts.yaml` ×2, `API_CONTRACTS.md`, and the D7/D8 entries — which is **strictly worse than the `(to be created)` hedges that same commit retired**, because a hedge tells a reader the file is missing and a confident pointer does not. Owner: **infrastructure** or **test** (story-runner / commit-gate machinery, not metrics-guard infrastructure under ADR-0031) — deliberately routed away from observability, who found it but does not own the pipeline.

- [ ] **D10 — `failure_reason` carries a different bounded value set on MH than on GC/MC, so `sum by(failure_reason)` silently does not line up across services (surfaced 2026-09-02 by @observability during story task 8)**: the same label key on the same JWT-validation concept enumerates `none, signature_invalid, expired, missing_token, scope_mismatch, malformed` in `docs/observability/metrics/gc-service.md:408` and `docs/observability/metrics/mc-service.md:141`/`:581`, but `none, signature_invalid, expired, scope_mismatch, malformed, validation_failed` in `docs/observability/metrics/mh-service.md:135`. Both six-valued; they differ by one member (`missing_token` vs `validation_failed`). **This is a real service divergence, not doc drift** — verified against code rather than catalogs alone: `crates/mh-service/src/observability/metrics.rs:230-231` documents MH's set and `:361` emits `validation_failed`, so the catalogs are accurate and the services genuinely differ. **Consequence**: a cross-service panel or alert aggregating `sum by(failure_reason)` produces two disjoint series for what a reader takes to be one taxonomy, and the divergence is invisible on any single-service dashboard. `signature_invalid` — the token the whole fleet does agree on — masks the problem, because the value an operator most often looks at lines up. **Not a finding against story task 8**: pre-existing, discovered while checking the vectors' crypto reject-reason spellings against fleet spellings (criterion 12), and it never entered fix-or-defer. **Deliberately NOT folded into D4/D5**: those carry the media-path identity/oracle mechanism; this is label-value drift on a control-plane metric, a different mechanism, and merging them would muddy D4/D5's argument. **Action**: decide whether the two sets should converge (and if so which spelling wins — note `validation_failed` and `missing_token` are not synonyms, so this is a semantics question before it is a naming one) or whether the divergence is intentional and should be documented as such in all four catalog locations. Ties into the existing "Label-value drift is unguarded on every surface" entry below, which is the mechanism that would have caught this. Owner: observability, with auth-controller/global-controller/meeting-controller/media-handler as per-service ratifiers.

- [ ] **Guard path roots are unasserted inputs, and where every consumer reads "empty" as success the guard goes silently green (found 2026-09-01 by @observability at Gate 1 of `docs/devloop-outputs/2026-09-01-internal-contract-reshape/`, self-audit prompted by @security's dead-GSA-glob finding; owner: observability per ADR-0031 "Guard infrastructure for metrics/dashboards/alerts")**: every path root in the observability-owned guards is a hardcoded constant that is never asserted to exist; each extractor early-returns an empty set on a missing directory. Whether that is safe depends entirely on **consumer polarity** — whether some consumer treats an empty result as a *failure*. Two sites have no such consumer and are the load-bearing instances: (a) `crates/dt-guard/src/application_metrics.rs:176` (`ALERTS_SUBDIR` = `infra/docker/prometheus/rules`) feeds only check 4 ("alert-rule metrics exist in source code"), so an empty set means "nothing to validate" and alert-rule validation stops happening, green; (b) `crates/dt-guard/src/kustomize.rs:145` returns zero hits when `infra/grafana/dashboards` is not a dir **or** `infra/grafana/kustomization.yaml` is not a file — OR-gated on two inputs, one of them a single file (likelier to be renamed than a directory), silencing a *bidirectional* coverage check in both directions at once, with both paths as inline literals rather than named constants. Three further roots are polarity-safe today and should get the assertion anyway for uniformity: `application_metrics.rs:116` (`DASHBOARDS_SUBDIR`, saved by check 5 reading empty as "every metric undashboarded"), `application_metrics.rs:66` (`CATALOG_SUBDIR`, saved by check 6 reading empty as "every metric undocumented"), and `metric_coverage.rs:69` (`tests_dir`). **Fix — one predicate, and it is joint with @security's half**: a guard's path roots are *inputs* and must be asserted present; "directory absent" is a hard error, never an empty result set. Twelve call sites across the guard crate; fourteen more in the GSA canon (see the `.proto`-scanner and dead-glob entries under §"From ADR-0024 §6 Amendment" — same mechanism, and that half is **live** where this half is **latent**, so schedule theirs first). **Two in-tree precedents to copy rather than a principle to argue from**: `crates/dt-guard/src/metric_coverage.rs:66-67` documents *why* its empty case is safe ("caller treats this as a per-service uncovered signal"), and `crates/dt-guard/src/alert_rules.rs:260-262` returns an **error** when the runbooks dir cannot be resolved. The right answer has been in the same crate the whole time; it simply never generalised. **Enumeration is PARTIAL and the bound is stated so a later reader does not treat it as exhaustive**: these five were found by grepping one idiom, `if !x.is_dir() { return ... }`. Sites using `exists()`, `try_exists()`, a `match` on the read result, or `let Ok(..) else` are invisible to that grep and were not assessed. Same class as the "control checks the wrong target set" family already recorded in this section. Task-sized: shared gate tooling with its own `.test.sh` and fixtures, not foldable into a feature devloop. Defer trigger: the next dt-guard metric-guard devloop, or @security's `.proto`-scanner work, whichever lands first.
- [ ] **`gc_mc_assignments_total{rejection_reason="no_mcs_available"}` conflates two failure modes with opposite remediations; `rpc_failed` is declared-but-unemitted (found 2026-08-21 by @observability at review of `docs/devloop-outputs/2026-08-21-gc-invalid-request-rejection-reason/`)**: the terminal match in `crates/gc-service/src/services/mc_assignment.rs` records `no_mcs_available` for BOTH a genuinely empty candidate pool AND an all-candidates-unreachable exhaustion — the `Err(e)` RPC-failure branch pushes to `tried_mcs` and continues WITHOUT setting `last_rejection_reason`, so an all-RPC-failed run falls through to `None => ("error", Some("no_mcs_available"))`. The two modes have OPPOSITE remediations (empty pool → scale / check MC registration + heartbeats; all-unreachable → NetworkPolicy / DNS / mTLS / partition, runbook Scenario D) yet render identically in `sum by(rejection_reason)` — operators cannot distinguish them from metrics alone; the only distinguishing signal today is the `gc.service.assignment` warn `"MC RPC failed, will retry"` in GC logs (a log-grep standing in for a metric). Consequently `rpc_failed` is DECLARED (catalog `docs/observability/metrics/gc-service.md`, runbook Scenario D, and two tests: `crates/gc-service/src/observability/metrics.rs`, `crates/gc-service/tests/mc_assignment_metrics_integration.rs`) but NEVER emitted in production. **The catalog's `(declared/unemitted)` tag on `rpc_failed` is PROVISIONAL pending THIS reconciliation** (keep-and-annotate was locked at Gate 1 explicitly on that basis). **Action**: decide the design — either (a) the `Err` exhaustion path emits a distinct `rejection_reason` (wire `rpc_failed`, or split `no_mcs_available` into empty-pool vs unreachable), restoring metric-only distinguishability and making Scenario D filterable as designed; or (b) formally retire `rpc_failed` from catalog + tests and document `no_mcs_available` as the deliberate union — then remove the provisional tag. Either way reconcile the catalog note, runbook Scenario D/legend, and the two tests in lockstep. Interim mitigation (landed this devloop): runbook Scenario D carries a "not currently emitted; confirm via GC logs before running the checks" caveat, and new Scenario F covers `invalid_request`. Owner: global-controller (the `Err`-branch code decision) + observability (catalog/label taxonomy) + operations (runbook Scenario D) + test (the two test refs). Defer trigger: the next devloop touching GC's assignment path (`mc_assignment.rs`); @operations holds the re-raise trigger. Task-sized — cross-owner design decision + test surface, not an inline edit inside this feature.
- [ ] **No guard reconciles catalog enumerated label VALUES against emission-site literals — root cause of the whole `rejection_reason` drift class (found 2026-08-21 by @observability + @operations at Gate 1 of `docs/devloop-outputs/2026-08-21-gc-invalid-request-rejection-reason/`)**: the `rejection_reason` drift (documented `rpc_failed` never emitted; emitted `unspecified`/`no_mcs_available` undocumented until this devloop) went undetected because NO guard checks that the label VALUES enumerated in `docs/observability/metrics/<svc>-service.md` match the string literals actually emitted at production `record_*` call sites. Confirmed coverage gap: `crates/dt-guard/src/application_metrics.rs` (behind `validate-application-metrics.sh`) enforces metric-NAME↔catalog coverage + dashboard/label-KEY policy only; `crates/dt-guard/src/metric_labels.rs` enforces label-KEY PII/cardinality/naming + value BOUNDEDNESS (length / unbounded patterns) but NOT enumerated-value-set membership; `metric_coverage.rs` is name-level src-vs-test. So a catalog value list can drift from emission in EITHER direction, silently, indefinitely — same "control checks the wrong target set" family as the dashboard-panel-label gap under the dashboard-completeness entry below and the GSA-glob-drift entries under §"From ADR-0024 §6 Amendment". **Action**: add a dt-guard subcommand that extracts the `rejection_reason`-class label-value literals from emission sites (the terminal match / `record_*` wrappers — marked by the ANCHOR(DRY) comments landed this devloop in `mc_assignment.rs` + `gc-service.md`) and reconciles them against the catalog enumerations, failing on drift in either direction — closing the class, not just this instance. The ANCHOR(DRY) comments are the manual stand-in the guard would automate. Owner: observability (label taxonomy) + dt-guard tooling. Task-sized — new guard + self-test + fixtures in `crates/dt-guard/tests/fixtures/`; shared gate tooling, not folded into a feature devloop. Defer trigger: next dt-guard metric-guard devloop, or the next `rejection_reason`-class drift incident.
- [ ] **Top-line `TOTAL_RESULT=N/A` on a fully-green run is not self-describing** (surfaced 2026-08-20 during the validation-run-all devloop review; **pre-existing aggregator behavior, NOT introduced by that diff** — but that diff promotes it from intermittent to the common case). `layer-all.sh`'s top-line `TOTAL_RESULT` is the worst-child over per-layer results, and `N/A` ranks **above** `OK` in the `__status_rank` ladder (rank 4 > rank 3) — deliberately, so a real documented-gap N/A is never masked by an OK sibling. Consequence at the top line: a clean run where Layer 4 aggregates `N/A` (proto's `test.sh` placeholder emits `not-applicable-to-this-lang`) and Layer 6 aggregates `N/A` (rust/ts audit `SKIPPED-NO-DIFF no-dep-changes` + proto `N/A`) produces `TOTAL_RESULT=N/A` even though everything that ran passed. **Why this change matters here**: with the skip-if-untouched short-circuit retired (2026-08-20), every layer runs every language on every task, so a no-dep-change task now *reliably* shows L4=N/A + L6=N/A → `TOTAL_RESULT=N/A` on essentially every green run, where before it was intermittent. The **exit code is still 0** (`N/A`→exit 0 via `status_to_exit_code`), so gating is correct — this is purely a top-line-label legibility defect: an operator glancing at `TOTAL_RESULT=N/A` cannot distinguish "clean pass" from "nothing ran / real gap." **Proposed fix**: collapse the *top line only* to `OK` when every contributing child is ∈{`OK`,`N/A`,`SKIPPED-*`} with at least one `OK` (i.e. the aggregate is a success-exit class with real work done), leaving the per-layer `LAYER=n RESULT=…` lines and `__status_rank` worst-wins semantics untouched (those must keep N/A>OK to avoid masking mid-pipeline). **Blast radius**: touches the top-line emit in `layer-all.sh` + must be reconciled with `status_to_exit_code` and every consumer that greps `TOTAL_RESULT=` (CI summary jobs, run-story gate telemetry); needs its own design cycle + hermetic cases in `layer-all.test.sh`. Deliberately out of scope for the validation-run-all devloop (own design decision, own test cycle). Owner: observability.
- [ ] **No system-overview (single-pane-of-glass) dashboard**: only per-service dashboards exist (`infra/grafana/dashboards/{ac,gc,mc,mh}-overview.json` + `errors-overview.json`; `_template-service-overview.json` is a per-service template, not system-wide). Add a cross-service overview: a curated RED-method top line (rate / errors / duration per service) + key business SLIs (meetings active, join success rate, participants active) + optionally the most important client metrics (see the client-governance entry below). Two design constraints: (a) **ownership is cross-cutting → observability-owned**, NOT any single service — ADR-0031 makes per-service dashboards service-owned, but a system overview crosses boundaries; (b) **curate, don't dump**, and avoid drift from the per-service panels (share the query source or treat it as an explicit curated derived view). Owner: observability. Surfaced 2026-08-08.

- [ ] **Client metrics have plumbing but no governance — dashboards, alarms, AND a coverage guard all missing (ADR-0032 analog for the client); needs a `/debate`**: the GC telemetry proxy (R-2: `POST /api/v1/telemetry/v1/{metrics,traces}`) and its proxy-side ingest metrics (#16: `gc_telemetry_ingest_total` etc.) exist, but the client metrics that flow *through* it have **no Grafana dashboards, no alerts, and — critically — no coverage guard**. Service metrics are governed by ADR-0032 (every metric must have a test + dashboard + catalog entry, enforced by `validate-application-metrics` / metric-coverage guards); the client has none of that machinery. When the deferred client-metrics catalog is built out (browser-client-join stubbed it), without a guard client metrics will drift into the exact silent-failure state ADR-0032 fixed for services — a browser-emitted metric with no dashboard/alert/test, invisible until an incident. **Not a copy of the Rust guard**: client metrics travel a different path (browser → GC proxy → OTel collector → Prometheus, not a service scrape) and live in TS, so the guard must be telemetry-path-aware and `ts`-lang-aware (ties into the ADR-0033 polyglot pipeline — the `ts` lang wants its own metric-coverage guard). Needs a `/debate` (cross-cutting: observability + client + infrastructure + polyglot-guard) to set the client-metric governance pattern, mirroring ADR-0032. Owner: observability + client + infrastructure. Surfaced 2026-08-08.

- [ ] **Dashboard-set completeness guard — no canonical service should silently ship a partial dashboard trio (task #62 finding)**: task #62 found MH shipped only `mh-overview.json` for weeks — `mh-logs.json`/`mh-slos.json` were never created, and no guard caught the gap. `scripts/guards/simple/validate-kustomize.sh` enforces dashboard↔kustomize coverage bidirectionally (every JSON in `infra/grafana/dashboards/` is listed in `infra/grafana/kustomization.yaml` and vice-versa), but nothing asserts that each canonical service HAS the full `overview`+`logs`+`slos` trio — a service with only an overview passes every existing guard. **Action**: add a guard asserting every service in the canonical set {ac, gc, mc, mh} has all three of `<svc>-overview.json`, `<svc>-logs.json`, `<svc>-slos.json` under `infra/grafana/dashboards/`. Derive the service list from a single source (e.g. the crate/service table or an existing manifest), NOT a hardcoded literal, per SSoT — and fail loudly on any missing member. **Related guard gap (task #62, @test)**: the `dashboard-panels` guard (`crates/dt-guard/src/dashboard_panels.rs`) validates metric existence + kind + rate-wrapping, but does NOT validate panel label keys/values against the metric catalog — a panel referencing a real metric with a nonexistent label (e.g. `mh_jwt_validations_total{status="success"}` instead of `result="success"`) passes the guard yet renders "No data" in Grafana (the same silent-broken-dashboard-through-the-gate class that motivates the completeness guard above). Extend the guard to also check panel label keys/values against `docs/observability/metrics/<svc>-service.md`. Owner: observability (with infrastructure for guard wiring). Surfaced 2026-08-06 during task #62 by @observability + @test.
- [ ] **`level` template-var in `*-logs.json` uses empty Loki stream — scope to `{app="<svc>-service"}` across all four services (task #62 / security finding)**: the `level` (Log Level Filter) template variable in the per-service logs dashboards (`{ac,gc,mc,mh}-logs.json`) ships with `stream=""`, so it enumerates log levels across ALL apps in Loki rather than the dashboard's own service. `mh-logs.json` (task #62) was intentionally kept byte-consistent with the `mc-logs.json` reference to avoid diverging one service from the set. **Action**: scope the `level` var's query to `stream="{app=\"<svc>-service\"}"` (mirroring the already-scoped `pod` var) on ALL FOUR logs dashboards in one change, so the pattern stays uniform. Pure query-scoping refinement (Loki label-values query var; no untrusted free-text input, no `regex` reflecting input) — small, cross-service, mechanical. Owner: observability (with security). Surfaced 2026-08-06 during task #62 by @security (plan review); deferred by @team-lead to keep the {ac,gc,mc,mh} set consistent rather than scoping MH alone.
- [ ] **MH SLO dashboard gauges reference alert-hard-limits / unratified targets for handshake + token-refresh latency (task #62 finding)**: `infra/grafana/dashboards/mh-slos.json` (task #62) grounds four of six gauges in documented SLOs or live alert boundaries, but two latency gauges lack a ratified SLO: (a) **WebTransport handshake p99** — the catalog defines no handshake SLO; the gauge's 1s red boundary is borrowed from the `MHWebTransportHandshakeSlow` warning alert, which is defined on **p95** (the gauge shows **p99**, so it can turn red before that alert fires — a deliberate quantile mismatch, no p99 alert exists). (b) **Token refresh p99** — no documented latency SLO; thresholds (2.5s/5s) are derived from the histogram bucket ceiling (5s), mirroring `mc-slos.json`. **Action**: when MH SLOs are ratified in ADR-0011 (the `mh-alerts.yaml` header already notes MH has no error-rate SLO defined yet), reconcile these two gauge thresholds to the ratified targets and add the matching alert(s) (a p99 handshake burn alert, a token-refresh latency alert) so dashboard and alerting agree. Owner: observability (with operations for alert authoring). Surfaced 2026-08-06 during task #62 by @observability + @operations.
- [ ] **GC `normalize_endpoint()` has no telemetry arm — telemetry requests conflate into `endpoint="/other"` on `gc_http_*` (browser-client-join Task #16 → GC follow-up)**: `normalize_endpoint()`/`normalize_dynamic_endpoint()` (`crates/gc-service/src/observability/metrics.rs:182-228`) recognize only `/`, `/health`, `/metrics`, `/api/v1/me`, and the `/api/v1/meetings*` family; the telemetry proxy routes `/api/v1/telemetry/v1/{metrics,traces}` (`crates/gc-service/src/routes/mod.rs:170-175`) normalize to `"/other"`. **Consequence**: the pre-handler telemetry rejects that never reach `gc_telemetry_ingest_total` — 401 (route-layer auth) and the DoS-backstop 413 (body > 2× max_bytes) — are only observable on `gc_http_requests_total{endpoint="/other"}`, conflated with every other unrecognized path, so the catalog's size-rejection UNION and the Scenario 10/11 HTTP-split diagnosis steps are approximate (each carries an explicit caveat referencing this entry). **Action**: add two static match arms for the telemetry routes (bounded literals, no cardinality risk — mirror the existing `/api/v1/meetings` static-arm pattern) + extend `test_normalize_endpoint_known_paths`; then remove the `endpoint="/other"` caveats from `docs/observability/metrics/gc-service.md` (honesty block + `error`-status note), `docs/runbooks/gc-incident-response.md` Scenarios 10-11, `docs/observability/alerts.md` (both new entries), and the `GCTelemetryProxyHighRejectionRate` description. Est. <20 LoC. Owner: global-controller (with observability). Surfaced 2026-08-03 during task #16 by @implementer (observability); deferral approved by @team-lead (decision C — barred by task #16's no-instrumentation constraint, not effort).
- [ ] **MC emits no log event on the `MediaConnectionUpdate` success path — `statuses_count` is unreachable from logs at any `RUST_LOG` (browser-client-join Task #20 → MC observability)**: `handle_media_connection_update` (`crates/mc-service/src/webtransport/connection.rs:663-724`) carries `#[instrument(name = "mc.media_connection_update", fields(connection_id, statuses_count = update.statuses.len()))]`, but emits **no event on the success path** — its only log is a `debug!` on the actor-gone error branch (`:715-721`), which is itself invisible under the deployed `RUST_LOG=info,mc_service=debug` because the target `mc.webtransport.connection` does not prefix-match `mc_service`. Since `tracing_subscriber::fmt::layer().json()` (`crates/mc-service/src/main.rs:120`) writes one line per **event** and no `.with_span_events(...)` is configured, the span exists only as context on events that never occur. **Consequence**: `statuses_count` — the per-join media fan-out width, and the single richest completeness datum MC has — cannot be read from logs at any log level. The `mc_participant_mh_status_total{state}` counter (`crates/mc-service/src/actors/participant.rs:478`) is the only available substitute, and it is strictly weaker: it increments *inside* the per-entry loop, so an **empty** fan-out produces no increment at all and is indistinguishable from "the client never sent an update" — precisely the case (GC returns empty `media_servers`, client still reaches `joined`, MC still logs `Join succeeded`) that most needs an observable. **Action**: add an `info!` on the success path of `handle_media_connection_update` carrying `statuses_count` (and `connection_id`), target `mc.webtransport.connection` so it is visible at the deployed `info` global. ~3 LoC; the span field already computes the value. Then update `docs/runbooks/client-dev-local.md` §4.2, which currently documents the counter-delta workaround and explicitly warns readers **not** to reach for the span field — that warning can be removed once the event exists. Owner: observability (with meeting-controller — the edit is in MC's WebTransport connection handler). Surfaced 2026-07-29 during task #20 by @observability: the runbook draft cited a `jq` filter on the span name as "the completeness signal", which returns empty output on a healthy join — a phantom signal in a document that trains readers to treat absence as evidence. Caught at review; the runbook now uses the counter delta.
- [ ] **R-56 inbound HTTP trace-context extraction for AC (browser-client-join Task #25 → GC #26 landed → AC follow-up)**: AC has no inbound gRPC boundary, so R-56's Tonic server-interceptor has no attach point; task #25 wired only the global W3C propagator (registered for free by `init_otel`). **UPDATE (task #26, 2026-07-03)**: the shared `common` HTTP-transport layer now exists — `crates/common/src/observability/otel_http.rs` (`--paired-with=observability`), framework-agnostic `HeaderInjector`/`HeaderExtractor` over `&http::HeaderMap` + `inject_trace_context`/`extract_trace_context` functions, mirroring `otel_grpc.rs`'s pattern. The producer side is also landed: GC's `ac_client.rs` now injects `traceparent`/`tracestate` on every GC→AC HTTP call (task #26 surface (b)), so AC's inbound requests carry a real header to extract, not a hypothetical one. **Remaining action**: AC itself still needs to wire `otel_http::extract_trace_context` at its own inbound HTTP boundary (axum middleware, same shape as GC's `middleware/otel.rs`) — that consumption is NOT part of task #26's scope (GC-only devloop) and remains open. **Security constraint (@security, unchanged)**: the extraction call reads only `traceparent`/`tracestate` — never `authorization` or any bearer header, and never copies header values into span attributes (enforced structurally by `otel_http.rs`'s write-only `HeaderInjector`/keyed-only `HeaderExtractor`, confirmed by @semantic-guard). Owner: auth-controller (consumption) + observability (layer, already landed). Surfaced 2026-06-25 (task #25); shared-layer blocker resolved 2026-07-03 (task #26).
- [ ] **AC + GC `DEPLOYMENT_ENVIRONMENT` hardcoded `development` in the prod base configmap (browser-client-join Task #25 / #26 → prod OTel graduation)**: `infra/services/ac-service/configmap.yaml` and `infra/services/gc-service/configmap.yaml` both set `DEPLOYMENT_ENVIRONMENT: "development"` in the prod base; the Kind overlay only flips `OTEL_ENABLED` to `true`. OTel is dev-only + default-off (`OTEL_ENABLED=false` in base) today, so zero runtime impact — but once prod OTel graduates (`OTEL_ENABLED=true` in a prod overlay), prod telemetry would be mistagged `deployment.environment=development` for BOTH services. **Action**: prod OTel graduation MUST overlay-patch `DEPLOYMENT_ENVIRONMENT` (e.g. `production`) for every service that lands this pattern (AC done, GC done as of task #26; MC/MH will add the same footgun when #6/#27 land — carry this caveat forward to those tasks too). Owner: operations. Surfaced 2026-06-25 (task #25) by @operations (Gate-1 point 4); carried forward 2026-07-03 (task #26). **Update 2026-07-05 (task #6)**: MC now also ships `DEPLOYMENT_ENVIRONMENT: "development"` in its prod base (`infra/services/mc-service/configmap.yaml`) with the Kind overlay flipping only `OTEL_ENABLED` — so the same prod-mistag footgun now applies to MC. The prod-overlay `DEPLOYMENT_ENVIRONMENT` patch must cover ac + gc + mc (and mh once #27's base carries it). **Update 2026-08-28 (env-config guard devloop)**: MH's base no longer carries `DEPLOYMENT_ENVIRONMENT` — it was one of four orphan keys removed from `infra/services/mh-service/configmap.yaml` (referenced by neither MH deployment; see the MH Kind-overlay entry below). So the prod-overlay patch list is **ac + gc + mc** today, and the MH caveat re-attaches at the moment MH's keys are re-added, not before — whoever performs that re-add owns adding MH to this list in the same change.
- [ ] **TS metric emission policy — Case A vs Case B (ADR-0033 follow-up)**: ADR-0033 (Polyglot Validation Pipeline) is silent on whether the TypeScript client emits its own application metrics (Case B — browser RUM / OpenTelemetry web SDK to a collector) or is purely a metrics consumer that reports server-side via API (Case A — server emits, no TS-side metric guards needed). The decision drives whether `validate-metric-coverage.sh` and `validate-application-metrics.sh` need to extend to TS file globs and whether ADR-0032's presence-guard pattern generalizes to TS. (The former sub-question "whether `lang/ts/changed.sh` should treat metric-emission sites specially" is moot as of 2026-08-20 — `changed.sh` was retired, ADR-0033 §2/§3.) **Action**: needs its own ADR or design discussion before client telemetry tasks (R-19, R-24, R-25, R-26, R-27 in browser-client-join story) land. Surfaced 2026-05-06 by observability in ADR-0033 Round 1.
- [ ] **GC `otel_collector_endpoint` is a BARE BASE — INFRA-OTEL (#28 / R-59) must not bake in the suffix (browser-client-join Task #10 → #28 contract)**: task #10's telemetry forwarder takes `OTEL_COLLECTOR_ENDPOINT` as a bare base (scheme+host+port, no path, no trailing slash, e.g. `http://otel-collector.default.svc.cluster.local:4318`) and appends the per-signal suffix `/v1/metrics` or `/v1/traces` itself (GC owns the suffix; one config key serves both signals). The story's R-59 text (line ~407) currently shows the GC env value in templated **suffixed** form (`…:4318/v1/{metrics,traces}`). **Hazard**: if INFRA-OTEL populates the GC configmap with the suffixed form AND GC also appends, every forward becomes `…/v1/metrics/v1/metrics` → collector 404 → **502 on every telemetry request** — silent in unit tests, breaks only in the live cluster. **Action for #28**: populate the GC `OTEL_COLLECTOR_ENDPOINT` configmap value with the BARE base (no `/v1/...`), and reconcile the story's R-59 text to match. Contract is documented on the `Config::otel_collector_endpoint` field doc-comment + `DEFAULT_OTEL_COLLECTOR_ENDPOINT` const + forwarder module doc. Distinct from R-55's `otel_endpoint` (the gRPC `:4317` span-export endpoint) — different port/purpose. Surfaced 2026-06-21 during task #10 (GC telemetry proxy) by @implementer + @operations. Owner: infrastructure (#28/INFRA-OTEL), reconcile with operations.

They were orphans — present in the ConfigMap but referenced by neither `mh-0-deployment.yaml` nor `mh-1-deployment.yaml`, so no MH pod ever received them, and an operator setting `OTEL_SAMPLE_RATE` mid-incident would have seen nothing change. - [ ] **MH Kind-overlay `OTEL_ENABLED=true` patch + MH→otel-collector egress NetworkPolicy rule deferred (browser-client-join Task #27 → MH R-55 dev enablement)**: Task #27 wired MH's `init_otel` + the four ConfigMap keys (mirroring AC task #25's shape — see `infra/services/ac-service/configmap.yaml` + `infra/kubernetes/overlays/kind/services/ac-service/configmap-otel-patch.yaml`), but deliberately did NOT add MH's counterpart of the two pieces AC also shipped: (a) a Kind-overlay strategic-merge patch flipping `OTEL_ENABLED` to `"true"` for dev (`infra/kubernetes/overlays/kind/services/mh-service/` has no `configmap-otel-patch.yaml` today), and (b) an MH-egress → otel-collector:4317 rule in `infra/services/mh-service/network-policy.yaml` (today's egress list covers GC/MC/AC/DNS only). Task #27's own instructions scoped only the base ConfigMap; the overlay+netpol pair was called out as likely-in-scope-for-AC-only during planning and confirmed out of scope at Gate 1. **Hazard if skipped when someone else flips `OTEL_ENABLED=true`**: `init_otel`'s eager collector probe (fail-hard at init, `crates/common/src/observability/otel.rs`) would CrashLoop the MH pod on every restart until the egress rule is added — default-deny NetworkPolicy blocks the OTLP-gRPC connect. **Action**: add both pieces together (never `OTEL_ENABLED=true` without the egress rule already merged) once MC's R-57/R-55 (task #6) and GC's R-55 (task #26) reach the same state, so dev-cluster OTel enablement lands once, consistently, across all four services rather than piecemeal per-service Kind drift. Owner: media-handler + operations. Surfaced 2026-07-03 (task #27) per Gate-1 directive 5 (operations + observability). **Update 2026-07-05 (task #6)**: MC's counterpart pair LANDED — `infra/kubernetes/overlays/kind/services/mc-service/configmap-otel-patch.yaml` (flips `OTEL_ENABLED=true` for dev, wired into that overlay's `kustomization.yaml`) + the MC→otel-collector `:4317` egress rule in `infra/services/mc-service/network-policy.yaml`. So AC (#25) and MC (#6) now both ship the full base+overlay+netpol trio; MH (#27) remains the only service missing the overlay patch + egress rule (this entry's original gap stands for MH). **Update 2026-08-28 (env-config guard devloop) — MH's four base ConfigMap keys were REMOVED, so this entry is now the sole home of the MH OTel re-add recipe.** Why removed rather than kept: they were referenced by neither MH deployment (per-key `configMapKeyRef`, no `envFrom`), so they were never delivered to a pod — misleading rather than merely dead. Behaviourally a no-op: `crates/mh-service/src/config.rs` reads all four as optional-with-defaults identical to the removed values, pinned by `test_otel_defaults` (OTel off by default). Surfaced by the `dt-guard env-config` coverage fix, which had never checked mh-service at all.
  **The four keys, verbatim, for `infra/services/mh-service/configmap.yaml` `data:`** — `OTEL_ENABLED: "false"`, `OTLP_ENDPOINT: "http://otel-collector.dark-tower:4317"`, `OTEL_SAMPLE_RATE: "1.0"`, `DEPLOYMENT_ENVIRONMENT: "development"`.
  **Semantics that would otherwise be lost with the deleted comment block**: (a) enablement is an EXPLICIT BOOLEAN `OTEL_ENABLED`, NOT presence of `OTLP_ENDPOINT` — a populated endpoint with `OTEL_ENABLED` unset/false stays OFF (`init_otel` is never called, no layer composed); (b) the `http://` scheme is REQUIRED on `OTLP_ENDPOINT` (parsed via tonic `Endpoint::from_shared`; a bare `host:port` fails at init); (c) `OTEL_SAMPLE_RATE` is a head-sampling ratio in `[0.0, 1.0]` whose range has a SINGLE owner, `init_otel` — MH's config side is PARSE-ONLY and must not regrow a range check; (d) `DEPLOYMENT_ENVIRONMENT` is the ADR-0011 `deployment.environment` resource attribute, and the prod-mistag caveat in the AC+GC entry above re-attaches to MH on re-add.
  **Action — three ORDERED steps. Do not reorder; each step's hazard is created by the one before it.** **(1)** Re-add the four keys to the base ConfigMap **AND** add the four matching `configMapKeyRef` env entries to BOTH `mh-0-deployment.yaml` and `mh-1-deployment.yaml` (mirror `infra/services/ac-service/statefulset.yaml`'s OTel block). Re-adding the ConfigMap keys ALONE is inert and recreates exactly the orphan state this update removed — that omission IS the original bug. **(2)** Add the MH→otel-collector `:4317` egress rule to `infra/services/mh-service/network-policy.yaml` (today's egress allowlist covers GC/MC/AC/DNS only). **(3)** ONLY THEN create `infra/kubernetes/overlays/kind/services/mh-service/configmap-otel-patch.yaml` flipping `OTEL_ENABLED: "true"` **and** add the `patches:` stanza to that overlay's `kustomization.yaml` — it has NO `patches:` stanza at all today, unlike ac/gc/mc, so creating the patch file alone is a silent no-op. **Two distinct CrashLoops guard this order.** Doing (3) before (1): a Kustomize strategic merge on a ConfigMap is ADDITIVE, so patching `OTEL_ENABLED: "true"` against a base that lacks the key does NOT error — it CREATES it, MH starts with `OTEL_ENABLED=true` and an empty `OTLP_ENDPOINT`, and trips the cross-field validation in `crates/mh-service/src/config.rs` (`InvalidOtelConfig`: "OTEL_ENABLED=true requires a non-empty OTLP_ENDPOINT"). Doing (3) before (2): `init_otel` probes the collector eagerly and FAILS HARD at startup, and default-deny NetworkPolicy blocks the OTLP-gRPC connect, so the pod CrashLoops on every restart. AC (#25) and MC (#6) are the two worked examples of the completed trio.

All six reintroduced atop the new `mc_participant_mh_status_total{state}` metric: (a) the metric itself (`observability/metrics.rs` `record_participant_mh_status`); (b) `MCMediaConnectionAllFailed` alert in `infra/docker/prometheus/rules/mc-alerts.yaml` — observability-specced failed-share ratio (`sum(rate(…{state="failed"}[5m])) / sum(rate(…{state=~"connected|failed"}[5m])) > 0.80` + `> 0.1/s` volume floor, `for: 5m`, `severity: page`), replacing the deleted flat all-fail counter; (c) `mc-incident-response.md` Scenario 11 (Media Connection Failures) + TOC + Version History (operations-authored); (d) `mc-overview.json` "Client-Reported MH Status by State" + "Dropped Client MH Status" panels; (e) post-deploy acceptance gates in `mc-deployment.md` + `mh-deployment.md` (operations-authored; `mh-deployment.md` cross-boundary, `Approved-Cross-Boundary: operations` trailer); (f) catalog entry in `docs/observability/metrics/mc-service.md`. **Deviation (owner-approved):** the (e) gate is a **failed-share ratio `< 0.20`** (with `clamp_min` no-traffic guard) over 30m/2h/24h, NOT the literal `{state="failed"} increase == 0` this breadcrumb first specified — that text predated the redesign and would false-fail every deploy (the per-MH counter increments `failed` on any single client hiccup); observability (metric/alert taxonomy owner) reformulated it, consistent with the alert's `> 0.80` paging line.
- [ ] **R-26 bounded client join LOGS — emission deferred from Task #14 (browser-client-join, PATH B)**: Task #14 wired the five `dt_client_*` join METRICS (R-24/R-25) at their `MeetingSession.join` / `MediaTransport.connectAll` emission sites, but the R-26 bounded join LOGS (`logJoinEvent`/`JoinEvent`/`JoinLogRecord`, scaffolded in `packages/sdk-core/src/telemetry/logger.ts`) are NOT emitted yet. **Root cause (@observability §3, corrected framing)**: the 7 R-26 events span the WHOLE join lifecycle — `join.started`/`signup_complete`/`gc_token_received` all fire inside `MeetingSession.join` BEFORE `SignalingClient` even exists — but the only `dt_client.join` span is created INSIDE `SignalingClient.join`, so it covers only the signaling sub-portion. There is therefore NO single consistent `trace_id` across all 7 events. A read-only getter on SignalingClient does NOT fix this (the early MeetingSession events still have no span, and correlating some events but not others is worse than a clean defer). The only correct fix is the DESIGN DECISION to relocate the `dt_client.join` ROOT span to `MeetingSession.join` (signaling span becomes a child), which touches task #13 span semantics and warrants its own planning. The metric labels' `meeting_id_hash` (SHA-256 via `crypto.subtle.digest`) computed in THIS task is reusable for the log record, so the follow-up is span/trace_id wiring only. **Action**: (a) relocate the `dt_client.join` root span to `MeetingSession.join` so one trace_id roots the whole join; (b) make SignalingClient's signaling span a child; (c) emit `logJoinEvent` across all 7 `JoinEvent` states from `MeetingSession.join`. Owner: client (with observability). Surfaced 2026-06-29 (task #14); deferral accepted by @observability, scope split locked by @team-lead at Gate-1 (PATH B).

- [x] **MC runbook missing join scenarios**: Added Scenario 8 (join failures), Scenario 9 (WebTransport rejections), and Scenario 10 (JWT validation failures) to `mc-incident-response.md`. Anchors match `mc-alerts.yaml` runbook_url references. Fixed in task 17.
- [ ] **Cross-service status label inconsistency**: GC uses `status="error"` for failures (HTTP convention) while MC uses `status="failure"` (binary convention). Both are internally consistent. Standardize if/when a cross-service alerting layer is added.
- [ ] **Metric label-value assertion convention (MC service)**: Current convention (per `crates/mc-service/src/observability/metrics.rs:468-473`, ADR-0002) tests exercise recorder functions for coverage but do NOT assert emitted label values. `test_prometheus_metrics_endpoint_integration` (line 767) installs a `metrics_util::DebuggingRecorder` but only asserts "snapshot not empty + len >= 7" — specific label values (e.g., `mc_media_connection_failures_total{all_failed="true"}`) are unverified. This means alerting labels are load-bearing but untested.

    **Recommended approach** (refined 2026-04-17 after R-32 devloop):

    1. **Use `metrics::with_local_recorder` (metrics v0.24+)** to install a `DebuggingRecorder` on the current thread only. Eliminates the global-recorder contention that previously required `serial_test`. Check `Cargo.toml` for metrics crate version before starting; upgrade if needed.

    2. **Discipline: assert from production-driven tests, never from bare helper invocations.** Calling `record_foo()` directly and asserting the counter moved tests the `metrics` crate, not our code. The valuable form drives the real code path (actor handler, gRPC service method, WebTransport message branch) and asserts the metric as a side effect. Without this discipline, option (a) degenerates into tautology and catches only the typo of the metric NAME, not wrong-branch or wrong-label bugs.

    3. **Retrofit by example, not by debate.** Start with `mc_media_connection_failures_total{all_failed}` — it drives the `MCMediaConnectionAllFailed` P2 alert (`mc-alerts.yaml`), so a wrong label silently breaks paging. The existing `test_handle_media_connection_failed_*` tests in `connection.rs:834-893` already drive the real branch; adding `with_local_recorder` there is ~15 lines in one PR. That commit sets the convention by concrete example. Subsequent metrics retrofit incrementally as their call-site modules are touched.

    **Scope**: ~50 MC metric call sites. Same pattern extends to GC/MH (~30 more sites each). Alternative debate via `/debate` only if reviewers push back on the first retrofit PR. Original owner: MC metrics devloop (task 11 of the mh-quic-connection story).

- [ ] **`validate-metric-coverage.sh` source-scan regex is single-line, missing multi-line macro invocations**: The guard's grep-based source-site discovery (at `scripts/guards/simple/validate-metric-coverage.sh`) drops metric names whose `counter!/histogram!/gauge!` macro invocations span multiple lines. Observed during ADR-0032 Step 2: MH metric names `mh_errors_total`, `mh_grpc_requests_total`, `mh_mc_notifications_total`, `mh_token_refresh_failures_total` are all emitted in production (via `record_error`, `record_grpc_request`, `record_mc_notification`, the error branch of `record_token_refresh`) but the guard reports only 11 uncovered names instead of 15 — the 4 multi-line sites are not even counted as emissions. Worse failure mode than "uncovered": not-covered AND not-counted. Cross-service implication: AC/GC/MC are similarly under-counted; their true coverage gaps may be larger than the audit numbers at `docs/observability/metrics-coverage-audit-2026-04-20.md` suggest. Owned by observability. Resolution: upgrade the source-scan to a multi-line-aware parser (`syn` AST walk, or line-joining grep that preserves `counter!(` through the metric-name string literal). Small devloop scope.

- [ ] **ADR-0011 cardinality bounds — reframe as typed-label-source discipline (weak form)**: Today ADR-0011 documents per-label numeric cardinality bounds; this is symptom-treating. The actual discipline you want is "label values come from a typed value-set, not free-form strings" — that's what catches drift like the AC `clock_skew` finding (production emitted a 6th `error_category` value while the catalog claimed 5; the call site used `Some("clock_skew")` raw string instead of an `ErrorCategory` enum variant, so neither compiler nor reviewer flagged it). Recommended rule: *Label values MUST come from one of: (a) a Rust enum variant whose `Display` impl is the wire form, (b) a named `pub const &'static str` constant, or (c) the bounded output of a documented normalization function. Free-form `String` and inline string literals are prohibited at metric call sites. Catalog documents the source type, not just the value list.* The numeric per-label bound stays as a backstop ("no label key exceeds 50 values without explicit ADR amendment"), secondary to the typed-source rule. Migration cost: nontrivial — many existing wrappers take `&str` (e.g., `record_token_validation(status: &str, error_category: Option<&str>)`); typed-labels migration converts them to `record_token_validation(status: Status, error_category: Option<ErrorCategory>)`. ~100+ call sites across 4 services. Phasing: amend ADR-0011 with the rule + mark current state as drift-tracked-tech-debt; file a typed-labels migration as a user story; drain it service-by-service the same way ADR-0032 is doing for metric-test coverage. Add a guard (extend `scripts/guards/simple/validate-application-metrics.sh`) to flag raw-string label literals at metric call sites. Where strong form breaks: HTTP `path` post-normalization (bounded by router output), DB `table` (bounded by schema), federated `service_type` (open-ended) — these get the (c) clause exception. Surfaced during ADR-0032 Step 4 clock_skew disposition discussion. Owner: observability. Phase after Step 5 (GC backfill).

- [ ] **MC Redis-class metrics not driven by tests through the production path**: 16 `record_redis_latency` emission sites + 2 `record_fenced_out("stale_generation")` sites in `crates/mc-service/src/redis/client.rs` are not exercised by any test that drives the production code path. The 16 latency sites split 4/4/4/2/2 across operations `get`/`hset`/`eval`/`incr`/`del` (the `set` example listed in the `metrics.rs:163-165` doc-comment is NOT actually emitted in production). The guard at `scripts/guards/simple/validate-metric-coverage.sh` is satisfied by wrapper-invocation tests at `crates/mc-service/tests/redis_metrics_integration.rs`, but those tests do not exercise `FencedRedisClient::{get,hset,eval,incr,del}` themselves. Existing `crates/mc-service/src/redis/client.rs::tests` is pure-data serde — never instantiates `FencedRedisClient`. Two paths to close the gap: (a) introduce a `redis::aio::ConnectionManager` trait abstraction in `redis/client.rs` allowing fake injection in `client.rs::tests`, or (b) add a real Redis fixture in `tests/`. Surfaced during ADR-0032 Step 3.

- [ ] **GC MH-health-checker loop has no registered-handlers gauge refresh (asymmetric with controller path)**: The MeetingController health-checker loop closure (`crates/gc-service/src/tasks/health_checker.rs:88-95`) calls `refresh_controller_metrics()` after a successful `mark_stale_controllers_unhealthy`, which updates the `gc_registered_controllers` gauge via `metrics::update_registered_controller_gauges("meeting", …)`. The MediaHandler equivalent (`crates/gc-service/src/tasks/mh_health_checker.rs:54-56`) closure calls only `mark_stale_handlers_unhealthy` and refreshes **no** gauge — there is no `gc_registered_handlers`-equivalent maintained on the MH staleness path. Consequence: a registered-MH-by-status gauge is either absent entirely or not kept fresh when handlers transition to unhealthy via the health-checker, so MH fleet-health dashboards/alerts cannot rely on a per-status handler gauge the way the controller side can. Pre-existing production behavior — NOT introduced by the 2026-06-20 health-checker test-flakiness fix; surfaced by observability while reviewing that test-only diff (devloop `2026-06-20-harden-flaky-health-checker-tests`). Disposition: (a) add a `refresh_handler_metrics()` mirror + `gc_registered_handlers{status}` gauge (catalog entry in `docs/observability/metrics/gc-service.md`) and wire it into the MH loop closure, or (b) if MH fleet status is intentionally surfaced elsewhere, document why the asymmetry is deliberate and close. Owner: global-controller + observability.

Used in AC Step 4 by `key_rotation_metrics_integration.rs` (failure-path gauge adjacency on `ac_signing_key_age_days`, `ac_active_signing_keys`, `ac_key_rotation_last_success_timestamp`) and `token_validation_integration.rs` (happy-path counter adjacency on `ac_token_validations_total`). Histogram form has a drain-on-read caveat documented in the doc-comment (use BEFORE any `assert_observation_count*` on the same name+labels, or take a fresh snapshot). 13 module tests in `testing.rs` prove the API end-to-end including the drain trap.

Production has exactly TWO call sites — both `("error", Some("clock_skew"))` from `crypto/mod.rs:284` (`verify_jwt`) and `:439` (`verify_user_jwt`). The 4 other label combos (`success`, `error+authentication`, `error+authorization`, `error+cryptographic`, `error+internal`) shown in the in-src cluster test are forward-looking reservations. Disposition options when Phase 4 validation endpoints land: (a) wire the wrapper at the validation handler error branches and update the cluster test to drive each error_category from a real handler; (b) if Phase 4 ships without per-category fidelity, drop the `#[allow]` and narrow the wrapper signature to `record_token_validation_clock_skew()`. NOT VALID` seam (`break_auth_events_inserts`) or `DROP TABLE auth_events` seam (`break_auth_events_table`): `key_generated` (`key_management_service.rs:96`), `key_rotated` (`key_management_service.rs:166`), `key_expired` (`key_management_service.rs:384`), `service_registered` (`registration_service.rs:78`), `scopes_updated` (`registration_service.rs:124`), `service_deactivated` (`registration_service.rs:158`), `user_registered` (`user_service.rs:144`), `service_token_failed` (`token_service.rs:105`), `service_token_issued` (`token_service.rs:172`), and the parameterized site at `token_service.rs:362` covered via both `user_login` (success branch) and `user_login_failed` (failure branch — caller is `log_user_auth_event` driven from `issue_user_token`).
