  Two further layers surfaced during Gate 2 of that same devloop, both in the **review process itself** rather than in the artefact under review: a **gate verdict read through a proxy** (a shell wrapper's own `echo $?` reported as if it were the pipeline's verdict — the pipeline's actual token is `TOTAL_RESULT=`, emitted at `scripts/layer-all.sh:168`; self-caught), and **stale prose in a live-read doc** (`.claude/skills/devloop/SKILL.md:379` asserting fail-fast, entry above).

  **Self-caught: 3 of 5** — the verification fixture and the gate-verdict proxy by the people who made them, and the test fixture by the implementer running it (though catchable only because @test's T-7 mandated asserting the slug *value*; count it as self-caught in who found it, reviewer-mandated in what made it findable). The production controls were caught by reviewers, and the stale prose by the implementer contradicting the Lead who acted on it. **An entry in which every instance is someone else's is a rule about other people**, and this set is not that.

  **The `SKILL.md:379` instance is the one that upgrades clause 4 from hygiene to a defect class.** Every other prose-decoupling instance here was "nothing failed, the sentence just stopped being true" — tidiness. That one produced a **wrong decision by someone acting on it**: a Lead read a red Layer 2 as meaning layers 3-7 had not run. Stale prose in a doc a fresh `claude -p` live-reads per task is not a documentation nit; it is a control that silently inverted.

  **CONCRETE PROPOSAL — a guard, not a review habit** (@semantic-guard's, adopted as this entry's deliverable): *does every identifier this prose names in backticks actually appear in the artifact it describes?* Mechanical, cheap, and it would have caught **both** instances below — the `:379` fail-fast claim and the `LAYER_ALL_RC` token that replaced it. Same shape as `scripts/guards/simple/validate-slug-class-sync.sh`, which this devloop already built to pin the slug class across three files, so the pattern and its self-test convention already exist in-tree. Scope it to the skills and ADRs a live-read consumer depends on; start with `.claude/skills/*/SKILL.md`.
  **RE-DEFER 2026-08-21 (fast-fail-guards devloop):** this entry's class fired again — the devloop reconciled a stale SKILL.md restatement (`:387` "does NOT stop at the first red layer", now conditional) down to a citation of ADR-0033 §4, exactly the backticked-identifier-in-prose vs artifact drift this guard would catch. Still task-sized (a new `dt-guard` subcommand); re-deferred, with the SKILL.md/ADR mirrors landed consistent by hand this pass.

  **The worked instance that justifies it.** `SKILL.md:379` claimed `layer-all.sh` "stops on first failure". The fix replaced it with a pointer to read "`LAYER_ALL_RC`" — **an identifier the pipeline never emits.** Its entire provenance was a Lead's private shell wrapper (`./scripts/layer-all.sh > log; echo "LAYER_ALL_RC=$?"`), used in Gate-2 messages as if it were the pipeline's own token, then propagated in good faith into the live-read doc. **The sentence written to prevent the defect instructed the reader to reproduce it.** Caught by `grep`, not by reading.

  **PRIMING DOES NOT HELP — this is the claim the evidence supports, and it retires the softer ones.** That instance was introduced by the fix for this exact defect, written by the implementer who had diagnosed the class, instructed by a Lead who had just been burned by it, and reviewed by the reviewer whose entire lens is this class. Three people at maximum attention, none of whom caught it by reading; it fell out of a `grep`. "Be careful", "review closely" and "more eyes" are each refuted by that single data point. **The remedy is mechanical checks, not attention.**

  **The transferable diagnostics**, in preference to "be careful": a green test proves **a** path ran, not that **the** path ran; and **assertion strength, not case count**, is what separates real cases from copies of one.

  **Evidence**: six defects in one devloop's planning, each correct in mechanism and wrong in placement, scope, property or timing — and every one found by *executing* the path rather than reading its description. Clause 4 alone caught four stale-prose instances in a single changeset. Four reviewers independently audited their own earlier conclusions and found them wanting, which is what makes the failure mode structural rather than a matter of care. The transferable lesson is not "be careful" — it is "have someone run the path."

- [x] **CLOSED 2026-08-17 — [ADR-0035 · Group 3 — live-read hazard] `.claude/skills/devloop/SKILL.md:379` claimed `layer-all.sh` "stops on first failure" and marked it ENFORCED; the pipeline deliberately does the opposite.** Fixed in `docs/devloop-outputs/2026-08-17-manifest-single-home/` — the false clause and its ENFORCED label are deleted, and `:383`'s ADR pointer is scoped with what §4 actually says. **The pipeline was NOT changed and must not be**; the skill was the stale artifact. Evidence retained below because the diagnosis outlives the four-word fix.

  *Closed by Lead reversal, recorded because the reversal is the useful part.* The fix was first deferred on a Lead scope ruling, then reversed after @semantic-guard escalated and @operations asked for a re-rule. Both premises had moved: the file had by then been edited twice in the same changeset (13 insertions / 3 deletions), and it was in that devloop's Cross-Boundary Classification table as Mine (Minor-judgment, owner `operations`) — so "not otherwise in scope" was false. The deferral burden (fix-now-cost > fix-later-cost + tracking) was never attempted by anyone, which is what tipped it from a disagreement to an unmet test.

  **Evidence, all checkable.** `SKILL.md:379`: *"**ENFORCED** — single command runs all seven layers in order, stops on first failure."* `scripts/layer-all.sh:107` does the opposite **and says so at the code**: `set +e/-e` around the pipeline *"so a non-zero layer doesn't abort the loop (**replaces the old `if ! …` guard**)"* — i.e. the behaviour changed deliberately and the skill was never updated. **ADR-0033 §4 says only "Runs `layer1.sh` through `layer7.sh` **sequentially**" and contains no fail-fast language anywhere** (`grep -rniE "stops? on first|fail.?fast|first failure|abort the loop"` over the ADR → zero hits).

  **Code and ADR agree; the skill is the outlier.** Two further parts of §4 actively **require** running on: the mandated *"final summary table: layer, status, duration"* cannot be emitted from an early stop, and the always-run budget check sums layers **3 + 6**, vacuously 0 on any run that stopped at layer 2.

  **The trade-off is DECIDED, recorded so the next reader sees a decision rather than a surprise.** Measured 2026-08-17: Layer 2 reded at 2s and the pipeline still spent **873s** on Layer 7. That cost is accepted — running on is why a single run reported "only layer 2 is red, everything substantive passed", instead of the fmt fix landing and a Layer 4 failure surfacing on attempt 2, with Gate 2 attempts capped at 3. **No ADR amendment; the pipeline is correct as built.**

  > **AMENDMENT (2026-08-21, fast-fail-guards devloop) — this "must not be changed / no ADR amendment" decision is REVERSED for INTERACTIVE runs, and ADR-0033 §4 IS now amended.** The pipeline now fail-fasts for interactive runs (stops at the first red layer) while KEEPING run-all for unattended callers, config-driven via `DEVLOOP_FAIL_FAST`. **Answering the counter-argument recorded above** ("running on conserves the 3-attempt budget by reporting everything in one pass"): that value accrues to callers who do NOT re-run interactively — the story runner and CI — and Change 2 PRESERVES run-all for exactly those (unattended detection + the story-close gate forces `DEVLOOP_FAIL_FAST=0`), so the recorded value is kept where it applies. For interactive runs a human is present and re-runs after fixing anyway. And the COST BASIS changed after this entry: post-2026-08-20 Layer 7 is unconditional (~10-15 min cluster bring-up every run) and every layer runs every language, so "running on" now costs far more than the 873s measured here, on every interactive iteration — the earlier measurement predates that. The honesty invariant that made "must not be changed" attractive is preserved structurally: a green run is byte-identical under both modes (nothing to stop at), and un-run layers render `NOT-RUN` (never aggregating, never exit-0), so fail-fast cannot forge a `GATE2=PASS`. So the interactive/unattended split reconciles the 2026-08-17 concern with the new cost reality rather than discarding it. The SKILL.md live-read hazard this entry originally fixed is respected: SKILL.md now CITES ADR-0033 §4 rather than restating the (now-conditional) invariant.

  **Why it is worth fixing rather than tolerating.** `SKILL.md` is live-read by a fresh `claude -p` per task, so line 379 is read by every Lead running Gate 2. It caused a real misjudgment on 2026-08-17: **this devloop's Lead (`@team-lead`)** read a red Layer 2 as meaning layers 3-7 had not run, and attributed the fail-fast claim to ADR-0033 §4 — plausible because `SKILL.md:383`, four lines below, points at *"ADR-0033 §4 for the wrapper contract"*. The claim was corrected only because the implementer read §4 rather than transcribing the summary. **The layout invites the conflation; this is not carelessness.** The adjacent citation is part of why the false belief was durable, and whoever fixes line 379 should check whether that pointer needs scoping too.

  **Scope note**: a *separate* citation error in the same session (a wrapper's `echo` exit status read as the pipeline's verdict) is **not** attributable to line 379 and is recorded only in the review-question entry's pattern set. Folding it in here would make line 379 look responsible for a failure it did not cause.

  **Deliberately not fixed in `2026-08-17-manifest-single-home`**: that devloop had one approved, narrowly-scoped departure into `SKILL.md`, and a second edit would widen a live-read exposure accepted on the grounds that the first was two sentences inside one bullet.

- [ ] **[ADR-0035 · Group 3 — live-read hazard]** **/devloop: make headless the only described mode** — already tracked under §Developer Experience; listed here for the batch.
- [ ] **A story containing an escalated task cannot be resumed at all — the escalate → rerun recovery loop is unreachable through the runner (2026-08-13)**: Found at Gate 3 of devloop `docs/devloop-outputs/2026-08-13-run-story-seams-and-hermetic-tests/main.md`; ruled **out of scope** for that task by @team-lead (pre-existing; not covered by R-1..R-5; the fix is a state-machine change deserving its own review). Not a resume *bug* — resume-after-escalation is **terminal in practice** for a lane both ADR-0035 and the story text treat as retryable ("rerunning is the documented recovery for several lanes"). `crates/dt-story/src/engine.rs:48`/`:87` implement escalation-is-retryable (candidates filter `Pending | Escalated`; a selected escalated task is reopened, `reopened = task.status == Status::Escalated`); the runner cannot reach it.

  **Mechanism**: `dt-story next` **rewrites the story file** when it reopens an escalated task (documented at `crates/dt-story/src/main.rs:20-22`, "status back to pending, escalation cleared, **file rewritten**"). `scripts/workflow/run-story.sh` calls `next` **before** its fresh-start clean-tree check, so the runner's own manifest write makes the tree dirty and its own guard then refuses. Reproduced end-to-end against a fixture with task 1 `escalated` and task 2 `pending, deps: [1]`:

  ```
  dt-story: reopening escalated task 1 for retry
  STORY_RUN: dirty tree and no resumable devloop for task 1
  ```

  Runner exits **2**. Every subsequent invocation reproduces it, so the story is stuck: the manifest is already reopened, the tree is already dirty, and there is no resumable devloop pointer to satisfy the alternative branch.

  **Candidate fix** (not applied): commit the reopen immediately, exactly as the audit-remediation path in the same file already does for `add-task` (`git add "$STORY_FILE"` + `git commit` before continuing). The precedent and the shape both exist; what it needs is review of the manifest-write ordering between `dt-story next`'s rewrite and the clean-tree guard, which straddles @protocol's `dt-story` contract and infrastructure's runner lane. Owner: infrastructure, paired with protocol.

  **Found by a test case that was requested but never written.** The suite's `E4` was a `# E4:` heading followed by a fixture restore and nothing else — no `e4-` label existed anywhere in the file, and the label set ran `e1 e2 e3 e5 e6 e7`. It also encoded a wrong model ("the dep never completes because it is escalated"). So the case that would have caught this read as coverage in review and provided none. That is the argument for the **"a comment is not a case"** rule added to `scripts/workflow/run-story.test.sh`'s header: every `# En:` heading must be followed by assertions carrying that label, with the grep to check it. The written-out case (`E4b`) was **withdrawn rather than rewritten to assert the broken behaviour** — pinning a defect as correct is the same phantom-case failure in different clothes — and is recorded in that file's NOT-COVERED block with this measurement.

- [x] **[ADR-0035 · Group 2]** **devloop.sh: run an arbitrary command in the prepared container** — done 2026-08-12. `devloop.sh <slug> [base-branch] -- <command...>` runs the command instead of the interactive claude attach, exports `DEVLOOP_SLUG` (which run-story's AUTH-EXPIRED lane now interpolates into its `--refresh-creds <slug>` recovery line), reports commits, and exits with the command's own code rather than falling through to the interactive post-session menu, whose `read -p` would block an unattended run on a keypress nobody is there to give. Built first as a `--run-story` flag and replaced with the general form — encoding run-story's option set inside devloop.sh would be a second copy of that interface, drifting from the one it describes. Unattended story run: `devloop.sh <slug> -- scripts/workflow/run-story.sh <story> --stop-after=1`.

## Input Validation

- [ ] **AC: Validate displayName at registration**: `users.display_name` is `NOT NULL` but nothing rejects empty or degenerate values at the registration boundary, so `""` (or `" "`, or a single character) flows legally through AC's fail-closed meeting-token minting (`resolve_meeting_display_name` — which correctly refuses only *missing* users, not empty names) into MC's roster broadcast and renders blank in every participant's UI. Non-empty alone is likely insufficient: at implementation time, determine a reasonable minimum length (post-trim; consider what the roster UX and abuse surface actually need — e.g. ≥2 visible characters), enforce it in the registration handler with a clear 400, mirror the constraint client-side in the SignUp form for immediate feedback, and add the negative registration test. Surfaced 2026-08-06 while tracing display-name plumbing for story task #65 (MC drops the claim; the empty-string edge is AC's half of the invariant). Owner: auth-controller.

## Media Path Obligations (ADR-0036 frame v2)

Constraints the `media-protocol` codec **cannot** enforce, recorded here so they do not depend on a
task prompt surviving a re-scope. Each is also stated in the crate's rustdoc so it travels with the
wire format; this file is the copy that survives a prompt rewrite. Landed 2026-08-31 with the frame
header v2 codec (`docs/devloop-outputs/2026-08-31-media-frame-v2-codec/`).


- [ ] **`crates/dt-guard/**` has no derivable owner from the path** — owner: infrastructure. The Lead's ADR-0034 ruling in the `2026-09-02-frame-v2-cross-language-vectors` devloop established infrastructure as owner, but that ruling lives only in a devloop output. **Do NOT fix by adding a key to `scripts/guards/simple/cross-boundary-ownership.yaml`** — that file is a GSA mirror, `gsa_sync.rs:196-205` rejects stray keys, and populating all five mirrors would wrongly declare dt-guard a Guarded Shared Area (it meets no ADR-0024 §6.4 criterion). The gap is the absence of a general non-GSA path→specialist map; `cross_boundary_scope.rs` reads owners from `main.md` tables, not from any manifest. Cheap interim: name `crates/dt-guard/**` → infrastructure in CLAUDE.md's Specialists table. Proper fix is task-sized and needs a micro-debate on whether a second, non-GSA ownership manifest should exist. *(Wording supplied by @infrastructure, who retracted their own earlier recommendation after reading the manifest; filed by protocol so one pen owns the entry. Raised because it is why task 8 had to escalate an owner ruling at all, and it recurs on the next dt-guard change. Note the derivation does exist one-directionally today — `docs/specialist-knowledge/infrastructure/INDEX.md:30` lists `crates/dt-guard/` under Code Locations — but it is discoverable from infrastructure, not from the path.)*

- [ ] <!-- frame-vectors-ts-leg --> **The cross-language frame-vector gate has only its Rust leg; the TypeScript leg lands at story task 15** (owner: client + protocol; recorded by protocol at task 8, required by `scripts/guards/simple/validate-frame-vectors.sh` check g14). `proto/test-vectors/frame-v2.vectors.json` declares `gated_by: {rust: true, typescript: false}` and `cross_language_property_established: false`, so **the file never claims a green it does not have**. Until the TS codec exists, the AAD span, the signed range and the detached-tag split are gated by the non-production Rust reference on one side and by **nothing** on the other — they are *authored and self-consistent*, not *cross-validated*. **ADR-0036 Assumption 4 is therefore NOT discharged**: authoring the insider-forgery row is not running the test, which needs a second implementation to reject the frame, and the ADR states the header "is not frozen in code until they have run". The guard prints a non-suppressible `WARN frame-vectors:` banner on every run naming task 15 as the closer, and hard-fails on drift in either direction — flipping `gated_by.typescript` to `true` without the conformance marker, or landing the marker while the flag still says false. **The HTML comment marker above is what g14 greps**, not this checkbox and not the prose, so `/close-story` ticking this `[x]` satisfies the guard rather than breaking it; moving or deleting the marker reds the guard with `todo-tracking-entry-missing`. Delete this entry when `gated_by.typescript` is `true`.

- [ ] **Receiver must validate `stream_id` against its own declared slots (§6)** (raised by @security, endorsed by @client). The relay region is 6 bytes per frame that a compromised media handler writes freely and that nobody authenticates — accepted by ADR-0036 §2, but bounded *only* if the receiver rejects a `stream_id` it never declared. Without the check a relay gains 16 free bits per frame and can place a frame in a slot the subscriber never assigned. Applies to **both** audio (datagram) and video (stream) receive, since the relay region is present in every frame regardless of transport. Target: SDK receive path, story tasks 15/19. Delete this entry when the check lands.
- [ ] **`decode_stream_frame` returning `Ok(None)` must be paired with a caller-side bound** (raised by @observability and @security). `Ok(None)` means "incomplete", carries no reject reason, and must not be counted as a drop — so a peer that opens a stream and stalls mid-frame is silent by construction, which CLAUDE.md's fail-loudly rule forbids. The caller must bound **both** buffered bytes per stream (`media_protocol::frame::MAX_FRAME_BYTES`) and time-to-completion, and **count the give-up**. The codec sees one buffer and has no notion of time or stream count, so it cannot enforce this. Two callers, not one: MH's video forward path (task 16, @media-handler has accepted ownership) **and the SDK's video-stream receive reader** (@client's catch — video is carried on unidirectional QUIC streams per §1, so the SDK is itself a `decode_stream_frame` caller and owes the identical bound). Note task 19 is *not* affected: audio is datagrams, hence `decode_datagram`, which has no `Ok(None)`.
- [ ] **dt-guard `pii_vocabulary.rs` CATEGORY_A does not contain `transmit_key` / `wrapped_key`** (raised by @observability, confirmed by @semantic-guard). A log line naming `wrapped_transmit_key` would report clean. Not needed inside `media-protocol` itself — that crate has no `tracing` dependency, so the name cannot be logged from it — but MH (task 16) and the SDK (task 19) do log. **@security's warning, which must travel with the fix**: do not use the `\b…\b` word-boundary idiom from `rust_pii.rs` / `metric_labels.rs`; it cannot see inside compound names (`\btoken\b` matches neither `userToken` nor `user_token`), so the vocabulary would land and report clean forever. The working idiom is segment equality, `crates/dt-guard/src/ts_retained_credentials.rs:198::segments()`. Owner: dt-guard tooling + security.
- [ ] **Neither fuzz workspace is compiled by the pipeline, and `cargo-fuzz` is absent from the container** (raised by @operations as a pair, not an instance; @test owns the structural fix as story task 23). `Cargo.toml:22-25` excludes **`crates/ac-service/fuzz`** *and* **`crates/media-protocol/fuzz`** from the workspace, so `scripts/layer-all.sh` compiles neither — and a fuzz target that does not build cannot fail. Separately, `cargo-fuzz` is not installed in the devloop container, so no fuzzing is executed anywhere in the pipeline. The fix is toolchain provisioning **and** workspace membership, not a wire-format change: cargo-fuzz requires its own workspace and a nightly toolchain, so the likely shape is a dedicated pipeline step that runs `cargo +nightly check --all-targets` in each excluded fuzz workspace, plus installing `cargo-fuzz` if targets are ever to be *run*. Naming only one of the two paths would leave the other to be rediscovered. Owner: test + infrastructure.
- [x] **LANDED (story task 8).** The rule and its escalation addressee are recorded in `proto/test-vectors/README.md`, including why it bites hardest on the extension grammar (the AAD-span rows are externally anchored; the TLV registry is ours alone and can prove internal consistency only). Original text: **A task-8 vector that contradicts a recorded ruling is a defect to escalate, not a precedence question** (raised by @security after @client had adopted the opposite rule during Gate-2 coordination). The cross-language vectors are the SSoT for Rust↔TypeScript agreement and rightly beat *prose* — but they are **generated from the Rust encoder and then frozen**, so a vector that disagrees with a recorded ruling means the Rust codec implemented that ruling wrongly and the freeze captured the error. Conforming the TypeScript codec to such a vector makes both implementations **consistently wrong with the drift guard green**, which is strictly worse than a divergence, because a divergence is loud and this is silent. The rule: vectors beat prose; a vector contradicting a ruling is escalated to protocol + security and is **not built against** in the meantime. This bites hardest on the **extension-grammar** rows: unlike the AAD-span rows, which are anchored externally against the SFrame specification, the TLV registry is ours alone — nothing outside this repository says what type `0x01` means or that its accepted value set is `0..=100` — so those vectors can prove internal consistency only, never correctness. Target: story tasks 8 and 15. Delete when task 8 lands the drift guard together with this escalation rule.
- [ ] **The reject-reason `{reason}` counter must label by entry point, and triage must consult `RejectReason::reachable_on_stream_carried_frames()` rather than infer media from a token name** (raised by @operations, accepted by @media-handler as owner). `rewrite_relay_region` is a fourth fallible entry point that MH calls on stream-carried video while re-framing, so `truncated` and `payload_length_exceeds_available` are reachable on the video path — from `rewrite_relay_region`, not `decode_stream_frame` (which maps a shortfall to the non-reject `Ok(None)`). On the video path those two tokens therefore signal an **MH-side re-framing defect in the handler's own stream reader, not a sender fault**, and `RejectReason::operator_meaning` forks on entry point. Consequences: (a) task 16's counter must carry an entry-point dimension so a `truncated` from `rewrite_relay_region` is not collapsed with a datagram-decode `truncated` and mis-attributed to the sender; (b) tasks 21/22 must use `reachable_on_stream_carried_frames()` (true for 7 of the 8 tokens; only `trailing_bytes` is datagram-only) to decide which series a panel or alert can ever populate, never inferring media from an entry-point name. Collapsing to a single `truncated` label fails silently: no guard fires, the metric looks correct, and the loss surfaces only at 3am when the triage fork has no data behind it — the original defect's shape one layer up. Target: MH forward path (task 16) and MH dashboards/runbook (tasks 21/22). Owner: @media-handler. Delete this entry when the entry-point-labelled counter and the `reachable_on_stream_carried_frames()`-driven triage both land.

- [ ] **Four `crates/media-protocol/` obligations from the ADR-0036 signalling reshape, all landing in story task 8** (raised by @operations, @security and @dry-reviewer at Gate 1 of `docs/devloop-outputs/2026-08-31-signaling-media-contract-reshape/`; owner: protocol + media-handler). Deferred out of that devloop for one reason: `crates/media-protocol/**` is a Guarded Shared Area requiring protocol **and** media-handler co-sign at Gate 1 *and* Gate 3 (ADR-0024 §6.4), and MH was not on that team — a full review cycle from a specialist with no other stake in the diff, for comment lines. Task 8 is a `media-protocol` task where that co-sign is already being paid, and is the next thing to edit these sites, so the marginal cost there is ~zero.
  - [x] **(a) LANDED (story task 8).** `header_version` is present and guard check **g3** asserts it equals `PROTOCOL_VERSION`, with a `rust-version-const-not-found` bail so a renamed declaration reds rather than comparing nothing. The "with drift-guard coverage" clause was the point: an unguarded key would have read as guarded and not been. Original text: **`proto/test-vectors/frame-v2.vectors.json` must carry a header-version key WITH drift-guard coverage.** `signaling.proto`'s `SendDirective.header_version` and `ParticipantCapabilities.supported_header_versions` both `ANCHOR (DRY)` to that file as the cross-language source of truth. Adding `supported_header_versions` is what made the header version cross-language: TypeScript now needs a value to derive from, and a Rust-only anchor would leave it hardcoding its own `2`. **The "with drift-guard coverage" clause is the point** — an unguarded key satisfies "task 8 owes a header-version key" while leaving the anchor dangling, which is worse than no anchor because it reads as guarded and is not.
  - [x] **(b) LANDED (story task 8).** `PROTOCOL_VERSION` gains the reciprocal `ANCHOR (DRY)` line noting its new cross-language status. It carries none today, unlike its neighbours, because nothing outside Rust consumed it until now.
  - [x] **(c) LANDED (story task 8).** The invariant is recorded at the impl: the disclosed lengths are compile-time constants and therefore not a function of the secret, and a future field whose length varies with its content must not have its length printed. Original text: `frame.rs`'s `impl Debug for WrappedTransmitKey` gains the safety invariant in a doc comment (verified absent at the time of writing): printing metadata in the clear is safe *because the disclosed lengths are compile-time constants and therefore not a function of the secret*. A future field whose length varies with its content does not satisfy that and must not have its length printed. That impl established the redaction pattern the signalling layer now copies in `crates/proto-gen/src/lib.rs`, so the reasoning belongs at its origin.
  - [x] **(d) LANDED (story task 8).** `KEY_ID_SENDER_ID_BITS` / `KEY_ID_STREAM_BITS` / `KEY_ID_GENERATION_BITS` are named constants with a compile-time assertion that they exactly fill `KEY_ID_BYTES` (a gap would be undecoded bits the nonce derivation depends on; an overlap would alias two key ids). The `KEY_ID_` prefix is deliberate so `KEY_ID_STREAM_BITS` is not conflated with the relay `STREAM_ID_FIELD_BYTES`, which is subscriber-scoped and rewritten by MH. The generator derives its bounds from them rather than from inline literals. Original text: Promote `KEY_ID_BYTES`'s prose key-id layout (`sender_id(16) | stream(8) | generation(40)`) **to named constants.** The signalling contract now anchors three separate bounds to that prose — `sender_id`'s 16 bits, `stream_number`'s 8, and (separately) `STREAM_ID_FIELD_BYTES` for `slot_id`. The TypeScript SFrame work will re-spell the same layout, and task 8's vectors are the answer for that half. **No guard covers the proto-side anchors today**: they are comments, and nothing fails if the layout moves and they do not.

- [ ] **The frame-header-version allowlist and minimum-version floor are named as owned by story tasks 14 and 10, but neither task owns them — the binding is dangling (raised by @operations at Gate 3 of `docs/devloop-outputs/2026-08-31-signaling-media-contract-reshape/`; owner: meeting-controller + security)**: `proto/dark_tower/signaling/v1/signaling.proto::ParticipantCapabilities.supported_header_versions` originally stated "Enforcement owners: story task 14 (allowlist and floor), story task 10 (join-time rejection)", and `docs/API_CONTRACTS.md` repeated the rule. But `supported_header_versions` and `allowlist` each appear **zero times** in `docs/user-stories/2026-08-27-hear-yourself-through-handler.md`, and neither task 14's nor task 10's prompt mentions a version floor — task 10's only "floor" is the unrelated trust-on-first-use security floor. So the control is specified in the contract, credited to a named owner, and implemented by nobody. That is worse than an unassigned obligation: a reader who checks the comment concludes it is covered. The rule being dropped is fail-open by construction — the comment itself states that an empty or below-floor `supported_header_versions` list must be rejected at join and never accommodated by lowering the meeting, because otherwise one patched client declaring only version 1 drags the whole meeting onto a header with no sender-authentication or key-carriage guarantee. Latent today only because `crates/media-protocol/src/frame.rs::PROTOCOL_VERSION` is 2 with no v1 decode path, so there is currently exactly one selectable version; it becomes live the moment a second version exists, which is the moment nobody will re-read this comment. **Fix**: either extend task 14's prompt to carry the server-side allowlist + configured minimum floor (config-driven, not a constant — CLAUDE.md "config over hardcoding") and task 10's to carry the join-time rejection, or repoint the proto/API_CONTRACTS comments at whichever task really takes it. **Half-fixed at Gate 3**: the misattribution is gone — both sites now read "ENFORCEMENT NOT YET ALLOCATED TO A TASK … Do NOT read this obligation as covered", which is the honest state and better than repointing at a task that does not really take it. **What remains is the allocation itself**, which is a story-decomposition call for the Lead, not something the protocol side can assign from the proto: some task must own the server-side allowlist and the configured minimum floor (config-driven, not a constant — CLAUDE.md "config over hardcoding") plus the below-floor join rejection, and it must land before a second header version exists. Delete this entry when a task owns it and the enforcement lands.

- [ ] **Five MC↔MH control-plane obligations from the ADR-0036 `internal.proto` reshape (landed 2026-09-01, `docs/devloop-outputs/2026-09-01-internal-contract-reshape/`)**. Each is a rule the proto **cannot** enforce and whose violation is **green under every test the story that introduces it can construct** — @test's "equivalent mutants under the reachable input space". The mitigation ladder, strongest first, is: (1) structural impossibility beats a test; (2) where structure cannot carry it, pin the killing test *with its distinguishing input named*, because "add a test" without the input is not a pin. Normative source for all five is the field comment cited in each; this file is the copy that survives a prompt rewrite.
  - [ ] **(a) `sender_id` is meeting-scoped; MH must never build a global `sender_id` → connection index (owner: story task 5; raised by @security, forcing function added by @auth-controller)**. Normative source: `proto/dark_tower/internal/v1/internal.proto::SubscriberSlot.sender_id`. A `sender_id` is a 16-bit **per-meeting ordinal** (key id = `sender_id`(16) | `stream`(8) | `generation`(40)), so `sender_id` 5 exists concurrently in every meeting on a handler; a global index is a **cross-meeting media-crossing primitive**, and cross-tenant leakage is the one threat class a keyless relay does not otherwise expose. `EgressStream` references `sender_id`, not `participant_id`, so the resolution index does not exist yet (`crates/mh-service/src/session/mod.rs:133` keys `meeting_id → participant_id → Vec<ConnectionEntry>`) and its shape is a free choice. **Acceptance pin — an executable MULTI-MEETING negative test: a `sender_id` valid in meeting A must be REJECTED when it appears in meeting B's `RegisterMeetingRequest`.** The test *shape* is part of the requirement, not an implementation detail: loopback is one meeting with one participant, so a correct implementation and a cross-tenant-leaking one are **observationally identical across all seven validation layers**, and a single-meeting fixture cannot distinguish them. @media-handler's stronger remedy, preferred where it fits: a composite `{ meeting, sender }` key that is *unconstructible without a `MeetingId`*, making the wrong index a compile error rather than a runtime rule — and a **fresh** `SenderId` newtype, never `crates/common/src/types.rs:66::UserId(pub u64)`, which would re-import the global-identifier semantics this reshape deletes.
  - [ ] **(b) MH must NOT enforce the `policy_generation == 0` rejection until MC emits `policy_generation` >= 1 (owner: story tasks 5 and 6, in that order; raised by @operations, endorsed by @media-handler)**. Normative source: `internal.proto::RegisterMeetingRequest.policy_generation`. The reject is implemented at task 5 (MH) but its precondition lands at task 6 (MC); enforced in that order it rejects **every** registration for the whole window — no meeting registers, every client is provisionally accepted and kicked at `MH_REGISTER_MEETING_TIMEOUT_SECONDS`, which is ADR-0036 §8's opening paragraph almost verbatim ("a permanent media blackhole for that meeting until it emptied, reached through an ordinary rolling deploy"). **The obvious workaround is wrong and is recorded so it is not rediscovered as a fix**: having MC send `policy_generation` 1 now makes MH apply "1 = no edges", and task 6's first *real* assignment is also naturally 1 (the value derives from assignment-output change), so MH no-ops the real policy — trading a loud outage for a silent one.
  - [ ] **(c) `process_start_epoch_ms`'s PRODUCER obligation, split across two stories (owner: story task 5 for the first half, handler-restart story for the second; raised by @test)**. Normative source: `internal.proto::RegisterMeetingResponse.process_start_epoch_ms`. MH must sample it **once at process start**, per-process — not per-call `now()`, not per-pod-name, not per-config. Both wrong implementations are observationally identical to the correct one until a **second process incarnation sharing a pod identity** exists. **Within-process stability IS constructible at task 5**: two `register_meeting` calls in one process MUST return the same epoch — that alone kills the per-call `now()` implementation. **Changes-on-restart is the load-bearing half and is not constructible until the handler-restart story**: incarnation A reports E1, restart into the same pod identity, incarnation B reports E2, E1 ≠ E2 → restart detected. Without the split this is one unpinnable obligation instead of one pinned now and one pinned where it becomes constructible.
  - [ ] **(d) MC must treat an `UNSPECIFIED` transport-mode echo as a MISMATCH, not a skip (owner: story task 6; raised by @operations and @observability, test named by @meeting-controller)**. Normative source: `internal.proto::RegisterMeetingResponse.transport_mode`. A pre-reshape MH echoes `TRANSPORT_MODE_UNSPECIFIED` (proto3 absent enum = zero) and the natural defensive form `if echoed != UNSPECIFIED { compare }` **fails open against precisely the peer population the check exists to police**. That form survives code review as defensiveness, and its skip-branch is **unreachable in any test written against a same-version peer**, so it is invisible to the test suite permanently. **Acceptance pin — the negative test with its distinguishing input named: a same-version peer echoes `TRANSPORT_MODE_UNSPECIFIED` and MC must report MISMATCH, not skip.** Three sibling reader obligations ride with it: `applied_generation` absent/0 → re-assert never success; `applied_generation` < sent → re-assert; `handler_id` != the dialed `MhAssignment.mh_id` → fail loud. All four fail-loud paths land in the bounded `outcome` label enumerated at `RegisterMeetingResponse.applied_generation`.
  - [ ] **(e) The single `transport_mode` echo expires at story 3 (video) (owner: protocol)**. Normative source: `internal.proto::RegisterMeetingResponse.transport_mode`. One field echoes a property that is **per-egress-stream** in the request; it is unambiguous only while a meeting's egress streams are homogeneous, which is true for story 1 (one audio egress stream, datagram) and false the moment video lands (§1: audio on datagrams, video on stream-per-group). MH therefore MUST reject a heterogeneous registration rather than pick one mode and echo it — echoing one of N would make the check silently meaningless, the precise failure §8 warns about. **The expiry is named rather than left open because a scope limit with no named expiry is how a temporary shape becomes the shape.** At story 3 the field moves per-egress, which the reject forces to happen loudly.

- [ ] **`dt-guard` has no `.proto` scanner, so ADR-0036's "the credential-leak guard covers KEK and transmit-key material in internal messages and logs" is true for logs and was false for internal messages (owner: security for the check definition, dt-guard/infrastructure for implementation; raised by @security)**. Enumerated, not sampled: every credential/PII module in the guard crate is extension-scoped to `.rs` or `.ts`/`.tsx`/`.svelte` — `rust_secrets.rs:38`, `rust_log_secrets.rs:41`, `rust_pii.rs:34`, `instrument_skip_all.rs:40`, `ts_secrets.rs:40`, `ts_pii.rs:31`, `ts_retained_credentials.rs:119`, `ts_metric_naming.rs:32`. `.proto` appears in that crate only in `cite_extract.rs`, `knowledge_index.rs` and `gsa_sync.rs` — citation resolution, index scope and the GSA path list, none of them scanners. The semantic layer is not a fallback either: `scripts/guards/semantic/checks.md` scopes its Credential Leak check to Rust (items 1-4) and TS (items 5-10) in its own preamble. This is row one of ADR-0036's own line-1158 taxonomy — *a control alive but out of scope, which reads as coverage*. **The ADR sentence was corrected in place on 2026-09-01** (visibly, with the correction noted, per the `docs/protocol/CONVENTIONS.md:35-56` precedent) rather than left standing against a tracked TODO, because a tracked TODO does not make a false sentence true — and because the false sentence does not merely misinform, it **redirects scrutiny**: a reviewer who reads that the check is mechanised spends their attention elsewhere, which is what makes the gap durable. Note there is **no loudness leg available** here, unlike the `breaking.ignore` carve-out: there is no scanner to print a warning from, so the corrected text is itself the control. Natural home for the fix is the planned Wave-3 `secret-scan` subcommand. **Compensating control today, stated as the weaker form it is**: no key-shaped field exists anywhere in the reshaped message set, and reintroducing one is a Gate-3 failure under @security's standing revocation condition — reviewer-enforced, not structural.

- [ ] **MH's egress-drop counter is structurally blind to quinn's own silent drop-oldest one layer below it; the far-end hop-sequence gap counter is the compensating control, and that relationship must be stated at BOTH metric definitions (raised by @dry-reviewer and @paired-media-handler, verified and ruled by @observability at Gate 1 of `docs/devloop-outputs/2026-09-01-mh-transport-seam/`; owner: observability for the catalog wording, media-handler for story task 16's counter, operations for story task 21's egress-exhaustion runbook)**. Verified against the locked versions (`Cargo.lock`: wtransport 0.7.2, quinn 0.11.11, quinn-proto 0.11.17). Three facts, none visible from wtransport's own API: **(i)** `quinn_proto::SendDatagramError` (`quinn-proto-0.11.17/src/connection/datagrams.rs`) carries a fourth variant, `Blocked(Bytes)` — precisely the payload-returning back-pressure shape MH wants — but `quinn::Connection::send_datagram` (`quinn-0.11.11/src/connection.rs`) calls `datagrams().send(data, true)` with `drop` hardcoded `true` and handles the blocked arm as `unreachable!()`, so on the API wtransport actually calls (`wtransport-0.7.2/src/driver/mod.rs`) transport back-pressure is unreachable **by construction**, not merely unsignalled. The egress loop must therefore derive back-pressure from its own queue depth and never from a transport error return — there is no transport error to key on. **(ii)** quinn's eviction path pops the oldest datagram and emits only a `trace!`, incrementing no `ConnectionStats` field; and because an evicted datagram was never transmitted, it never enters quinn's *lost-packet* statistics either. Its only native signal is a log line gated on trace level — which ADR-0036 §11 rules out in terms ("A log level is not an acceptable gate"). **(iii)** Consequently, under congestion severe enough to saturate quinn's buffer but not MH's, **MH's egress-overflow counter reads flat at exactly the moment loss is worst**: "no loss" and "loss we cannot see" are indistinguishable on that signal. ADR-0036 §1 already decided the remedy — size `datagram_send_buffer_size` so "the application-level egress queue bound must trip **before** the transport ceiling", expressed in frames of audio — and story task 12 makes it a startup validation. But that is the "does it apply" half only, and it holds **only while the configured relationship holds**; it does not make the counter honest under misconfiguration, nor if quinn's buffer is pressured by anything other than MH's queue depth. The honest compensating control is the far-end **hop-sequence gap** counter: MH assigns `hop_sequence` before handing the datagram to the transport, so a quinn-evicted datagram consumes a hop number and never arrives, and the receiver observes a genuine gap. **Obligation**: state the relationship at *both* entries in `docs/observability/metrics/mh-service.md` — the egress-overflow entry must record that it counts MH's own queue drops only and that quinn may evict silently beneath it, and the hop-gap entry must record that it is the far-end signal for that eviction. Absent the cross-reference each reads as redundant with the other and the pair is a candidate for being "simplified" to one. Task 21's runbook must carry it as an interpretation caveat on the egress-drop signal, because a flat counter during a congestion incident is the actively misleading case, not merely an absent one. **Naming constraint on the same counter, and it is stronger than "blind spot" (raised by @security as S5 at Gate 1 of the same devloop, accepted by @observability as catalog/alert wording)**: the transport below the seam never refuses at all — in production the refusal is produced by MH's own bounded egress queue *above* the seam — so the counter is wholly a property of MH's own queue, and neither its catalog entry nor any alert, dashboard panel or runbook sentence may describe it as observing transport back-pressure. The two facts compose and both are needed: the counter observes only MH's queue (S5), **and** genuine loss occurs beneath it that the counter structurally cannot see (the eviction above). A reader given only the first believes the counter is complete for what it names; given only the second, that it is a degraded version of a transport signal it was never measuring.

## Rate Limiting

- [x] **AC: Make rate limit constants env-configurable**: All 4 rate limit constants (login + registration) now env-configurable via `AC_RATE_LIMIT_WINDOW_MINUTES`, `AC_RATE_LIMIT_MAX_ATTEMPTS`, `AC_REGISTRATION_RATE_LIMIT_WINDOW_MINUTES`, `AC_REGISTRATION_RATE_LIMIT_MAX_ATTEMPTS`. Relaxed values set in Kind configmap.
- [ ] **GC: Wire up rate limiting middleware**: `crates/gc-service/src/config.rs` has `RATE_LIMIT_RPM` (default 100) and `GcError::RateLimitExceeded` exists, but no middleware enforces it. Add a tower rate limiting layer (e.g., governor) to routes, especially the public guest token endpoint (`/api/v1/meetings/{code}/guest-token`).
- [ ] **MC: Evaluate rate limiting needs**: MC has no rate limiting. WebTransport connections are long-lived so per-request limiting is less relevant, but the gRPC endpoint from GC should have some protection against runaway reconnection storms.

## Infrastructure Validation in Devloops

- [ ] **Deployed-artifact IDENTITY is unasserted — the other half of "the shipped artifact is the one the pipeline built"** (added 2026-09-01, devloop `docs/devloop-outputs/2026-09-01-release-premise-and-preflight-guards/`; owner `infrastructure`, needs an `operations` ruling on tag policy). `dt-guard release-build-profile` asserts the shipped artifact's build *configuration* (release profile, debug-assertions off across seven channels). It says nothing about whether the image a cluster runs is the image that pipeline produced — mutable tags, no digest pinning, no provenance. Different inputs, different mechanism, and it needs a tag-policy decision before any guard can be written, which is why it is a task and not a fold-in.

- [ ] **Shape-based complement to the release-profile premise guard: observe the effective `cfg`, not its causes** (added 2026-09-01, same devloop; proposed by @security; owner `infrastructure` + `media-handler` co-sign). `dt-guard release-build-profile` enumerates seven channels that can enable `debug_assertions` in a shipped binary, and an enumeration is incomplete by construction (ADR-0036 §11: *"Vocabulary additions cannot be cited as the protection"*). The total-by-construction alternative is a compile-time assertion on the **effective cfg**:

    ```rust
    #[cfg(all(feature = "release-artifact", debug_assertions))]
    compile_error!("release artifact built with debug_assertions enabled");
    ```

  with the service Dockerfiles passing `--features release-artifact`. That catches all seven channels *and* ones nobody has enumerated — §11's own technique, applied to the premise instead of to the tracing feature: "a compile error has nothing to notice." **Complement, not replacement**: the guard catches the source change early, legibly, across all four services at review time and can say *which* channel broke; the `cfg` assertion catches the artifact totally but can only say "compile error". Deferred because it touches four service crates (three of them other specialists' domains), their `[features]` tables, and four Dockerfiles.

- [ ] **`dev-web.sh`'s WebTransport preflight reads the on-disk ConfigMap, and that is now a BLOCKER rather than a misleading green** (added 2026-09-01, devloop `docs/devloop-outputs/2026-09-01-release-premise-and-preflight-guards/`; owner `infrastructure`; condition attached by @operations at Gate 1 as OPS-3). **This entry is the rollback plan for a decision that was made deliberately — recorded before shipping, not after it bit.** `scripts/dev-web.sh::check_wt_endpoint` reads MC/MH advertise addresses from the committed configmap files. On the static host topology the script targets, on-disk and live agree. On a **devloop** cluster `setup.sh` patches the live ConfigMap while the on-disk file goes stale, so the check can validate an address nobody is using. Before this devloop that produced a harmless yellow `!`; the WARN→HARD-FAIL escalation makes it **stop the dev server on a healthy cluster**. Accepted because `dev-web.sh` is the host-side launcher for the static topology (its own header says so) and devloop work does not go through it, and because every listener failure now prints the ground-truth `kubectl get cm <label>-config -n dark-tower -o jsonpath='{.data.<MC|MH>_WEBTRANSPORT_ADVERTISE_ADDRESS}'` command plus a pointer to runbook §1/F8, turning a blocker into a 30-second diagnosis. **The fix if it ever does bite is to read the LIVE ConfigMap, not to soften the check** — and explicitly **not** a bypass environment variable, which is the "silently skip a failure to make progress" pattern CLAUDE.md forbids and which the first person to hit it would leave permanently on.

- [ ] **`crates/mh-test-utils/src/lib.rs`'s doc comment cites Dockerfile LINE NUMBERS that nothing keeps current** (added 2026-09-01, same devloop; surfaced by @dry-reviewer as P-DRY-5; owner `test` / `media-handler`). The comment's security argument cites `infra/docker/mh-service/Dockerfile` lines 53, 59, 78 and 135 by number. Any edit to that Dockerfile silently re-points them at unrelated lines, and `cite-no-line-numbers` does not reach Rust doc comments — so this is a citation class with no guard behind it at all. The *claim* those lines support is now enforced (`dt-guard release-build-profile`, cross-referenced from that comment via an `ANCHOR (DRY)` line); the *citations* are still unguarded. Either extend the doc-citation guard family to Rust doc comments or replace the line numbers with symbol/stage cites.

- [ ] **`producer | grep -q` under `set -o pipefail` is a latent false-negative class — swept and counted, NOT fixed** (surfaced 2026-08-15 at Gate 3 by @infrastructure while fixing one instance in `infra/kind/scripts/setup.sh:provision_run_org()`; generalisation prompted by @test; devloop `docs/devloop-outputs/2026-08-15-layer7-per-run-org/`). **Mechanism**: `grep -q` exits at the FIRST match and closes the pipe; if the producer has more to write it takes SIGPIPE (141) and `pipefail` makes the **whole pipeline non-zero even though the pattern WAS found**. Reachability is a function of (producer's stdout buffer) vs (position of the match), so it is invisible on small inputs and appears as the input grows — the worst possible discovery curve for a gate. This is the sibling of the `find | head` / `tr | head` trap already documented at `scripts/workflow/run-story.sh:748-776`; that one is about the producer *failing*, this one is about the producer *succeeding and being cut off*.
  **Measured in this repo, today** (not theoretical): `git ls-files | grep -qxF <first-path>` false-negatives against this repo right now (71 KB of output, needle on line 1); with the needle on the LAST line it passes, which is the tell that the match position is half the mechanism. Independently reproduced by @test from a **script file** rather than an agent Bash shell, per this devloop's own §Issues #2 lesson.
  **TWO DISTINCT MECHANISMS — do not collapse them, they imply different regression tests** (corrected 2026-08-15 by @test, re-measured independently by @infrastructure; earlier drafts of this entry described only the first and framed it as a clean size threshold, which was one sample per point and wrong in both directions):
  - **(a) Incremental producer** (`git ls-files`, `git show --name-only`, `kubectl`, shell loops) — a **scheduler RACE**. It fires well below the pipe buffer and never becomes a clean threshold. 30 runs/point, needle on line 1: `kubectl config get-contexts` 100 contexts **2/30**, 200 **18/30**, 300 **29/30**, 3000 **30/30**; `git show --name-only` N=80 **0/30**, N=200 **8/30**, N=300 **27/30**, N=400 **29/30**.
  - **(b) `cat`-backed producer** — `__changed_files()` is `cat "$cache"` (`_changed_helpers.sh:39-43`), a plain file read, which makes reproduction **byte-driven**. But it is *not* cleanly deterministic at the buffer boundary either: 20 runs/point over a synthetic cache, needle on line 1, `grep -qxF` consumer — 24 KB **0/20**, 48 KB **0/20**, 72 KB **1/20**, 77 KB **0/20**, 96 KB **1/20**, 144 KB **20/20**. So there is an intermittent band around the pipe buffer and determinism only well above it. **Neither mechanism licenses a "safe under N" claim** — and this is the one line to keep when the numbers above drift, because it is the instruction, not the data.
  **METHODOLOGICAL WARNING, earned the hard way — read before trusting any number in this entry.** Over the drafting of *this single entry* a size claim was flattened into a threshold **three times**, and every one was wrong: "the detector stops firing on any commit touching ~80+ files" (@infrastructure; actually 0/30 at N=80), "deterministic at ~68 KB" (@test; actually an intermittent band), and "no arrangement of that gate holds" (@test; actually a real conditional rescue in the middle band). Each was caught only by **re-running at more than one sample per point**, and the third was written *after* its author had authored the correction for the second — knowing the failure mode did not prevent repeating it. Two independent reviewers, three instances, one entry. So: every number here is a distribution, not a step; single-sample measurements of SIGPIPE races are worthless; and any future edit that turns one of these bands back into a threshold is reintroducing the defect the entry is about.
  **CONSUMER LANDSCAPE UPDATE (2026-08-20, validation-run-all devloop) — the bug and the file are UNCHANGED and STILL LIVE; only which consumers are exposed shifted.** `_changed_helpers.sh` stays; `:54`/`:66`/`:100` are untouched. But the per-lang `changed.sh` classifiers (`rust/changed.sh:6`, `ts/changed.sh:7`) that this entry cites as consumers were **DELETED** when the skip-if-untouched policy was retired (ADR-0033 §2/§3). Consequences to re-read accordingly: (1) the "big Rust change silently skips compile/fmt/clippy/test" scenario below is **OBSOLETE** — those language layers now **always-run** regardless of any diff predicate, so a `diff_touches_path` false-negative can no longer skip them. (2) The **security-critical consequence is UNCHANGED and remains the whole point**: the surviving `:66`/`diff_touches_root_files` + `diff_touches_glob` consumers are `_audit_gate.sh` (the **dependency audit gate**) — a false-negative there still silently skips the security audit with a green pipeline. (3) `diff_touches_path` (`:54`) now has one surviving consumer, `layer7.sh` Phase-1b's `diff_touches_path "infra/kind/"` cluster-rebuild check — a false-negative there runs the suite against a **stale cluster**. Fix `:54`/`:66` per the prescribed shape below regardless; the audit-gate exposure is why this stays high-priority. The measured bands are preserved verbatim below for the record; read the `rust/changed.sh` mentions as historical.
  **The sharp remaining sites, all gate logic, all under `set -euo pipefail`, owner `operations`:**
  - `scripts/lang/_changed_helpers.sh:54` — `diff_touches_path()`'s `__changed_files | awk 'index($0,p)==1 { found=1; exit }'`. **THE WORST SITE IN THE REPO, and it was missed by the first two passes of this entry** (found by @test): `awk … exit` is an early-exit consumer exactly like `grep -q`, and it reproduces where the others do not — 20/20 false negatives from ~72 KB up, versus `grep -qxF`'s 1/20 at the same size. **The composite consequence is worse than either predicate alone, and it has THREE BANDS — do not flatten it in either direction.** `scripts/lang/rust/changed.sh:6` is `diff_touches_path "crates/" || diff_touches_root_files "Cargo.toml" "Cargo.lock" "rust-toolchain.toml"`, so the `||` reads as a safety net. Measured on a faithful replica and confirmed by @test against the real script with a real cache file, 20 runs/point, needle at git-realistic position:
    - **< ~1500 changed files (~72 KB)** — both branches fine (pure-`crates/` N=100 and N=1000: 0/20).
    - **~1500–2500 files** — `diff_touches_path` fails **20/20**, and the fallback rescues the run **only if a root manifest is in the changeset**. A pure-`crates/` changeset here is already **skipped 20/20 at N=2000 (92 KB)** — and that is the common shape of a large Rust change. With `Cargo.toml` also touched, N=2000 recovers 20/20 (`diff_touches_root_files` 0/20 at that size).
    - **> ~3000 files (~138 KB)** — **both branches fail 20/20 regardless**; N=3000 and N=4000 classify Rust as untouched even with `Cargo.toml` in the diff. At this size no arrangement of the gate holds.
    So the `||` is not a degraded safety net — it is a **second independent instance of the same bug** that merely fails later, and @test's `Cargo.lock`/`rust-toolchain.toml` finding below removes the only operands that could have sorted late enough to survive. (An earlier draft of this bullet said the fallback simply "returns 1 too"; @test then measured it failing at N=4000 and generalised to "no arrangement holds". Both overshoot: the middle band is a real, conditional rescue. Recorded precisely because a band structure nobody would guess is exactly what makes this gate's behaviour untrustworthy — it is size-dependent, and the dependence is invisible.)
    The trap is self-reinforcing: the changeset must be large to fire, and a large Rust changeset is precisely a pure-`crates/` one, so **the bigger the Rust change, the more certainly its validation is skipped**. **Severity in the language of the thing protected: on a large Rust changeset, compile / fmt / clippy / test AND the Rust dependency audit are all skipped, silently, with the pipeline green.** That is "fail loudly, never mask" inverted, measured rather than argued. Fix `:54` first.
  - `scripts/lang/_changed_helpers.sh:66` — `diff_touches_root_files()`'s `__changed_files | grep -qxF -- "$f"`. Same class, less exposed than `:54` (intermittent from ~72 KB, deterministic only ~144 KB ≈ 3000 changed files), but the blast radius is the widest (@test): the predicate feeds `scripts/lang/_audit_gate.sh:101,116` — the **dependency audit gate** — as well as `scripts/lang/rust/changed.sh:6` and `scripts/lang/ts/changed.sh:7`. A false negative on the Rust needle therefore **silently skips the security audit**, with a green pipeline. Exposure is asymmetric and measurable: `Cargo.toml` sorts at position **54 of 1395** tracked paths (uppercase sorts before lowercase, so it is in the first 4% of any changeset containing it — maximum SIGPIPE exposure), while `pnpm-lock.yaml` sorts at **1274 of 1395**, so the TS half is far less exposed. **`diff_touches_root_files` has ZERO direct self-test coverage** — `scripts/lang/_changed_helpers.test.sh`'s own header says it is "focused on `diff_touches_glob`" and calls that sibling "security-load-bearing — a wrong match/skip = wrong audit run/skip", while leaving this one untested. Scope the regression test to the **audit gate**, not merely to layer skipping.
  - **Compounding, and it makes the position-54 point decisive** (@test's separate finding, verified): **`Cargo.lock` is gitignored** (`.gitignore:5`; `git ls-files --error-unmatch Cargo.lock` → not tracked) and **`rust-toolchain.toml` does not exist** in this repo. So of `diff_touches_root_files "Cargo.toml" "Cargo.lock" "rust-toolchain.toml"`, **exactly one operand can ever match — `Cargo.toml`, the one at sort position 54 of 1395, i.e. worst-case needle position.** There is no second operand to fall back on. Worse, `scripts/lang/_audit_gate.test.sh:39` asserts `"rust: Cargo.lock changed -> run"`, i.e. it certifies coverage on an input the pipeline **cannot produce** — a test that passes on an impossible input rather than one that cannot fail. Different species from the vacuous-pass bugs this devloop fixed, same family. Include in the operations devloop's scope: either track `Cargo.lock` (it is a binary/application workspace, so committing it is the conventional call and would also make the audit gate mean what its test claims) or drop the dead operands and fix the test.
  - `scripts/workflow/run-story.sh:1212` — `git show HEAD --format= --name-only | grep -q '^audit-suppressions\.toml$'`. `audit-suppressions.toml` sorts very early in git's path order (only dotfile dirs precede it), so the needle is near line 1 — the worst position. Reproduction rate rises with commit size per the race numbers above (0/30 at 80 files, 29/30 at 400); it never becomes a clean threshold.
  **Lower-risk siblings, assessed rather than assumed** (owner `infrastructure`, mine; enumerated so nobody re-derives the list): `infra/kind/scripts/setup.sh:355,1365` and `infra/devloop/devloop.sh:653` (`kind get clusters | grep -q` — a handful of lines, one write; and a false negative leads to a loud `kind create cluster` failure, not a silent pass); `infra/kind/scripts/setup.sh:393` (`kubectl get pods -l k8s-app=calico-node | grep -q calico-node` — small label-selected output, match is NOT on line 1, and it sits in a retry loop so a spurious miss costs one iteration); `infra/devloop/devloop.sh:357,406` and `scripts/lang/rust/test.sh:65` (`podman ps --format | grep -q '^name$'` — reachable at ~100+ running containers, consequence is starting a duplicate container, i.e. visibly wrong rather than silently green). Also in the sweep: `scripts/lang/ts/audit.sh:107`, `scripts/dev-web.sh:269,271`, `scripts/test-oauth-integration.sh:41,43,54,112` (`echo "$KUBECTL_LOGS" | grep -q` — a builtin producer with a potentially large log variable, same class).
  **Why not fixed in the R-7 diff**: cross-owner (all three sharp sites are `operations`-owned validation-pipeline gate logic → Domain-judgment under ADR-0024 §6.3, routes to owner-implements), and each site needs its own reachability measurement plus a regression test, which is its own test surface. Fixing them blind by mechanical rewrite is exactly the change-pattern §6.2 forbids classifying as Mechanical.
  **Fix shape — THE PRECEDENT ALREADY SHIPS IN THE SAME FILE, three functions down** (@test). `diff_touches_glob()` at `_changed_helpers.sh:100` measures **0/20 at every size tested, up to 144 KB**, because it reads via **process substitution** — `while IFS= read -r line; do … return 0; done < <(__changed_files)` — so there is no pipe, and an early `return` cannot become a producer SIGPIPE. The operations devloop does not need to invent a shape: **make `:54` and `:66` look like `:100`.** That is more convincing than a recipe because it already ships and already passes, and it keeps the three predicates in one idiom instead of two. Where process substitution does not fit, read into a variable and match with a herestring (`x="$(producer)" || x=""; grep -qxF "$needle" <<<"$x"` — the shape used for the `setup.sh` fix); only where streaming genuinely matters, `producer | grep -qxF … || rc=$?` with the 141 case handled explicitly.
  **REGRESSION-TEST SHAPE IS PRESCRIBED, and a count-based repro MUST NOT SHIP** (@test). An earlier draft of this entry said "drive a producer past its buffer"; per the race numbers above that yields a test which fails to reproduce the bug ~1 run in 30 even at N=400 — a test that cannot be trusted to have caught anything, under ADR-0028's zero-retry policy. That is this devloop's own vacuous-pass class one level up. Use a producer that **blocks after emitting the match**, which forces the SIGPIPE window open with certainty and needs no size tuning: `{ printf 'needle\n'; sleep 0.2; printf 'more\n'; } | grep -q '^needle$'` — verified **30/30** rc=141, versus 2/30–29/30 for every count-based driver. Use a blocking read rather than `sleep` to keep it at ~0s. **Schedule as an operations devloop**; the `infrastructure`-owned siblings above ride along or are explicitly declined with the reasoning already recorded here. Owner: operations (lead), infrastructure (siblings).

- [ ] **Deploy-step validation**: Devloops that modify K8s manifests, kustomization files, setup.sh, or other deploy infrastructure currently have no way to validate their changes actually work — issues like Kustomize path restrictions, postgres security context incompatibilities, and selector mutation only surface when running `setup.sh` against a real cluster. Need to figure out how to incorporate deploy validation into the devloop workflow (e.g., `kustomize build` dry-run, Kind cluster in CI, or a lightweight deploy-test step).

- [ ] **absorb-devloop.sh: detect duplicate tracking-table rows after cherry-pick** (surfaced 2026-06-22 after many manual dedup commits during the post-PR-58 debt-repayment wave): `.gitattributes` sets `docs/user-stories/*.md merge=union` so parallel devloops appending different tracking rows don't conflict. But when a devloop modifies an existing row IN-PLACE (the Step 8 Pending→Completed flip) AND the host has made any intervening edit to user-story.md between clone-creation and absorb, the cherry-pick context drifts and `union-merge` silently keeps BOTH the original Pending row AND the new Completed row instead of replacing. The result is a duplicate row that the operator has to spot and manually remove every absorb. Observed at #46/#47/#48/#49/#52/#9/#10 (every absorb that landed after an intervening host edit) but NOT at #50/#54/#55 (no intervening host edit between clone-create and absorb). Fix: post-cherry-pick scan in `infra/devloop/absorb-devloop.sh` for duplicate task-ID rows in the affected user-story file (e.g., `awk -F'|' '/^\| [0-9]+ \|/ {print $2}' | sort | uniq -d`); if any appear, exit non-zero with a clear message naming the duplicated IDs so the operator can dedup before the absorb completes — same "refuse to silently apply a bad resolution" discipline the script already has for other conflict classes. Strictly an after-the-fact detector, not a preventer, but removes the silent-bad-resolution failure mode. Same dupe class probably lurks for `docs/TODO.md` (also `merge=union`) — we just haven't seen the host edit TODO.md between clone-create and absorb yet. **Update 2026-08-17**: the tracking table this was filed against was retired by the story-runner-hardening story, but the hazard moved rather than closed — the `dt-story` manifest now lives in the same `merge=union` file with the same in-place flip (`status: pending` → `status: completed`, plus a new `slug:` line). It should now fail *loudly* rather than silently, since duplicate keys in one YAML mapping do not parse, and the story-runner-hardening absorb came through clean (4 `status:`, 4 `slug:`); neither is a tested guarantee, so the post-absorb check is `dt-story validate` on the affected story file. Owner: infrastructure.

- [ ] **`absorb-devloop.sh` cannot resume, and its own hand-off trips the Gate-2 pre-commit hook** (both surfaced 2026-08-17 absorbing the story-runner-hardening story; they compound, so they are filed together). (a) **No resume**: the script has no in-progress detection — no `sequencer`/`CHERRY_PICK_HEAD` check — and `:168` starts a fresh `git cherry-pick`, which git refuses while one is active. Its documented contract (`:32` exit 3, `:171`) is to hand off to plain git on conflict, so re-running after resolving does nothing useful. (b) **The hand-off then blocks**: a clean `git cherry-pick` does not run `pre-commit`, but `git cherry-pick --continue` completes through the normal commit path and does — so the Gate-2 binding refuses every devloop commit in the queue for want of a verdict artifact, which lives in the run dir and does not travel with the patch. Its advice ("run `./scripts/layer-all.sh`") is unfollowable on the host, where layer 7 has no cluster. Net effect: the first conflict in any absorb strands the operator. Fix: run the script's own picks with hooks disabled (`git -c core.hooksPath=/dev/null cherry-pick …`) and say so in the summary — legitimate because the content was validated on the source branch and the hook documents itself as "LOCAL-ONLY / ADVISORY … not a security boundary", with CI's from-scratch re-run as the real enforcement. Add in-progress detection that continues the existing queue instead of starting a new one. Note (a) has a second trigger independent of conflicts: `git cherry` compares patch-ids, so a planning commit **amended on the target after the clone branched** is not recognised as already-absorbed and is queued as an empty pick the operator must `--skip` — which is exactly how this absorb began. Owner: infrastructure.

- [ ] **Devloops wiring up new code-quality tooling don't auto-fix the existing surface** (surfaced 2026-06-24 during task #28's Gate 2): when a devloop introduces a new lint/format/type-check rule that applies to an existing language surface, the rule starts producing violations on code that was authored before the rule existed. The devloop introducing the rule typically ships green (its own code conforms; the rule has nothing to compare against in-diff). But every subsequent devloop touching the same surface inherits the violations as "pre-existing branch debt" and blocks at Gate 2/3 — surfacing as "I want to do my work but X unrelated files violate a rule I didn't introduce." Two concrete cases on this branch: (1) task #17 wired up Prettier + ESLint config over `packages/sdk-core/`, but #11's TS code (authored before #17 was wired) never saw prettier; the formatting violations surfaced first during #12's planning and then again during #28's Gate 2, with both implementers stalled on cross-boundary cleanup that isn't their lane. (2) Same shape would apply to a future devloop wiring up a new clippy lint, a new dt-guard check, or a stricter tsc flag — the rule lands clean against an empty diff, then bites every subsequent devloop on the same surface. Whatever the fix shape is (rule-introducing-devloop runs auto-fix as part of its scope / Lead-side checklist when reviewing tooling-introducing plans / a separate "branch-health" devloop variant that lands the auto-fix sweep alongside the rule / something else), the principle is: a devloop introducing a rule should leave the existing surface compliant with the rule by the time it commits, so subsequent devloops never inherit "pre-existing rule violations" as a Gate-2 blocker. Solution open — needs scoping. Owner: operations (devloop workflow design) + infrastructure (any tooling support).

- [x] **`validate-env-config` silently skips MC + MH (per-instance deployment filenames) — false-confidence STATUS=OK** (surfaced 2026-07-06 by @operations at task #6 Gate-3 review; accepted spin-out, @team-lead-endorsed). `dt-guard env-config`'s workload discovery `find_workload()` (`crates/dt-guard/src/env_config.rs:53-58`) only recognizes the literal filenames `deployment.yaml` / `statefulset.yaml`. AC (`statefulset.yaml`) and GC (`deployment.yaml`) match and are validated; **MC and MH use per-instance names** (`mc-0-deployment.yaml` / `mc-1-deployment.yaml`, `mh-0-deployment.yaml` / `mh-1-deployment.yaml`) so `find_workload` returns `None` → the guard emits `WARN dt-guard auxiliary skip (no workload manifest)` and moves on, yet still exits `STATUS=OK REASON=env-config-clean-4-services`. Net effect: the config↔deployment cross-check (Checks 1-3: required env vars present in workload; `configMapKeyRef` keys exist in configmap; configmap keys referenced by ≥1 workload) provides **zero coverage for two of four services**, and the `-4-services` REASON overcounts (only AC+GC were actually validated). This is the same false-confidence / silent-skip class task #50 addressed — a green guard that isn't checking what the reader assumes. Concretely bit task #6: the 4 OTel keys had to be surfaced in BOTH `mc-0` and `mc-1` deployments (the "easy to half-do" case), and that invariant shipped correct ONLY because @operations hand-verified it — the guard could not. **Fix (three parts):** (a) `find_workload` must discover multiple per-instance workloads — glob `*-deployment.yaml` / `*-statefulset.yaml` in the service dir (falling back to the canonical singular names), returning a set rather than an `Option`; (b) decide **union-vs-intersection semantics** across the discovered instance workloads — a key present in `mc-0` but missing in `mc-1` is precisely the drift to catch, so Checks 1 & 3 want **all-instances (intersection of presence)** not any-instance, while Check 2 (`configMapKeyRef` → configmap key exists) is per-workload; (c) covers **MH identically** (same per-instance topology). Add multi-workload test fixtures (a service dir with two `-N-deployment.yaml` files, one missing a key → must FAIL). Consider hardening the no-workload skip to a hard FAIL (not WARN) when the dir contains `*-deployment.yaml`/`*-statefulset.yaml` but no recognized workload, so a future naming scheme can't silently reopen the hole. Non-trivial (>10 LoC + design decision on semantics + new test surface + touches two services) → correctly outside task #6's changeset. Cross-ref: §Polyglot Pipeline Follow-ups (dt-guard / ADR-0034 guard pipeline is the same binary). **Schedule as a focused infrastructure dt-guard devloop.** Owner: infrastructure (dt-guard maintainer). Provenance: `docs/devloop-outputs/2026-07-05-mc-otel-wiring-task6/main.md`. **RESOLVED 2026-08-28** in `docs/devloop-outputs/2026-08-28-env-config-guard-per-instance-workloads/main.md`. **Semantics that actually landed** (this entry posed the union-vs-intersection question, so recording the answer is the point — the next person to touch this guard will look here first): **Check 1 (required env vars) — PER-WORKLOAD.** Every discovered workload must independently declare every `MissingEnvVar` from `config.rs`. This is the entry's "intersection of presence": a var in `mc-0` but not `mc-1` is a real defect (that pod CrashLoops at half capacity while its sibling stays healthy) and is now caught. **Check 2 (`configMapKeyRef` resolves) — NAME-SCOPED, per workload.** The `(configMapKeyRef.name, key)` PAIR is resolved against that specific ConfigMap's `data:` block, never a directory-wide union of every key in sight. A union would have been strictly WEAKER than the guard it replaced: it would newly pass `mh-0-deployment.yaml` naming `mh-service-config` for a key that only `mh-0-config` declares — a manifest kubelet refuses to start the pod on. An unresolvable ConfigMap name is its own rule id, `configmap_not_found`. **Check 3 (orphan keys) — NAME-SCOPED UNION.** A key is an orphan iff NO workload references *(that ConfigMap's name, that key)*. Union across workloads (so `mh-0-config`'s key, referenced only by `mh-0-deployment.yaml`, is correctly not an orphan) but scoped by ConfigMap name (so a key added to the wrong instance's ConfigMap is still caught, which a name-blind union hides because *a* workload references *that key name*). **Discovery — by parsed `kind:`, NOT the `*-deployment.yaml` glob this entry proposed.** A glob replaces one literal with a longer one and reopens the hole at the next naming change, and it would have minted a third mirror of the per-instance ordinal convention already encoded in `infra/kind/scripts/setup.sh` and the service `kustomization.yaml`s. A manifest kind classified as neither pod-carrying nor pod-spec-free is now a hard FAIL, so a future naming or kind change cannot silently reopen the hole. **The no-workload skip is a hard FAIL**, as this entry suggested considering, along with four sibling silent-skip paths found in the same function (missing `config.rs`/infra dir, `envFrom`, unparseable manifest, workload without a pod spec). `checked_services` now counts only services actually checked, and the OK line reports `env-config-clean-<S>-services-<W>-workloads` so a dropped instance manifest is visible in the status line itself. Remediated the four orphan keys this surfaced in `mh-service-config` (see §Observability Debt).
- [ ] **`dt-guard env-config` does not cover `infra/kubernetes/overlays/**` — a declared coverage boundary, not an oversight (surfaced 2026-08-28 by @security at Gate-1 review of the env-config per-instance-workload devloop)**: the guard resolves ConfigMap keys and `configMapKeyRef`s within `infra/services/<svc>/` only. Kind overlays carry live `data:` keys the guard never sees — `infra/kubernetes/overlays/kind/services/{ac,gc,mc}-service/configmap-otel-patch.yaml`, wired via each overlay's `patches:` stanza. Because a Kustomize strategic merge on a ConfigMap is **additive**, an overlay can introduce a key that no base ConfigMap declares and no workload references, and it is invisible to Checks 2 and 3 — in the surface most likely to be edited during an incident. Verified at the same review: no overlay today creates a ConfigMap that a base workload references, and there is no `configMapGenerator` anywhere in the tree (all eight service kustomizations checked), so there is no live defect — this is a scope gap, not a bug. **Why it is not folded into the guard now**: overlays need *patch* semantics (a partial document merged onto a named base resource) rather than the *declaration* semantics the guard implements; that is a different resolution model plus a kustomize-build dependency, and it is task-sized. **Why it is tracked rather than left implicit**: after the 2026-08-28 fix the guard emits `env-config-clean-<S>-services-<W>-workloads`, which a reader will reasonably take to mean "this service's ConfigMap surface is checked." Shipping that line over an unaudited surface would reproduce, one directory up, the exact false-confidence defect the fix removed. The boundary is also stated in the `crates/dt-guard/src/env_config.rs` module doc so it is visible at the code, not only here. **Action**: extend `env-config` (or add a sibling policy) to resolve overlay ConfigMap patches against their base, so an overlay-introduced key is either referenced or reported. **Related design note**: if a workload ever needs to reference a ConfigMap supplied from OUTSIDE the service directory, the agreed shape is an **explicit declared list of externally-supplied ConfigMap names** (visible, reviewable, config-over-hardcoding) — NOT an implicit union fallback that resolves a name it cannot see against keys it can, which was considered and rejected at Gate 1 as empty-collection-means-clean in a different hat. Owner: infrastructure (dt-guard maintainer). Provenance: `docs/devloop-outputs/2026-08-28-env-config-guard-per-instance-workloads/main.md`.
- [ ] **dt-guard wrappers exec `target/release/dt-guard` gated only on existence, never freshness — a stale binary can produce a green verdict against unbuilt code (surfaced 2026-08-28 by @security + @operations at review of the env-config per-instance-workload devloop)**: `scripts/guards/simple/_dt_guard_wrapper.sh` resolves `DT_GUARD=${DT_GUARD:-$REPO_ROOT/target/release/dt-guard}` and gates solely on `[[ -x "$DT_GUARD" ]]` — it never checks the binary is newer than `crates/dt-guard/src/`.
