Owner: operations + security. Effort: medium (wtransport upgrade has regression test budget for the WebTransport surface; sqlx-mysql trim may need workspace-level Cargo config).

## Layer 7 Playwright Lane (ADR-0033 §3 vs `layer7.sh` drift)

- [ ] **ADR-0033 §3 promises a Layer-7 Playwright lane that does not exist** (surfaced 2026-07-30 at task #58 Gate 1 by @test + @operations independently; **pre-existing drift, NOT introduced by #58** — recorded here rather than as a finding against that diff). ADR-0033:152 gives the Layer-7 row as "dev-cluster + Rust env-tests + Playwright `@smoke`", and :87 lists `e2e.sh # nx affected -t test:e2e (Playwright)` in the dispatcher layout. Neither exists: `scripts/layer7.sh:410` runs `cargo test -p env-tests --features all` and nothing else, `scripts/lang/ts/e2e.sh` is absent (ADR-0033:479 says it lands "when task #15 lands"), and `packages/web-app/playwright.config.ts` + `packages/web-app/e2e/` are task #18 deliverables. **Consequence at task #58**: verification item (c) ("`MeetingSession.join` succeeds against the live Kind cluster using ONLY a userToken") had no executable lane, and was re-scoped to #18 by joint @test + @operations ruling — the server half is covered by `crates/env-tests/tests/24_join_flow.rs::test_mc_webtransport_connect_and_join` (now pinned observationally via `AuthClient::call_count()`), the client half moves to #18. Between #58 and #18 the browser join path has **no live-cluster coverage at all**, and #58 is the commit that changed that path's contract. **Requirement on #18**: wiring the lane into `layer7.sh` (or `lang/ts/e2e.sh` + the Layer-7 call site) is part of the task, not a follow-up — specs that no layer invokes read as coverage and provide none, which is the same masked-coverage shape #58 spent its review budget removing. Either wire the lane or correct ADR-0033 §3 so the matrix stops promising it. Owner: test (#18) + operations (pipeline surface).

- [ ] **Browser-E2E lane fails on missing generated protobuf code — no pipeline step ever runs `buf generate`** (surfaced 2026-08-14 at Gate 2 attempt 1 of devloop `2026-08-14-gc-meeting-refusal-causes`; **pre-existing pipeline defect, NOT introduced by that diff** — recorded here rather than as a finding against it). Layer 7's Rust env-tests passed (`STATUS=OK REASON=env-tests-passed`); the browser suite failed with `Cannot find module '/work/packages/sdk-core/src/proto/dark_tower/signaling/v1/signaling_pb.js'` → `No tests found` → `STATUS=FAIL REASON=browser-e2e-failed`. **Root cause, verified**: `packages/sdk-core/src/proto/**/*_pb.ts` is gitignored (`.gitignore:13`) generated output, produced by `buf generate` per `proto/buf.gen.yaml:6` (`out: ../packages/sdk-core/src/proto`) via the Nx `proto-gen:codegen` target. `grep -rn "buf generate" scripts/ .github/` returns **nothing** — no pipeline step generates it; `scripts/lang/proto/compile.sh:22` runs `buf build`, which only validates. And `scripts/layer7.sh`'s Phase-2 browser invocation runs the suite as `pnpm --filter @darktower/web-app test:e2e` (symbolic ref — the exact line shifts as layer7.sh evolves), which selects the package and runs its script directly, **bypassing the Nx task graph** — so the `web-app → proto-gen:codegen` dependency never fires. This is exactly **F5 in `docs/runbooks/client-dev-local.md` ("Vite cannot resolve `signaling_pb.js`")** reproduced inside the validation pipeline: same idiom, same bypass, same missing artifact, in the lane whose job is catching what the other layers cannot. **Why it fired (as of 2026-08-14)**: the browser lane was diff-triggered and `crates/gc-service/` was in `__BROWSER_E2E_TRIGGER_PATHS`, so it triggered; Layer 1's proto stage was skip-if-untouched and that diff touched no `proto/`, so codegen never ran. Any diff that triggered the browser lane without touching `proto/` hit this — a diff that *did* touch `proto/` masked it. That masking is why it survived this long. **UPDATE 2026-08-20 (validation-run-all devloop) — the premises above changed and the defect is now MORE exposed, not fixed.** The browser lane is no longer diff-triggered (`__browser_e2e_triggered`/`__BROWSER_E2E_TRIGGER_PATHS` retired — it runs on EVERY Layer-7), and Layer 1's proto stage is no longer skip-if-untouched (it always-runs). But neither change generates the `_pb.ts` codegen — `lang/proto/compile.sh` still runs `buf build` (validate-only), and no step runs `buf generate`. So this now fires on **every** run-story task's Layer 7 (since Layer 7 + the browser suite both run every task) whenever the checkout lacks the gitignored generated proto — strictly wider exposure than the diff-triggered world. @operations flagged this increased exposure at the validation-run-all planning gate; the fix (a Phase-1g precondition check and/or Nx-aware invocation) is unchanged and still deferred to this entry's owner. This is the single biggest operational reason to prioritize it now. **The status was wrong, not just the outcome**: it reported the *implementer* lane (`FAIL`) for a missing build artifact, which is an *operator*-lane precondition. Under the skill's Layer 7 policy that consumes an implementer attempt, so an unlucky devloop could exhaust both attempts on an environment fault and escalate against a clean diff — **R-4's defect** (an environment failure recorded as an implementer failure), inside the story whose whole purpose is making those lanes trustworthy. **Fix**: `layer7.sh`'s Phase-1g precondition lane already emits `PRECONDITION_FAILURE` for `dev-certs-missing` and `playwright-browser-missing` (symbolic refs — those checks now run unconditionally since the browser lane always-runs); a third check for the generated proto artifact belongs there — cheaper and more honest than making the invocation Nx-aware. Better still is both: the precondition check makes the failure *legible*, while switching the invocation to `pnpm nx run` makes it *not happen* (Nx caches codegen, so it is ~free when the proto is unchanged — see F5's "Fix"). Whichever lands, it needs a hermetic case in `scripts/layer7.test.sh`, per that file's existing convention. **Deferred from the surfacing devloop deliberately**: `scripts/layer7.sh` is not in that diff, the fix has genuine design ambiguity (precondition-check vs Nx-aware invocation vs both) and needs its own test cycle, and changing how Gate 2 executes from inside a Gate-2-blocked devloop is not a safe trade. **Note for whoever picks this up**: the Lead cleared the precondition for that run by executing `pnpm nx run proto-gen:codegen` manually. That unblocked the run; it did **not** fix the defect, and the next devloop that triggers the browser lane in a fresh container fails identically. Owner: operations (`scripts/layer7.sh`), coordinating with test (Layer 7 lane policy) and client (`proto-gen` target wiring).

- [ ] **Layer 7 reports environment faults on the implementer lane — generalized from two distinct instances in one devloop** (surfaced 2026-08-14/15, devloop `2026-08-14-gc-meeting-refusal-causes`). The bullet above is instance 1: a **missing build artifact** (`buf generate` never runs) reported as `STATUS=FAIL REASON=browser-e2e-failed`. Instance 2 is **accumulated cluster state**: on the frozen-tree re-validation the browser suite failed 4/4 with `MeetingForbiddenError: Organization meeting limit exceeded` — R-7 cap exhaustion on a shared `devtest` org (`max_concurrent_meetings DEFAULT 10`, `migrations/20250118000001_initial_schema.sql:10`), the same suite having passed 8/0 an hour earlier on the same cluster, with no production code marking meetings ended. Same misreport: `FAIL`, implementer lane. **The generalizable defect is that `layer7.sh` cannot distinguish "the environment cannot support this test" from "the code failed this test."** The two instances need different remedies — instance 1 a precondition check for the generated artifact, instance 2 owned by task #3 (R-7 per-run org provisioning) — but both need the *same* lane fix, which is why they are recorded together: fixing only the artifact case leaves the misattribution intact. **Positive discriminator available in both cases, and cheap**: on the 2026-08-15 run the Rust env-tests **passed while successfully creating meetings** (`crates/env-tests/tests/23_meeting_creation.rs`) in the same execution where the browser suite hit the cap. A create path that works for one suite and refuses another is org-state-specific by construction, not a code regression — a check `layer7.sh` could make itself rather than leaving a human to reason it out post hoc. **Note the dependency inversion this creates**: instance 2's fix (task #3) is gated behind the commit of task #2, so the story cannot resolve it without first proceeding past a red it predicted. That is a structural argument for the lane fix, not just an inconvenience — an operator-lane classification would have let this run be re-run or waived without an adjudication. Owner: operations.

## Guard Timeout vs Violation — a gate that can invent its own red

- [x] **CLOSED 2026-08-21 (fast-fail-guards devloop).** Reclassified guard timeout/kill (exit 124/137) from `STATUS=FAIL` to `STATUS=PRECONDITION_FAILURE` (operator lane, exit 2) at the `scripts/guards/run-guards.sh` (`classify_guard_exit`) emission site — REASON tokens (`guard-timeout-<name>`/`-kill-<name>`) byte-identical. Layer 3 now aggregates to `PRECONDITION_FAILURE`→exit 2 (the per-guard STATUS line is collected by `tee_collect_statuses`); `run-story.sh` routes gate rc≥2 to the operator lane automatically (no runner change). Interactive Gate-2 attempt-budget de-scoped in `SKILL.md` (a `PRECONDITION_FAILURE` from ANY layer no longer burns an attempt) — WITH a reproduce-on-retry discriminator so a diff-caused timeout still routes to the implementer. `run-guards.sh` gained a violation/timeout counter split + `guard-violations`/`MIXED_LANE` traces (standalone exit mirrors the ladder). Runbook §3/§6.3/§8, ADR-0034 §9, ADR-0033 §4 updated. See `docs/devloop-outputs/2026-08-21-fast-fail-guards/main.md`. Original entry retained: **A guard timeout is reported in the same shape as a guard violation, so machine load is indistinguishable from a code defect** (surfaced 2026-08-14 at Gate 2 attempt 2 of devloop `2026-08-14-gc-meeting-refusal-causes`; raised jointly by @operations and @implementer, with @team-lead recording that they created the contention that produced it). The pipeline was launched into the middle of a concurrent `cargo` workload; Layer 3 — 13s on a quiet machine — took **713s** and returned `STATUS=FAIL REASON=guard-timeout-validate-kustomize`. L1 went 10s→975s and L4 186s→954s in the same run. **Nothing was wrong with the code, and it consumed a real Gate 2 attempt on a clean diff.** **The defect is the REASON taxonomy, not the timeout**: `guard-timeout-*` and a genuine violation both surface as `STATUS=FAIL`, so a reader — human or the story runner's classifier — cannot distinguish "this guard found a problem in your diff" from "this guard never finished measuring." **Prior art for the fix is one layer up and already accepted**: `layer7.sh` draws exactly this line with `PRECONDITION_FAILURE` (operator, exit 2) vs `FAIL` (implementer, exit 1), documented in `docs/runbooks/devloop-validation.md` §3's STATUS ladder with `scripts/lang/_common.sh:__status_rank` as the rank SSoT (this entry originally mis-pointed at `scripts/guards/common.sh:__status_rank`, which has no STATUS handling — corrected 2026-08-21). A timeout is an environment fact about the machine, not a fact about the diff, so it belongs on the operator lane by the same argument R-4 makes for exit 2. **Distinct from the sibling entry above**, and worth keeping distinct: the Layer-7 codegen defect is a control that reports *about the code without observing it*; this one is a control that **fabricates a verdict out of machine load** — a gate that can invent its own red rather than one that misses a real one. A false red trains reviewers to re-run rather than read, which is how a real red gets re-run away. **Scope note**: raising the timeout is not the fix, and neither is removing it — an unbounded guard hangs the pipeline. The fix is that the *classification* be honest, so an operator-lane timeout can be re-run without spending an implementer attempt, and so an under-contention run is legible as such rather than as a diff defect. Owner: operations (`scripts/lang/_common.sh` STATUS taxonomy, `docs/runbooks/devloop-validation.md` §3 ladder), coordinating with test (the story runner's failure classifier consumes these REASONs). Not work for the surfacing diff — it changes how every layer reports.

## Dependency Vulnerabilities (pnpm audit)

- [x] **`pnpm audit` HIGH/CRITICAL advisories block Layer 6 branch-wide — 11 advisories, 6 packages** (surfaced 2026-07-29 at task #20 Gate 2; **RESOLVED 2026-08-08 via devloop-outputs/2026-08-08-clear-ts-audit-advisories, task #66**). All 11 advisories in this set are cleared: the `brace-expansion`/`fast-uri`/`postcss` families by the `pnpm.overrides` entries now in root `package.json`, and `@vitest/browser`/`axios`/`js-yaml` by the intervening dependency-graph bumps. A single new high advisory then surfaced on the forced weekly Layer-6 run — **GHSA-2v37-7h3g-55p8 (CVE-2026-67213), `nanoid` <3.3.17, infinite-loop DoS in `customAlphabet`/`customRandom` when `size=0`**, transitive via `. > eslint-plugin-svelte > postcss > nanoid` (dev-lint tooling only; both SDK packages `private:true`, prod graph clean). Fixed with a **real transitive override, not a suppression**: `"nanoid": ">=3.3.17 <4"` added to root `package.json` `pnpm.overrides` (bounded to the 3.x line — an unbounded `>=3.3.17` resolves to the ESM-only `nanoid@6`, which breaks the CJS toolchain; caught by @test at Gate 1). Resolves tree-wide to `nanoid@3.3.18`, single version. `audit-suppressions.toml` and `.pnpm-audit-ignore.json` untouched. Verified: `DEVLOOP_AUDIT_FORCE_RUN=1 scripts/lang/ts/audit.sh` → `STATUS=OK REASON=pnpm-audit-passed` (no `SUPPRESSED=` line); `pnpm lint` (nx lint + svelte-check) and `pnpm test:unit` green. This is also the canonical home for the resolution — the §Supply Chain "Layer 6 is currently FAILING" pointer (2026-07-30) is resolved by the same change. Historical detail from the original triage retained below for the audit trail.

  Suggested spin-out slug (historical) `2026-07-30-dep-bump-vitest-brace-expansion`. Original entry follows.

- [ ] ~~**`pnpm audit` HIGH/CRITICAL advisories block Layer 6 branch-wide — 11 advisories, 6 packages** (surfaced 2026-07-29 at task #20 Gate 2).~~ **Act soon** — a live CVSS 9.4; whether the ADR-0033 §12 MTTR tripwire has formally fired is **source-dependent and unresolved**, see below.

  **Pre-existing, NOT introduced by task #20**: that devloop's diff is eight docs/scripts files and touches no `package.json` and not `pnpm-lock.yaml`, so `pnpm audit` returns an identical result with or without it. `cargo audit` passes; only the pnpm half is red. Any devloop running `scripts/layer-all.sh` on this branch sees the same failure until this lands.

  **Owner**: client (owns the `packages/*` pins) + infrastructure co-sign (lockfile + pipeline). Security owns the policy call and re-verifies the two prod-graph commands at that devloop's Gate 2. Task #20 (operations) recorded this entry only; remediation is not operations-owned.

  **Suppression posture — scoped per advisory, not blanket.** `audit-suppressions.toml` is untouched by task #20 and nothing has been added under §Suppressed Advisories.
  - **GHSA-3jxr-9vmj-r5cp and GHSA-p63j-vcc4-9vmv: NOT suppressed, and must not be.** Both have in-range, semver-compatible fixes (verified per-parent below), so neither can make the "no in-range fix exists" claim that earns a slot in the manifest.
  - **GHSA-mh99-v99m-4gvg: NOT suppressed either — it decomposes by path, and only two of three are blocked.** Its patched version is `>=5.0.8`, and whether that is in range depends entirely on which parent you are under (@security, 2026-07-30):

    | Path | Parent declares | `5.0.8` in range? | Posture |
    |---|---|---|---|
    | `5.0.6` ← `minimatch@10.2.3` (`nx`, `typescript-eslint`, `api-extractor`) | `^5.0.2` | **YES** | **Fix now** — semver-compatible, no policy question |
    | `1.1.15` ← `minimatch@3.1.5` (`eslint`) | `^1.1.7` | no | blocked in-range |
    | `2.1.1` ← `minimatch@9.0.9` (`vite-plugin-dts`) | `^2.0.2` | no | blocked in-range |

    **Do not write "GHSA-mh99 has no in-range fix"** — flat, that is false. It is fixable today on the widest consumer set. Suppression is reachable only for the `1.x`/`2.x` paths, only after step 3 below has been attempted and failed, and only **path-scoped** — never ID-wide (see the ID-scoping hazard below).

  ### MTTR tripwire (ADR-0033 §12) — determination is SOURCE-DEPENDENT, not established

  §12 measures from advisory **publication**, not discovery — but **GitHub and OSV disagree on both publication dates by roughly three weeks, in the same direction**, and the two sources give opposite answers:

  | Advisory | GitHub published | OSV published | Age on GitHub | Age on OSV |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | GHSA-p63j-vcc4-9vmv (`@vitest/browser`, CVSS 9.4) | 2026-07-08 | 2026-07-21 | 22 days — **breached** | 9 days — **not breached** |
  | GHSA-3jxr-9vmj-r5cp (`brace-expansion`, CVSS 7.7) | 2026-06-29 | 2026-07-20 | 31 days — **breached** | 10 days — **not breached** |

  (Ages as at 2026-07-30.) **On GHSA dates both breach the 14-day threshold; on OSV dates neither does.** This entry deliberately does **not** resolve it by picking a source — @security explicitly declined to rule OSV canonical, and selecting whichever database is in front of you and asserting it is the exact move that produced several corrections during task #20.

  So: "breached", "next-up not schedulable", and "§12's revisit clause has fired" are **true on one source and false on the other**, and are recorded here as unresolved pending a decision on which governs.

  > **The underlying gap, which is the durable finding: ADR-0033 §12 says MTTR is measured from "advisory publication date" and never names a source of record.** That ambiguity is harmless while sources agree and decisive when they differ by three weeks — here it is precisely the difference between a fired tripwire and a comfortable margin. Security owns audit policy under §11 and is raising the ADR amendment separately, alongside the ID-scoping hazard below.

  **Independent of which source governs**: the arithmetic still favours acting soon on a CVSS 9.4. But *"act soon because it is serious"* and *"the tripwire has fired"* are different claims, and only the first is established.

  ### Installed versions

  - `@vitest/browser` **4.1.8** — vulnerable (`>=4.0.0 <4.1.10`), target **4.1.10**. Four *exact* pins: `web-app` and `sdk-svelte` (`@vitest/browser` + `@vitest/browser-playwright`), and `sdk-core` via `@vitest/coverage-v8`.
  - `brace-expansion` — **three** versions installed, and **all three are vulnerable**:

    | Installed | Affected range (GHSA-3jxr) | Patched | Parent declares | Path |
    |---|---|---|---|---|
    | `1.1.15` | `<1.1.16` | `1.1.16` | `minimatch@3.1.5` → `^1.1.7` | `eslint@9.39.4` / `@eslint/config-array` |
    | `2.1.1` | `>=2.0.0 <2.1.2` | `2.1.2` | `minimatch@9.0.9` → `^2.0.2` | `@vue/language-core@2.2.0` ← `vite-plugin-dts@4.5.4` ← devDeps of `sdk-core`, `sdk-svelte`, `test-utils` |
    | `5.0.6` | `>=3.0.0 <5.0.7` | `5.0.7` | `minimatch@10.2.3` → `^5.0.2` | `@microsoft/api-extractor@7.58.7` ← `vite-plugin-dts@4.5.4`; also `@typescript-eslint/typescript-estree@8.62.0`; also `nx@20.3.0` |

  > **Do not record `5.0.6` as non-vulnerable.** An early triage pass classified it that way and it is wrong — GHSA-3jxr-9vmj-r5cp's third range `>=3.0.0 <5.0.7` contains it. Independently confirmed against the upstream advisory by @team-lead and @security, and against our own `pnpm audit` output. The `5.x` path also reaches a **different consumer set** (`api-extractor`, `typescript-estree`, `nx`) than the `1.x`/`2.x` paths.

  All three GHSA-3jxr overrides are **semver-compatible** — each patched version satisfies its parent's declared range (verified per-parent in the table above), so none is a major-version injection.

  ### ⚠ The obvious three-override fix does NOT clear Layer 6

  A **second** `brace-expansion` advisory is in play that the first triage pass missed:

  | Advisory | Vulnerable | Patched | Hits |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | GHSA-3jxr-9vmj-r5cp | `<1.1.16`; `>=2.0.0 <2.1.2`; `>=3.0.0 <5.0.7` | `1.1.16` / `2.1.2` / `5.0.7` respectively | all three installed versions |
  | **GHSA-mh99-v99m-4gvg** | **`<=5.0.7`** (flat, spans all majors) | **`>=5.0.8`** | **all three installed versions** |

  Overrides of `1.1.16` / `2.1.2` / `5.0.7` clear GHSA-3jxr on all three paths but leave **GHSA-mh99 red on the `1.x` and `2.x` paths**, whose parents cannot admit `>=5.0.8` in range. The `5.x` path *can* (`^5.0.2`), so a single `5.0.8` override there closes **both** advisories on the widest consumer set at once.

  ### Remediation order (@security ruling, 2026-07-30 — supersedes the earlier "three options")

  1. **Fix the `5.x` path now** — override to `5.0.8`. Satisfies `^5.0.2`, and clears GHSA-mh99 *and* GHSA-3jxr there simultaneously (`>=5.0.7` ⊆ `>=5.0.8`). No policy question.
  2. **Fix the `1.x`/`2.x` paths for GHSA-3jxr** with the semver-compatible `1.1.16` / `2.1.2`.
  3. **Then evaluate parent bumps** for the two remaining GHSA-mh99 paths — `vite-plugin-dts` (and through it `@vue/language-core`) and `eslint`/`@eslint/config-array` — to releases whose `minimatch` admits a `5.x` `brace-expansion`. The `2.x` path is the more tractable: `vite-plugin-dts@4.5.4` is ours to bump directly. The `1.x` path runs through eslint's own pin and may genuinely be stuck.
  4. **Suppression only if step 3 fails, and only path-scoped.** The manifest's admission criterion is "no in-range fix exists," which must be *established* per path by attempting the parent bump — not inferred from override arithmetic.

  > **ID-scoping hazard, if it ever reaches step 4.** GHSA-mh99's range is `<=5.0.7` — flat, spanning all majors. An ID-scoped suppression justified by an exposure analysis of the `1.x`/`2.x` paths would **silently cover the `5.x` path too**, so if a future change ever dropped the `5.x` override the advisory would go quiet under a justification that never examined it. The verify-command-of-record for such an entry must therefore assert the `5.x` path is **fixed**, not merely that the prod graph is empty:
  >
  > ```
  > pnpm why -r brace-expansion            # the 5.x line MUST show >=5.0.8; 1.x/2.x are the suppressed scope
  > pnpm why -r --prod brace-expansion     # must print nothing
  > pnpm why -r --prod @bufbuild/protobuf  # CONTROL
  > ```

  This is also the same "do we use `pnpm overrides` for remediation" convention question already open in the pre-existing `nx@20.3.0 → minimatch@9.0.3` entry (§Inter-Service Protocol Inconsistency, 2026-05-06); **resolve the two together** — same packages, same fork.

  ### The other seven advisories

  All transitive, all HIGH, none mentioned in the original triage. They must be cleared too or Layer 6 stays red:

  | Package | GHSA | Vulnerable | Patched | Path |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | `axios` | GHSA-gcfj-64vw-6mp9 | `>=1.15.2 <1.18.0` | `>=1.18.0` | `nx > axios` |
  | `fast-uri` | GHSA-4c8g-83qw-93j6 | `>=3.0.0 <3.1.3` | `>=3.1.3` | `sdk-core > vite-plugin-dts > @microsoft/api-extractor > @microsoft/tsdoc-config > ajv > fast-uri` |
  | `fast-uri` | GHSA-v2hh-gcrm-f6hx | `>=3.0.0 <=3.1.3` | `>=3.1.4` | same as above |
  | `js-yaml` | GHSA-52cp-r559-cp3m | `>=3.0.0 <3.15.0` | `>=3.15.0` | `nx > @yarnpkg/parsers > js-yaml` |
  | `js-yaml` | GHSA-52cp-r559-cp3m | `>=4.0.0 <4.3.0` | `>=4.3.0` | `eslint > @eslint/eslintrc > js-yaml` |
  | `postcss` | GHSA-r28c-9q8g-f849 | `<=8.5.17` | `>=8.5.18` | `eslint-plugin-svelte > postcss` |

  ### Verify commands of record

  ```
  pnpm why -r --prod @vitest/browser     # must print nothing
  pnpm why -r --prod brace-expansion     # must print nothing
  pnpm why -r --prod @bufbuild/protobuf  # CONTROL: must print sdk-core, proving
                                         # empty above means ABSENT, not silently failed
  ```

  The control line is load-bearing. An empty result nobody falsifies is a verification claim that has quietly become decorative. Verified 2026-07-30: both prod-graph checks empty; the control printed `@bufbuild/protobuf@2.12.0 └── @darktower/sdk-core@0.0.0 (dependencies)`.

  **Fail-closed clause**: if either prod-graph command ever prints a consumer, the dev-only exposure argument below is void immediately, with no grace period.

  ### Exposure

  **Production exposure nil** — none of the six packages is in the prod graph or a shipped bundle; they are test runner, linter, build plugins and the Nx CLI. Both SDK packages are `private: true`, so there is no downstream publish/supply-chain surface either. Residual risk is **developer-workstation and CI-runner only**.

  `@vitest/browser`'s CRITICAL is a Browser-Mode file-access permission-gate bypass — a test-runner sandbox escape, not a product vulnerability, and only while browser-mode tests run. **The 9.4 vector is AV:N and needs network access to the Browser Mode API.** Neither `packages/sdk-svelte/vitest.config.ts` nor `packages/web-app/vitest.config.ts` sets `test.api` / `api.host`, and nothing in the repo passes `--host` or `0.0.0.0`, so Browser Mode binds its loopback default. Write this down as **mitigated by configuration, not eliminated**: it is an *inherited* default with no guard behind it, and a loopback-bound API with no authorization is still reachable by any same-host process. **Spin-out scope therefore includes** setting `api: { host: '127.0.0.1' }` explicitly in both browser-mode configs, so the property is asserted rather than inherited.

  `brace-expansion` is nil for a **second, independent** reason worth stating separately because it survives even if the packaging argument changes: the DoS input is a glob **pattern**, and every pattern on these paths originates from our own eslint config, `vite-plugin-dts`/`api-extractor` globbing our own source, or `nx`/`typescript-estree` walking repo-controlled config. Verified across all three consumer sets, including the `5.x` path's (`api-extractor`, `typescript-estree`, `nx`) — **analysed, not inherited by analogy from the `1.x`/`2.x` paths.** And killing the vector someone will inevitably ask about: `brace-expansion` expands the *pattern*, not the candidate, so a hostile filename in the workspace is never expanded as a glob.

  ### Verification must not rely on `pnpm audit` going green

  Assert **installed versions directly** rather than treating a clean audit as proof:

  ```
  pnpm why -r brace-expansion   # expect only >=1.1.16 / >=2.1.2 / >=5.0.7 (and >=5.0.8 if GHSA-mh99 is in scope)
  pnpm why -r @vitest/browser   # expect >=4.1.10
  ```

  A green audit proves what the advisory feed knew at that moment, not what is installed. **Advisory ranges can be widened after publication**, so a package that audited clean last week can be in range today with no dependency change at all. Version assertions do not have that property.

  > **Tombstone — do not re-add the worked example.** Earlier drafts of this entry asserted that GHSA-3jxr's `>=3.0.0` range specifically *"was added on 2026-07-20"*. That is **unverifiable, not merely unverified**: GitHub records only a `last updated` timestamp (which says *something* changed, not what), OSV records `published 2026-07-20 / modified 2026-07-21` with **all three ranges already present** and **no per-range history at all**, and neither database stores when an individual range entered an advisory. So the metadata that would substantiate it does not exist, and OSV showing all three ranges at its publication timestamp is mild evidence *against* it.
  >
  > The claim survived two retractions because each time it was re-derived as *support* for a conclusion everyone already agreed with. An unfalsified inference does not sit still — it gets cited, and each citation makes it look better-sourced than it is. The general property above needs no instance and the argument loses nothing without it.

  ### On the "our feed lags upstream" claim — not reproducible, do not propagate

  Two separate triage passes concluded that `pnpm audit` failed to flag `5.0.6` and that our advisory feed is behind upstream. **Re-derived twice from `pnpm audit --audit-level high --json` on 2026-07-30, that is not what our tooling does.** It reports all three GHSA-3jxr ranges, including `>=3.0.0 <5.0.7` against installed `5.0.6`, and it additionally reports GHSA-mh99-v99m-4gvg, which neither manual pass had at all:

  ```
  GHSA-3jxr-9vmj-r5cp | range=>=3.0.0 <5.0.7 | patched=>=5.0.7 | installed=5.0.6
  GHSA-mh99-v99m-4gvg | range=<=5.0.7        | patched=>=5.0.8 | installed=5.0.6
  ```

  So the feed was **ahead** of the human summaries, not behind. The conclusion "`5.0.6` is vulnerable" was right; the stated reason was not. **Do not write that Layer 6 under-reports** — it would tell every future reader that a green audit is untrustworthy for a reason that is not true, and the genuinely good reason to distrust a green audit is the range-widening one above. If a run ever does reproduce the under-reporting, record the command and its output here, because this one does not.

## Guard Self-Test Cleanup

- [x] **Remove `--self-test` mode + fixtures from existing guards**: Three guards have `--self-test` modes and fixture directories under `scripts/guards/simple/fixtures/{guard-name}/`: `validate-alert-rules.sh`, `validate-dashboard-panels.sh`, `validate-metric-labels.sh`. These fixtures are NOT invoked by `run-guards.sh` at Gate 2 or by any CI job — they run only when a developer manually passes `--self-test`. They provide initial-development correctness value but then drift as guard rules evolve, creating a false sense of test coverage. Remove the `--self-test` arg handling, `self_test()` functions, and fixture directories. Preserve any illustrative examples as top-of-file guard comments if still useful. Estimated: 50-80 LOC deletion per guard + fixture directory removal. **Policy going forward**: new guards don't add fixtures — implementer proves correctness with ad-hoc scripts during the guard-authoring devloop, discarded before commit. (resolved 2026-05-08 via devloop-outputs/2026-05-08-guard-self-test-cleanup)

## Roster Display Names (devloop 2026-08-06-roster-display-names)

- [x] **MC-consumption spin-out — meeting-controller (Domain-judgment, owner-implements per ADR-0024 §6.3)** (resolved 2026-08-06 via devloop-outputs/2026-08-06-mc-display-name-join-plumbing, task #65): MC now reads `claims.display_name` and threads it through `join_connection` → `ControllerMessage::JoinConnection` → `MeetingMessage::ConnectionJoin` → `handle_join` onto `Participant.display_name` (→ `ParticipantInfo.display_name` → proto `Participant.name`). The name is length-bounded at the connection trust boundary (`truncate_utf8(&claims.display_name, MAX_PARTICIPANT_NAME_LEN)`), and the generic `Participant N` label is kept ONLY as an empty-claim fallback in `handle_join` (backward-compat with `#[serde(default)]` empty names). The MINOR-003 stopgap at `meeting.rs` is removed. Original spin-out description follows:
- [ ] **MC-consumption spin-out — meeting-controller (Domain-judgment, owner-implements per ADR-0024 §6.3)**: The AC/common half now ships the registered display name IN the meeting token (`common::jwt::MeetingTokenClaims.display_name`, populated from `users.display_name` at issuance in `crates/ac-service/src/handlers/internal_tokens.rs`). MC does NOT yet consume it: `crates/mc-service/src/actors/meeting.rs:604` still sets `let display_name = format!("Participant {}", self.participants.len() + 1);` (the security-motivated MINOR-003 placeholder). To finish the feature, MC must read `claims.display_name` from the validated meeting token and thread it onto `ParticipantInfo.display_name` → proto `Participant.name`, which means adding a name parameter to `join_connection` (`crates/mc-service/src/actors/controller.rs:127`) and `ControllerMessage::JoinConnection`. This reverses MINOR-003, so it is a deliberate meeting-controller decision, not a mechanical edit. Until then, two users still see "Participant N" — end-to-end acceptance of the roster feature is gated on this spin-out. Backward-compat: `display_name` is `#[serde(default)]`, so a token minted with an empty name (or an old token without the field) must still be handled gracefully by MC's fallback.

- [ ] **Revisit meeting-token display-name FAIL-CLOSED when cross-cluster federation is wired**: `resolve_meeting_display_name` in `crates/ac-service/src/handlers/internal_tokens.rs` returns `AcError::NotFound` (404, counter `ac_meeting_token_display_name_total{outcome="user_not_found"}`) when there is no `users` row for the subject. This is correct ONLY under the current single-cluster assumption — every meeting-token subject (Member and External alike) is an AC-authenticated user found by `user_id` in this AC's shared `users` table. Once true cross-CLUSTER federation exists, an `External` subject's identity row will live in its home cluster and a local miss becomes legitimate; fail-closed would then wrongly block valid federated joins. At that point this branch must resolve the name federally (e.g., via the home cluster / a federated identity claim) instead of failing — NOT by silently stamping an empty string. Guarded by the `outcome="user_not_found"` metric so the transition is observable.

- [ ] **Metric-emission assertion for `ac_meeting_token_display_name_total{outcome="lookup_error"}` (observability, accepted deferral, devloop 2026-08-06-roster-display-names)**: The metrics integration suite (`crates/ac-service/tests/internal_token_metrics_integration.rs`) asserts the `resolved` and `user_not_found` label cells at the metric level, locking the emission mechanism and failure-label spelling. The `lookup_error` cell (emitted in the `Err(e)` arm of `users::get_by_id` in `crates/ac-service/src/handlers/internal_tokens.rs`) has NO metric-level assertion because inducing a real DB error in the `sqlx::test` harness needs a broken/closed pool (fragile, disproportionate). Accepted by observability reviewer: a `lookup_error` label typo still surfaces as a token-issuance error via the existing `record_error`/issuance-error SLO path (operator not blinded), and the emission mechanism is already locked by the sibling `user_not_found` assertion. NOTE: a unit test calling `record_meeting_display_name_outcome("lookup_error")` directly is NOT an acceptable substitute — it would pass the literal through the wrapper without locking the handler call-site spelling (false coverage). If picked up, cover it by driving the real DB-error path (e.g. a fault-injecting pool) so the call-site literal is exercised.

## Guard Precision — no-secrets-in-logs false-positive on `jwt` vocabulary

- [ ] **`no-secrets-in-logs` false-positives on the `jwt` Category-A token inside `target:`/module-path literals and message strings (owner: semantic-guard/security)**: `crates/dt-guard/src/rust_log_secrets.rs` matches `PII_TOKENS_CATEGORY_A` (incl. `jwt`) against raw line text, so it fires on non-secret log lines merely because `jwt` appears in `target: "common.jwt..."` or in a message like `"Failed to decode JWT header"`. This forced **15 interim per-line `// guard:ignore(...)` annotations** in `crates/common/src/jwt.rs` (a GSA) — every one logs only `error=%e`, `kid`, `kty`, `alg`, or `url` (all non-secret), surfaced only because the roster-display-name change pulled the whole file into the changed-file scan. Residual masking risk (code-reviewer, devloop 2026-08-06-roster-display-names): a `guard:ignore` suppresses the ENTIRE line's evaluation permanently, so if one of those exact lines is later edited to log a real secret (e.g. `kid = %kid` → `token = %raw`) the guard stays silent. Fix: make the guard precise — do NOT match Category-A vocabulary tokens that occur inside `target:`/module-path label positions or literal message strings (only in value/field positions). Once the guard no longer false-positives on `jwt` in `target:`, REMOVE the 15 interim ignores in jwt.rs. Cross-cutting dt-guard change, out of scope for the auth-controller carrier devloop.

## Guard Precision — validate-subdomain-regex-sync scans gitignored build artifacts

- [x] **`validate-subdomain-regex-sync` counts occurrences in gitignored generated artifacts, producing a false-positive drift failure (owner: operations + test; found 2026-08-21 at Gate 2 of `docs/devloop-outputs/2026-08-21-gc-invalid-request-rejection-reason/`)**: **RESOLVED 2026-09-01** (`2026-09-01-subdomain-guard-tracked-only`): the sweep now uses `git grep` (tracked files only), so gitignored `coverage/**`/`.nx/cache/**` copies of `limits.ts`/`SUBDOMAIN_REGEX` are never scanned — reproduced under `/usr/bin/grep` (the guard's real interpreter; the interactive-shell `grep` is a ugrep `.gitignore`-respecting shim that masked it) at 12 with artifacts present, now a stable 10 tracked-only regardless of build state. Same fix (single `git grep` path + `git init`/`git add -A` synthetic fixture) as the standalone entry in §"From ADR-0024 §6 Amendment" neighbourhood above; see that entry for the full mechanism. The interim `git clean -fdX` workaround is no longer needed. Original entry follows. — the guard pins the org-subdomain regex to exactly 10 tracked SSoT sites, but its file scan is a filesystem walk that does NOT respect `.gitignore`. After a `pnpm/nx test --coverage` run generates `packages/sdk-core/coverage/**/limits.ts.html` and `.nx/cache/**/limits.ts.html` (both gitignored, both containing `SUBDOMAIN_REGEX` copied from the real `limits.ts` SSoT site), a subsequent run of the guard counts **13** and fails `STATUS=FAIL REASON=subdomain-regex-drift`. Reproduced deterministically by running `scripts/layer-all.sh` twice: run 1's Layer 4 (`nx test`) generates the coverage/cache, then run 2's Layer 3 scans them. It also fires for any developer who runs `nx test` before the guard. This is NOT real SSoT drift — all 10 tracked sites are correct; the 3 extras are transient reflections of an already-counted source. **Fix:** scope the guard's scan to git-tracked files (`git ls-files` / `git grep`) or explicitly exclude `.nx/`, `**/coverage/`, and other gitignored build output. Same class as the "control scans the wrong target set" family already tracked under §"From ADR-0024 §6 Amendment" (guards checking artifacts that don't correspond to reviewed source). Interim workaround at Gate 2 was `git clean -fdX packages/sdk-core/coverage .nx/cache` before re-running — a workaround, not the fix. Small dedicated devloop (guard + its self-test `scripts/guards/validate-subdomain-regex-sync.test.sh`), shared gate tooling → not folded into an unrelated feature devloop.

## Guard Precision — `validate-cross-boundary-scope` reds on the headless escalation contract file

- [x] **Writing `.devloop-escalation.json` — which the devloop skill's Headless Mode rule 3 REQUIRES — trips `validate-cross-boundary-scope` with `scope_drift_inbound` (owner: operations, validation-pipeline guards; surfaced 2026-09-01 at the resumed Gate 2 of `docs/devloop-outputs/2026-09-01-internal-contract-reshape/`)**: **RESOLVED 2026-09-01** (commit `cddc576`): `crates/dt-guard/src/cross_boundary_scope.rs::is_symmetric_exclusion` now exempts the repo-root path `.devloop-escalation.json` by name, with a self-test asserting the root path is excluded AND that `docs/.devloop-escalation.json` still reds — the named-not-broad shape this entry asked for. The row-23 workaround below is therefore no longer required of future headless escalations. Original entry follows. — the Layer-A scope-drift guard flags any file present in the diff but absent from the devloop's `main.md` classification table. `.devloop-escalation.json` is written at the repo root by every headless devloop that escalates, so **obeying the skill reds a guard**, and the red then lands inside the record of the escalation it is reporting — noise on precisely the artifact a human is about to read. It is not a source change in any sense the guard cares about: it is untracked, transient, consumed by `scripts/workflow/run-story.sh`, and deleted by that script's session-limit-lane `git clean -fdq`.

  **Worked around, not fixed, in the surfacing devloop** by adding a truthful classification row for it (row 23). That is correct locally but wrong as a pattern — every future headless escalation would have to add the same row, and a reader who sees it without the accompanying note would conclude the devloop planned to escalate from the start.

  **Fix:** exclude `.devloop-escalation.json` from the guard's inbound diff set by name, alongside whatever other runner-contract artifacts live at the repo root. Prefer an explicit named exclusion with a comment pointing at the skill's Headless Mode rule 3 over a broad untracked-file exclusion — the guard's whole value is that it notices unplanned files, and untracked-and-unplanned is the case it most needs to keep catching. Add a self-test case asserting the file is ignored while a sibling untracked file at the same path depth still reds.

  **Defer trigger / why it is small but not zero:** it needs a self-test case to avoid becoming the "excluded so broadly it stopped catching things" failure the sibling `validate-subdomain-regex-sync` entry above already documents. Any devloop touching `validate-cross-boundary-scope`, or the next headless escalation that has to repeat the row-23 workaround, is the moment.

## Guard Precision — `todo-tracking` counts ordered lists and thematic breaks as debt bodies

- [ ] **`dt-guard todo-tracking`'s bullet predicate recognizes only `-`, `*` and `+`, so `1.` ordered lists and `---` thematic breaks are counted as inlined debt-body lines — one defect producing BOTH a false positive and a false negative (owner: operations for the finding, dt-guard tooling for the fix; raised by @operations, entry written by @dry-reviewer at Gate 3 of `docs/devloop-outputs/2026-08-31-signaling-media-contract-reshape/` so the numbers are measured rather than asserted)**: the rule is that a `docs/devloop-outputs/*/main.md` §Accepted Deferrals (or §Tech Debt Pointers) section is pointer-only, and **two consecutive non-bullet body lines** trip it. The root cause is `crates/dt-guard/src/todo_tracking.rs:92-101`, which admits a line as a bullet only when its first character is `-`, `*` or `+` followed by whitespace. Markdown's ordered-list form is not in that set, and neither is a thematic break.

  **Direction (i) — false positive, live today.** Two *adjacent* numbered pointers (`1. …` / `2. …`) fire the guard on a section that is doing exactly what the convention asks. Nothing in the convention says a pointer list must be unordered. The corpus escapes this only by accident of formatting: the two files that use numbered pointers inside a deferrals section — `docs/devloop-outputs/2026-05-13-base-ref-unification-task42/main.md:458,460,462,464` and `docs/devloop-outputs/2026-05-14-devloop-validation-runbook-task39/main.md:275,277,279,281,283` — blank-separate every item, and a blank line resets the run counter (`todo_tracking.rs:88-91`). All 9 ordered-list lines in the corpus sit in those 2 files. Change either file to tight list spacing and a correct section starts failing. That is luck, not enforcement.

  **Direction (ii) — false negative, sized.** The blank-line reset also means the guard is trivially satisfiable while its stated intent is violated: **34 of the 68** `main.md` files that have a deferrals section carry genuine prose body lines in it — **57 lines in total** — and every one of them passes clean today, because no two of those lines happen to be adjacent. Under a naive stricter predicate (any single body line trips, dropping the two-consecutive requirement but keeping the current bullet definition), **61 of the 68** files trip: **27 are false positives** — files whose only "body" lines are `---` thematic breaks or `1.` ordered-list items that the predicate misreads — and **34 are real**. That 27/34 split is the reason this cannot be fixed by tightening the run-length rule alone: fixing the bullet predicate is a prerequisite for any stricter body rule, or 27 correct files start failing.

  **Measurement, so it is reproducible rather than taken on trust.** Corpus = `docs/devloop-outputs/*/main.md`; section = the guard's own scan (start at `## Accepted Deferrals` or `## Tech Debt Pointers`, stop at the next `## `, skip fenced blocks and the `Example`/`Examples`/`or` template prose, apply the `todo_tracking.rs:92-101` bullet test); a line is "genuine prose" if it is a body line under that scan and is not `^\d+[.)]\s` and not `^(-{3,}|\*{3,}|_{3,})$`. Numbers measured 2026-09-01 against the tree at Gate 3 of the reshape devloop (which contributes its own `main.md` to the 68). **Note the correction**: the reshape devloop's plan (§P5) records this as **59** genuine body lines; the measured figure is **57**, and no variant of the definition that also yields the 34/68 file count reproduces 59. Use 57. The file counts (68, 34, 61, 27, 34) all reproduce exactly.

  **Fix**: extend the bullet predicate at `todo_tracking.rs:92-101` to recognize ordered-list markers (`^\d+[.)]\s`) and thematic breaks (`^(-{3,}|\*{3,}|_{3,})$`) as non-body, and extend `crates/dt-guard/tests/` with a case for each direction — a tight numbered pointer list that must PASS, and a two-line prose body separated by a blank line that must FAIL once the run rule is tightened. Then decide the body rule separately, with the 34-file corpus sweep that a stricter predicate implies. **Why not fixed in the reshape devloop**: a predicate change plus a 34-file sweep plus its own test cycle, in shared gate tooling, inside a proto reshape — and the decisive argument was contamination, since a guard-tooling change landing in the commit that devloop's `## Expected Layer-State` is diffed against would make that diff unreadable. Shared gate tooling → small dedicated devloop, not folded into a feature devloop. **Constraint while this is open**: `## Accepted Deferrals` sections must stay single-line `-` bullets and must not be renumbered into `1.` form, or they trip direction (i).

## Guard Precision — `validate-cross-boundary-classification` validates only the visible rows of a truncated table

- [ ] **A malformed Cross-Boundary Classification table (a Note cell containing a literal newline) truncated the table and hid six rows, while the Layer-B classification-sanity guard still reported `STATUS=OK` because it validated only the rows it could parse (owner: `operations` / validation-pipeline guards for the fix; test finding, found 2026-09-02 at Gate 2 of `docs/devloop-outputs/2026-09-02-frame-v2-cross-language-vectors/`)**: `scripts/guards/simple/validate-cross-boundary-classification.sh` enforces the GSA/ownership rules on the rows it reads, but it has no well-formedness check on its input — a raw newline inside a Markdown table cell ends the table early, and the guard validates the surviving prefix and passes. The hidden rows were caught only because a *second* guard (the orphaned-paths / scope-drift check) flagged the classification-table's own paths as absent from it. Defense-in-depth held this time, but the classification guard silently validating a subset of its input is the exact silent-partial-validation family this devloop hit repeatedly (see its §Lessons Learned). The Gate-1 review protocol treats the classification table as authoritative, so a validator that can be defeated by truncation is a real gap in a security/ownership-relevant gate. **Fix:** make the guard assert the table is well-formed before validating it — the table parses to a complete grid, no data-row cell contains an embedded newline, and (if cheap) the parsed row count is internally consistent — and fail on a malformed/truncated table rather than validating only visible rows. Add a self-test fixture with a newline-truncated table that must RED. Pre-existing gate tooling → small dedicated devloop, not folded into a feature devloop.

## JoinResponse sender_id — contract resolved, MC allocator outstanding (devloop 2026-08-31-signaling-media-contract-reshape)

- [ ] **MC does not yet allocate a `sender_id`; `build_join_response` sends `None` (owner: meeting-controller, story task 10)**: the contract half of the old `JoinResponse.user_id == 0` entry is **resolved** — resolution (a) was taken. `user_id` (a `uint64` "8-byte user ID for media frames" that MC had no numeric source for) is deleted and its tag reserved; `JoinResponse.sender_id` is a distinct per-meeting numeric identity MC mints, deliberately on a **fresh tag** rather than repurposing tag 2, because `uint64`→`uint32` are both varint and an in-place repurpose is the one change an old peer would decode successfully with the wrong semantics. It is `optional uint32` with **valid range 1..=65535 and zero never valid**: proto3 `uint32` has no presence, so a documented "0 = unassigned" sentinel would be indistinguishable from *MC forgot*, and a shared zero is not a recycled id but N concurrently-live colliding ones — which, since the SFrame key id encodes (sender, stream, generation) and the AEAD wrap nonce derives from the key id, means two senders wrapping distinct transmit keys under one KEK at one nonce (ADR-0036 §4: AES-GCM authentication-key recovery, not merely a confidentiality loss). **Remaining work**: `crates/mc-service/src/webtransport/connection.rs::build_join_response` sends `sender_id: None` and MC has no allocator. Task 10 must mint per-meeting ids, enforce the bound with **`NonZeroU16`** — *not* a bare `u16::try_from`, which accepts the reserved-invalid zero — and fail closed rather than truncating (an out-of-range value truncates into the key id's 16-bit sender field and surfaces as a signature failure against the *wrong* participant's key). The same task publishes the roster's `sender_id` and `identity_public_key`, both currently unpopulated. Contract, range and receiver-side rules: `proto/dark_tower/signaling/v1/signaling.proto::JoinResponse.sender_id`. **[RESOLVED 2026-09-01 — see end of this paragraph] task 10's manifest prompt in `docs/user-stories/2026-08-27-hear-yourself-through-handler.md` had gone stale and contradicted the shipped contract in two places** (found by @operations at Gate 3 of `2026-08-31-signaling-media-contract-reshape`): it said the join response returns the joiner's `sender_id` by "repurposing the existing `user_id` field, which MC must stop writing as a hardcoded zero", and that "MC range-checks it to 65535". The shipped proto does the opposite on purpose — `user_id` is deleted with tag 2 reserved and `sender_id` takes fresh tag 8, precisely because `uint64`→`uint32` are both varint and a repurposed tag 2 is the one change in the reshape an old peer decodes *successfully* with the wrong semantics; and a bare range-check to 65535 accepts 0, the single value the contract reserves as invalid. The manifest prompt is what the story runner feeds the task-10 implementer, so the stale text is load-bearing, not commentary: followed literally it reintroduces the exact hazard this reshape's `JoinResponse` and `StreamAssignment` comments exist to prevent. RESOLVED 2026-09-01: the task-10 prompt was corrected in place (prose only) under @team-lead Gate-3 authority — it now says `sender_id` returns on fresh tag 8 with tag 2 reserved (not repurposed), and MC enforces the bound with `NonZeroU16` (not a bare range-check that accepts 0). `dt-story validate` passes. The proto comment and this entry remain the authority for the range/receiver rules.

## Credential-leak guard + TS KEK sink (devloop 2026-08-31-signaling-media-contract-reshape, Gate-1 condition G1-5)

- [ ] **Extend the credential-leak semantic-guard vocabulary AND add the TypeScript-side KEK sink control (owner: story task 10)** (Gate-1 condition G1-5 of the reshape devloop, which committed to a durable `docs/TODO.md` entry rather than a main.md note precisely because main.md is not re-read at task 10; flagged again independently by @security and @semantic-guard at Gate 3). Both land at task 10, where the meeting KEK first becomes non-empty. (a) `scripts/guards/semantic/checks.md` §Credential Leak has no `\bkek\b` / `meeting_kek` vocabulary — `_` is a word character, so `\bkek\b` cannot match `meeting_kek` — so the guard reports clean while a KEK could ship in a log; add the vocabulary. (b) The Rust redacting `Debug` control (`crates/proto-gen/build.rs` `skip_debug` + `crates/proto-gen/src/lib.rs`) is Rust-only; protobuf-es has no `skip_debug` equivalent, so `JSON.stringify(serverMessage)` on a decoded `ServerMessage`/`MeetingKekUpdate` prints the KEK. Add a TS-side sink control (a guard or a redacting projection) before task 10 populates the KEK. The whitelist projections (`JoinedEvent`, `e2eBus.ts`) keep it non-live today, and the Gate-3 TS test now enforces `JoinedEvent` stays KEK-free — but neither covers a raw `ServerMessage` stringify.

## Restore buf breaking enforcement after ADR-0036 story 1

- [ ] **`proto/buf.yaml` carries `breaking.ignore: [dark_tower/signaling/v1/signaling.proto]` — remove it once the ADR-0036 story-1 reshape is in main (owner: protocol)**: added 2026-08-31 at the user's direction to pass the ADR-0036 intentional wire break (54 FILE findings, task-3 escalation of `docs/user-stories/2026-08-27-hear-yourself-through-handler.md`) through Layer 6 and CI, because `buf breaking` diffs against merge-base(origin/main) and every category (FILE/WIRE_JSON/WIRE) fires on the reshape, and no per-finding acceptance mechanism exists (ADR-0033 §13 Wave 3, trigger now met with two case studies). **Scope**: buf's `breaking.ignore` takes paths, not package names, so the carve-out names the one file that breaks. All 54 findings are in `signaling.proto`; `dark_tower/internal/v1/internal.proto` stays under full FILE enforcement, which matters because story tasks 4 and 8-10 reshape that MC↔MH contract while the carve-out is live. **Do not widen it to `dark_tower`** — a blanket entry silences internal.proto too (measured: an injected `RegisterResponse.media_handler_url` string→int32 change fires under the narrow entry and is swallowed under the blanket one). **Cost**: inside `signaling.proto` no break of any kind is caught while this is present, including unintended ones — read that file's diff by hand. **Loudness**: `scripts/lang/proto/breaking.sh` prints `SUPPRESSED=<paths>` on every Layer-6 run while the key exists, so this entry is not the only reminder. That line is *not* emitted by `.github/workflows/ci-client.yml`, which hand-rolls its own `buf breaking` step — see the separate ci-client entry below. **Restore**: delete the `ignore:` key as soon as the story PR merges; the first post-merge merge-base is post-reshape and FILE enforcement goes green on its own. Rebase any in-flight proto-touching branch onto post-merge main first, or its merge-base is still pre-reshape and it re-fires all 54. Deleting the key also removes the `SUPPRESSED=` line — that disappearance is the completion signal. Do not close the story's PR review without this entry visible.
- [ ] **`.github/workflows/ci-client.yml` hand-rolls `buf breaking` and so misses the `SUPPRESSED=` warning (owner: infrastructure)**: the `buf breaking (against PR base)` step runs `pnpm exec buf breaking proto --against …` with its own `git merge-base`, self-described in-file as an INTERIM PATCH pending "run CI in the devloop image". The `proto/buf.yaml` carve-out *does* apply there (buf reads the module config regardless of caller — verified on the CI-pinned buf 1.72.0), but the `scripts/lang/proto/breaking.sh` `SUPPRESSED=` warning does not, so a GitHub PR check shows a bare green while enforcement is off for `signaling.proto`. Fix is to route that step through the wrapper (`export PATH="$PWD/node_modules/.bin:$PATH"; bash scripts/lang/proto/breaking.sh`), which also removes the duplicated base-ref logic. **Not a drop-in**: ci-client.yml also runs on `push: [main, develop]`, where the hand-roll bases on `origin/main` but `_get_base_ref.sh` bases on `HEAD~1` — that semantic change needs infrastructure's call, which is why it is not folded into the reshape devloop.

- [ ] **No dated hard-fail expiry on the `proto/buf.yaml` `breaking.ignore` carve-out (owner: infrastructure + security)**: surfaced by @security at the delta review of `docs/devloop-outputs/2026-08-31-signaling-media-contract-reshape/` (S3). The restore path above is a prose entry plus the `SUPPRESSED=` warning `scripts/lang/proto/breaking.sh` prints on every Layer-6 run. The warning is loud and its *disappearance* is the completion signal, but a warning cannot force a restore — an indefinitely-lived carve-out remains possible. The repo's own precedent for a time-boxed suppression is `audit-suppressions.toml`, whose entries carry an `expires` date that the always-run `scripts/guards/simple/audit-suppressions.sh` hard-fails past ("Policy ownership: security"). **Do not reuse that manifest**: its schema is `ecosystem = rust|js` and entries are code-generated into `.cargo/audit.toml` / `.pnpm-audit-ignore.json`, so a proto entry breaks the sync-check. The shape is a sibling guard — `scripts/guards/run-guards.sh` auto-discovers `simple/**/*.sh` — that hard-fails when `proto/buf.yaml` still carries `breaking.ignore` past a stated date, plus the `.test.sh` companion the directory convention expects. Deliberately not built inside the reshape devloop: `scripts/guards/**` is dt-guard-tooling/infrastructure-owned and an always-run gate that can red the pipeline needs its owner. **Trigger**: build it if the carve-out outlives the ADR-0036 story-1 PR; it becomes moot the moment the `ignore:` key is deleted.

## Story-runner prompt collision: tasks 4, 5 and 6 each claim the same two MC test files (run-story 2026-08-27-hear-yourself-through-handler)

- [ ] **Three task prompts claim `crates/mc-service/tests/register_meeting_integration.rs` and `otel_grpc_outbound_integration.rs` (owner: story-runner / @team-lead)**: task 4's prompt says the MC-side consuming updates "are the media-handler and meeting-controller tasks sequenced immediately behind this one"; task 5's prompt says those same mocks are updated "in this same task"; task 6's prompt says the same two files "will not compile once the RPC is removed, so update those mocks and drop the imports **in this same task**". Compilation decided it: `scripts/lang/rust/lint.sh:7` runs `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` at Layer 5, so removing three RPCs from the `MediaHandlerService` trait makes every mock `impl` carrying them a hard error **at the proto commit**. Both files were therefore updated in **task 4** (`docs/devloop-outputs/2026-09-01-internal-contract-reshape/`, classified Minor-judgment cross-boundary with @meeting-controller's Gate-1 hunk-ACK). Tasks 5 and 6 will find the work already done. Recorded because the downstream prompts are the story runner's artifact, outside a devloop's edit scope, and a task-6 implementer following the prompt literally would look for compile errors that no longer exist. Also note the naming: tasks 5 and 6 say "generation"; the landed field is `policy_generation` on the request and `applied_generation` on the response.

## Guard Coverage Gaps — path roots and globs that resolve to nothing (@security S16/S17, devloop 2026-09-01-internal-contract-reshape)

**Predicate covering both entries below**: *a guard's path roots and path globs are
inputs and must be asserted present; "path absent" is a hard error, never an empty
result set.* **The assertion is the load-bearing deliverable; the path corrections
are what falls out of running it once.** A corrective rewrite alone leaves the next
drift undetected and reads as *more* trustworthy for having been recently audited.

**Diagnostic for finding further instances is consumer polarity, not usage counting.**
Being *exercised* does not protect a path root. Where some consumer reads an empty
result as a failure, a broken root fails loudly and self-corrects; where every
consumer reads empty as "nothing to check", there is no corrective. An audit asking
"is this root used?" clears every instance below.

### S16 — ADR-0024 §6.4 GSA canon: 7 of 14 enumerable globs match nothing

Measured with the guard's own matcher (`crates/dt-guard/src/common/manifest_match.rs::path_matches_glob`;
trailing `/**` is a prefix match).

**Class A — the protected concern exists at a different path. Live gap: edits draw no owner today.**

| Dead glob | Where the concern actually lives |
|---|---|
| `crates/ac-service/src/token/**` | `services/token_service.rs`, `handlers/internal_tokens.rs` |
| `db/migrations/**` | `migrations/` — 8 live SQL files. `CLAUDE.md:22` has contradicted the canon all along |
| `crates/common/src/webtransport/**` | `crates/mc-service/src/webtransport/`, `crates/mh-service/src/webtransport/` |
| `proto-gen/**` | `packages/proto-gen/`, `crates/proto-gen/` |
| `build.rs` | `crates/proto-gen/build.rs` — holds the `skip_debug` credential-redaction list |

**Class B — genuinely aspirational, no such code exists yet. Benign; do NOT rewrite uniformly with Class A.**
`crates/ac-service/src/jwks/**` (JWKS serving is `handlers/jwks_handler.rs`);
`crates/ac-service/src/audit/**` (no `audit` directory exists anywhere).

**Alive and correct**: `proto/**`, `crates/media-protocol/**`, the four
`crates/common/src/*.rs` entries, `crates/ac-service/src/crypto/**`. **`proto/**`
matches `proto/dark_tower/internal/v1/internal.proto`, so the 2026-09-01
internal-contract-reshape devloop's own three-owner intersection determination rests
on a live entry.**

**Live instance inside that devloop's own diff**: BOTH its GSA rows —
`packages/proto-gen/scripts/verify-codegen.sh` and `crates/proto-gen/tests/internal_roundtrip.rs` —
are classified GSA:YES under `proto-gen/**`, a glob matching nothing. They are inside a
Guarded Shared Area *only* because the implementer applied the §6.4 criterion by hand;
the Layer B guard would have read ordinary paths with no owner requirement.

**Why nothing caught it.** `gsa_sync.rs` has exactly three rules — `canon_missing`,
`count_mismatch`, `stray_yaml_key` — all comparing the five mirrors against *each
other*, with no filesystem access. Five synchronised copies of a dead glob is a clean
run. Sharper: the module **fails closed correctly on its own inputs** (`:221`, `:234`
push a hit when a mirror file is missing) roughly 200 lines below the `CANON` array it
never resolves. It distinguishes its *inputs* from the *content it validates* and
applies the polarity rule only to the first. And `CANON_MISSING_RULE_ID` is
**overloaded** — `:127`/`:142`/`:191` mean "a string absent from a mirror document",
`:223`/`:236` mean "a filesystem path that does not exist" — so a reader who has seen
this guard fire `canon_missing` on an absent file has *observed* the id covering
missing paths and will generalise. The module's own emitted behaviour teaches the
wrong inference.

**Additional structural gap** (verified, @implementer): `owner_not_in_manifest`
requires exact single-specialist equality, so a multi-owner Owner cell is rejected —
including for `proto/dark_tower/internal/v1/internal.proto`, the one path whose
manifest entry exists to name three owners. The guard therefore *structurally cannot*
evidence an intersection-rule co-sign.

**Fix — both halves, assertion first**: add a per-entry "matches ≥1 extant path, else
fail" assertion beside `validate-gsa-sync.sh`, then correct the drifted Class A paths.
Note the CANON array is itself one of the five mirrors, so a corrective rewrite touches
five files and the sync guard would pass on a typo.

**Method and its blind spot**: found by running each canon glob through the guard's own
matcher. That finds globs matching *nothing*; it would **not** find a glob resolving to
the *wrong live path*, which is the more dangerous variant since it reads as coverage
with a real file behind it. Enumeration is therefore partial.

**Owners**: security + auth-controller (Class A includes token issuance and the
credential-redaction list) + the guard-family owner for the assertion. **To be
scheduled — no slug.**

### S16b — `dt-guard` path roots unasserted (latent; @observability)

`application_metrics.rs:176` (`ALERTS_SUBDIR`) and `kustomize.rs:145` are
**feedback-free**: every consumer reads an empty set as success. `kustomize.rs:145` is
the sharper one — `if !dashboards.is_dir() || !kustomization.is_file() { return Ok(hits) }`
is OR-gated on two inputs so losing either silences it, one input is a single *file*,
it silences a *bidirectional* check, and the paths are inline literals with no constant
to grep. Polarity-safe ride-alongs that should get the assertion anyway:
`application_metrics.rs:66` (catalog) and `:116` (dashboards), whose checks 5/6 read
empty as "every metric undocumented/undashboarded" and scream.

**Latent, not live**: all 12 hardcoded path roots across 10 guard modules resolve today.
Distinct in urgency from S16.

**In-tree precedent for the correct pattern**: `metric_coverage.rs:66-67` documents why
its empty case is safe. The right answer has been three modules away the whole time.

**Method and its blind spot**: found by grepping `if !x.is_dir() { return }`, so
`exists()`, `try_exists()`, `match` on a read result, and `let Ok(..) else` are
invisible to it. Unassessed root-path guards: `knowledge_index.rs:203`,
`cite_extract.rs:368`/`:461`, `todo_tracking.rs:157`/`:165`, `histogram_buckets.rs:86`,
`kustomize.rs:88`/`:249`, `ts_dev_trust.rs:44`, `grafana_datasources.rs:290`/`:295`.
`cross_boundary_classification.rs:233` is explicitly **unassessed, not counted** — it
iterates diff paths where a missing path is plausibly a deletion.

**Owner**: observability (own row) + guard-family owner. **To be scheduled — no slug.**

### S17 — `rust_log_secrets` accepts a bare `guard:ignore` with no justification

`crates/dt-guard/src/rust_log_secrets.rs:135-140`:

```rust
fn is_allowed_line(line: &str) -> bool {
    line.contains("REDACTED") || line.contains("[REDACTED]")
        || line.contains("skip_all") || line.contains("// guard:ignore")
}
```

Bare substring. No reason extraction, no `is_lazy_reason()`, no `MIN_REASON_LEN`. So
`tracing::info!("token={}", token); // guard:ignore` fully suppresses the
secrets-in-logs guard. The module does not import `crate::ignore` at all, and its
header documents its six checks and a DRY vocabulary commitment while saying nothing
about the hatch — an unreviewed inconsistency, not a considered exception.

**Singleton, not an example.** Five modules consume `is_lazy_reason` from the canonical
`ignore.rs` — `ts_metric_naming`, `metric_labels`, `cite_extract`, `dashboard_panels`,
`alert_rules`. A crate-wide grep for bare-substring hatch forms returns exactly one hit.
The fix is therefore "import what this crate already imports five times", not "audit the
crate's hatches".

**Ladder inversion is the headline**: no hatch at all in `ts_retained_credentials.rs:43`
by deliberate design; enforced ≥10-char justification in dashboards and alert rules;
**bare marker in the guard covering passwords, tokens and keys in logs.**

**Cause is a missed migration, not a priorities gap** (@observability, verified):
`metric_labels.rs:4` is a "Verbatim port per @observability commitment #13" whose `:13`
enumerates the hatch semantics explicitly. The compliant modules inherited the
discipline from a port that had to spell out escape-hatch semantics to port them;
`rust_log_secrets` never went through that forcing function. Writing it as a priorities
story would be wrong.

**Adjacent, same three-line fix site**: `REDACTED` and `skip_all` are also bare
substring tests, so `tracing::info!("token={}", token); // not REDACTED yet` passes.

**Owner**: security for the policy, guard-family owner for the change. **To be
scheduled — no slug.**

## Guard STATUS Attribution — `run-guards.sh` knows things its STATUS line doesn't say

- [ ] **A Layer-3 guard cannot self-declare an operator-lane precondition, and a violation's
  STATUS line names no guard.** Surfaced 2026-09-01 during Gate 1 of devloop
  `2026-09-01-release-premise-and-preflight-guards`, while establishing whether a new
  `dt-guard` subcommand could route its own "I could not find my inputs" result to the operator
  lane. It cannot, and establishing why turned up a second, adjacent gap. Both live at the same
  emission site in `scripts/guards/run-guards.sh`. **Neither is work for the surfacing diff** —
  they are behaviour changes to shared pipeline files that diff does not touch.

  **The measured mechanism** (recorded because two reviewers got it wrong in opposite directions
  before it was measured; see the §8 caveat repair in `docs/runbooks/devloop-validation.md`):
  whether a `STATUS=` line reaches Layer 3's aggregation depends on **who printed it**. Lines
  printed by `run-guards.sh` itself (`:156`, `:168`, `:253`) land on Layer 3's stdout, are
  collected by `tee_collect_statuses` (`scripts/lang/_common.sh:471-480`), and participate in
  both `aggregate_worst_status` and `worst_reason_for_status` (`_common.sh:396-407`, called at
  `:507`) — the latter taking the **first** pair matching the winning enum, which is what puts a
  cause on the stderr `LAYER=3 … REASON=` anchor. Lines printed by an individual guard
  subprocess are captured (`run-guards.sh:217`, `OUTPUT=$(…)`, the non-verbose default that
  `scripts/layer3.sh:27` uses) and re-emitted only when they match
  `VIOLATION|violation|ERROR|error|WARN`, capped at `head -5`. A guard's own `STATUS=` line
  matches none of those tokens and is silently dropped.

  **(a) Operator-lane self-declaration — a design conversation, not a patch.** A `2)` arm in
  `classify_guard_exit` echoing `STATUS=PRECONDITION_FAILURE REASON=guard-precondition-${GUARD_NAME}`
  would let any guard binary declare a precondition failure via exit 2, and it *would* work,
  because run-guards.sh's own echo sits outside the capture. **But it is a fourth hand-rolled
  `STATUS=` emission, and `run-guards.sh:285-296` records that as the standing trigger to revisit
  routing through `emit_status`** — which requires sourcing `lang/_common.sh`, rejected on coupling
  grounds per ADR-0015 §Pre-commit standalone use (run-guards.sh must stay standalone for
  pre-commit). So this is not "add an arm"; it trips a recorded architectural trigger. Size it as
  a design decision.

  **(b) Per-guard attribution for violations — a patch.** `run-guards.sh:253` emits a **fused
  constant**, `STATUS=FAIL REASON=guard-violations`, once when `FAILED_GUARDS > 0`, naming neither
  the guard nor its token, while the 124/137 arms carry `guard-timeout-${GUARD_NAME}`. **The
  asymmetry: the operator lane is attributable per-guard; the implementer lane is not.** A timeout
  tells you which guard; a violation tells you only that something violated, with the name
  surviving solely in the human `FAILED: <name>` line and the capped `head -5` re-emission.
  `FAILED_GUARD_NAMES` is already in scope at that site (printed at `:255-257`), so
  `guard-violations-${FAILED_GUARD_NAMES[0]}` for the single-guard case (fused constant retained
  as the multi-guard fallback) needs no new plumbing. **Do not flatten (a) and (b) into one size
  because they share a file and a function** — (a) is a design conversation, (b) is a patch.

  **The pin (b) needs, and the trap in specifying it.** `scripts/guards/run-guards.test.sh` is
  documented at `run-guards.sh:285-296` as the ONLY mechanical drift guard on this hand-copied exit
  ladder — and it is **blind to (b)**: `assert_status` is pure substring
  (`scripts/lang/_test_helpers.sh:45-47`), so appending `-<name>` leaves
  `STATUS=FAIL REASON=guard-violations` intact as a substring and `:135`/`:169` stay green through
  the change. **"Tighten to exact-match" is the wrong instruction** — followed literally it reds the
  multi-guard case while leaving the single-guard false-negative open. The pin is three parts:
  positive newline-anchored `$'REASON=guard-violations\n'` for the multi-guard fused form; positive
  prefix `"REASON=guard-violations-"` for the single-guard form, mirroring the existing
  truncated-needle convention at `:166`; and `assert_absent` with the **newline-anchored** needle
  for the single-guard case — a *bare* `assert_absent "REASON=guard-violations"` false-fails,
  because the suffixed form contains it as a substring. **Load-bearing caveat, stated with its
  reason so it survives the person who breaks it**: the suite captures via `out="$(cat "$RG_OUT")"`
  and command substitution strips *trailing* newlines, so newline-anchored needles work only while
  `:253` is not the last line of the capture. It isn't — `:254-258` print the header and name list
  immediately after. Reorder that block and the assertion breaks in a way that reads as a guard
  regression rather than a harness artifact.

  **Preferred fix for the pin: add the primitive, don't be clever at the call site.** The real gap
  is that `_test_helpers.sh` cannot express "this whole line equals X", which is why both the
  reviewer and the specialist proposed wrong assertions in consecutive messages while explicitly
  reasoning about substring semantics. Add `assert_status_line` / `assert_status_line_absent`
  mirroring the existing positive/negative pairing. **Purely additive — no existing call site
  changes behaviour**, which is the cheapest possible shape for a shared-SSoT change. An inline
  `grep -qx` was considered and rejected: a one-off idiom in a suite that speaks `assert_*`
  everywhere is a consistency cost paid by every future reader; the conclusion is that the idiom
  should become vocabulary, not be inlined.

  **Open scoping decision for whoever picks this up — size it deliberately.** Is the task "add the
  primitive", or "add the primitive **and** sweep the sites where the fixed-constant-vs-variable-suffix
  distinction is already load-bearing"? Only the second retires the trap rather than making it
  avoidable for future authors. Measured bounds so the choice is made from numbers: **286**
  `assert_status`/`assert_absent` invocations across **10** suites (`run-story` 138, `layer7` 56,
  `layer-all` 28, `run-guards` 16, `validate-subdomain-regex-sync` 16, `setup` 16,
  `audit-suppressions-check` 15, `_audit_gate` 1, `_changed_helpers` 0, plus the incoming
  `dev-web`); of those only **55** assert on a needle containing `STATUS=` or `REASON=` — the shape
  where the distinction can bite at all. The other 231 assert message fragments and markers where
  substring semantics is intended and whole-line matching would be actively wrong. The 55 concentrate in
  `layer7.test.sh` (**29 — over half**), then `run-guards` 9, `audit-suppressions-check` 8,
  `layer-all` 4, `validate-subdomain-regex-sync` 3, `run-story` 1, `_audit_gate` 1. Note the
  inversion: `run-story.test.sh` is 138 of the 286 assertions but only **1** of the 55, so the
  sweep's centre of mass is `layer7`, not `run-guards` — anchor the sizing there. The existing
  deliberate-prefix convention is **4 distinct needles**, all in `run-guards.test.sh` (the
  `guard-timeout-` / `guard-timeout-kill-` family). **This is a latent false-negative class, not a
  defect list: none of the 55 has been audited and none is claimed wrong.**

  **The sweep is not uniformly mechanical — positive and negative prefix assertions convert
  differently, and getting it wrong would introduce the exact defect this entry documents.** Of
  the 4 known-intentional prefix needles, **two are `assert_absent`**:
  `run-guards.test.sh:103` (`t124-no-old-fail-timeout`) and `:121` (`t137-no-old-fail-kill`) assert
  that `STATUS=FAIL REASON=guard-timeout-` / `-kill-` is **gone**. They pin ADR-0034 §9's
  2026-08-21 amendment, which moved timeout from `FAIL` to `PRECONDITION_FAILURE` leaving the
  REASON tokens byte-identical — so the enum is the *only* detectable signal of a regression, and
  these two are what detect it. Convert them mechanically and
  `assert_status_line_absent "STATUS=FAIL REASON=guard-timeout-"` asserts no line *equals* the bare
  prefix, which is trivially true forever (a real emission is always `…guard-timeout-<name>`). The
  assertion would pass permanently, stop pinning the amendment, and let a genuine regression sail
  through — a green test that has quietly stopped testing anything, i.e. the fix introducing the
  failure class it was meant to close. **Rule for the sweep: positive prefix assertions may convert
  to whole-line; negative prefix assertions may not.** The negative ones need either a prefix-aware
  absent primitive, or to stay on substring semantics deliberately with a comment saying why. Two
  of four known sites fall in the second category, so this is half the known-intentional set, not
  an edge case — and it is why the "add the primitive **and** sweep" option sits further from the
  cheap end than a raw site count implies.

  **Process note worth keeping, because it is this project's own failure mode pointed inward.**
  Six claims reached the reviewer during this Gate 1 as settled fact — a `kubectl` remedy command,
  a "live defect" framing, the `devloop-validation.md` §8 caveat, a proposed replacement for that
  caveat, a line citation, and an `assert_absent` proposal. Measuring each found **all six wrong**,
  including two of the reviewer's own, and every correction came from opening
  `scripts/lang/_common.sh`, `scripts/guards/run-guards.sh` and `scripts/lang/_test_helpers.sh`
  rather than from any agent's summary. A confidently relayed claim reads as a verified one; that
  is the same shape as a control that reads as coverage, which is what the surfacing devloop
  existed to close. The cheap countermeasure is the one that worked here: open the file before
  repeating the claim.

  **Owner**: operations. Provenance: Gate 1 of
  `docs/devloop-outputs/2026-09-01-release-premise-and-preflight-guards/main.md`, @operations +
  @observability, @team-lead ruling 2026-09-01 that all three items stay out of the surfacing diff.
  **To be scheduled — no slug.**
